# Scoutech v1 — build-ready spec

**Date:** 2026-08-12 · **Effort:** `zipline-simulator-v1` · **Map:** [map.md](./map.md) ·
**Physics contract:** [docs/simulation-theory.md](../../docs/simulation-theory.md) ·
**Tickets:** [issues/](./issues/)

This is the destination artifact of the `/wayfinder` map beside it: fifteen resolved decision
tickets assembled into one specification that can be handed to `/implement` **without further
conversation**. Everything below is decided. Nothing below is an option, a proposal or a
question. Where a plausible alternative was killed, the spec says which one and why in one line
— because an implementer who does not know what was killed re-opens it, or "fixes" a deliberate
choice. Every number is either quoted from `docs/simulation-theory.md` or **measured** on a
resolved ticket, and it is labelled as one or the other.

`docs/simulation-theory.md` is the physics contract: 861 lines consolidated from four French
reference reports and two deep-research runs, with numerical fixtures (§15) cross-validated
against MoorPy 1.3.0. This spec decides *everything else* and re-decides none of it.

### The standing rules — an implementer must not break these

1. **Never re-derive a formula and never invent a constant** (`docs/conventions/simulation.md`).
   Physics is settled in `docs/simulation-theory.md`; cite it by § and quote it. If a number is
   not in a resolved ticket or in the theory doc, it does not go in the code. Never transcribe a
   value from `docs/references/` — §14 lists which worked examples are fabricated.
2. **The binding provenance rule** (`docs/simulation-theory.md` §0.2): any coefficient tagged
   `[PAYWALLED]`, `[CONVENTION]` or `[UNVERIFIED:R1]` may appear in the UI **only as a labelled,
   configurable default carrying a warning** — never as a silent constant, never presented as
   normative. The tags travel with the values into the UI. This is a UI design constraint, not
   only an engine one.
3. **French UI copy, English identifiers.** UI strings are French with the comma as decimal
   mark, inline, no i18n framework; code identifiers and comments are English. Vocabulary is
   fixed by `docs/simulation-theory.md` §1 (physics) and `CONTEXT.md` (app layer) — use it
   exactly. **There is no "results panel"**: the **Rail** is one scrolling column with five
   sections.
4. **Gate on `bun run typecheck` and `bun run lint-type-aware`** before declaring any step done.
5. **Never edit `src/routeTree.gen.ts`** (generated by `@tanstack/router-plugin`) and **never
   hand-write `src/components/ui/**`** (`bunx shadcn@latest add <component>`, then customise).
6. **Never commit.** The agent does not make git commits in this repo.

Stack, fixed: bun · TanStack Start (SSR for the document, `ssr: false` for the app route — § 8)
· React 19 · Tailwind v4 (CSS-vars theme, no JS config) · shadcn/ui on Base UI ·
**`@tanstack/react-form` · valibot** · Vitest 4, **`node` environment only**.

> ⚠ **Four decisions here are superseded** by
> [the build backlog's amendments](../zipline-simulator-v1-build/AMENDMENTS.md), taken after this
> document was assembled: **A** Vitest is unit-only, no component tests, no jsdom · **B**
> `agent-browser` owns point-to-point testing and § 10 A12's manual re-checks · **C** the TanStack
> versions are the actual latest (still pinned), with a re-probe obligation on § 8's five measured
> behaviours · **D** `@tanstack/react-form` owns form state and **valibot replaces the hand-rolled
> `validateSearch`** — under § 8's four hard rules, unchanged. The paragraphs affected carry a pointer.
> Everything else below stands.

## § 1 — Périmètre : what v1 is, and what is ruled out

Lifted from the map's **Destination** and **Out of scope** so that nothing here needs the map
open.

### What v1 is

A **single-page, fully offline, French-language zip-line (tyrolienne) statics simulator**:
parametric inputs in a scrolling **Rail** on the left, a **Chart Block** on the right carrying a
live profile and a clearance curve, a rider moved **quasi-statically** along the line, and
**profile-CSV terrain import** (`Distance;Altitude`, the Swiss geoportal profile export) with
anchor placement on the imported profile. Spans **50 m – ~2000 m**, one span, one rider. Physics
scope is core statics plus ground clearance plus safety verdict, exactly as fixed in
`docs/simulation-theory.md` **§4–§7, §10, §11**.

Scope decisions fixed while charting the map, not up for re-litigation inside any section below:

| Decision | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Physics scope | Core statics (§4–§7) + ground clearance (§11) + safety verdict (§10) |
| Rider slider | **Position only, quasi-static.** The slider is the trolley material coordinate `s_D` (§6.2). No velocity, no time axis |
| Persistence | URL search params for shareable state + `localStorage` for the last session. The imported trace stays local |
| Families | **Both** — steel wire rope (A) and textile rope (B), gated by the §10.4 use-class rule (`PERMANENTE` ⇒ steel only, hard validation). Separate code paths |
| Terrain | **One concept, two sources**: a straight line between the two anchor bases (manual), or an imported profile. The straight line is the two-point case of the same `Terrain` value |
| Terrain import | **Profile CSV** — `Distance;Altitude` plus optional coordinates. No network calls: the app is genuinely offline. **Amended 2026-08-05, was GPX** |
| UI language | French UI, English code identifiers and comments. No i18n framework; strings inline |
| Material data | **Formula-derived with raw override.** Steel: `MBF`/`EA`/`μ` from diameter, grade and construction via the EN 12385-4 K-factors (§13.1). Textile: `EA = k·MBL` with user-supplied `MBL` and `k` (§13.2). **Zero shipped product presets** — every product row in the references is compromised |

### Out of scope

Ruled beyond this destination. These never graduate; each would need the destination redrawn as
a fresh effort. **All eleven rulings, with the reason that makes each one stick:**

| # | Ruled out | Why it stays out |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **GPX import** | v1 imports profile CSV instead ([ticket 06](./issues/06-csv-profile-import-and-anchor-placement.md)). The two formats differ **in kind**, not in syntax: a GPX track is *recorded* (noisy, irregularly spaced, curved, elevation optional per point) where a profile CSV is *drawn and DEM-sampled* (straight, monotonic, altitude always present) — so a GPX path would **resurrect** the resampling, smoothing and chord-projection questions the CSV pivot deleted. **Ticket 01 (GPX parsing in the browser) is superseded**: nothing in it is inherited, and it is the reference to start from only if GPX ever returns as a fresh effort |
| 2 | **Intermediate supports / multi-span lines** | v1 is one span, anchor to anchor |
| 3 | **Multiple simultaneous riders** | §6.1's solver handles `n` point loads and the type keeps `loads: PointLoad[]`; the **UI** exposes exactly one rider |
| 4 | **Wind, lateral blow-out, 3-D** | already out per `docs/simulation-theory.md` §8.4 — the model stays 2-D in the vertical plane |
| 5 | **Any map or plan view; a winding track projected onto a chord** | straight-line traces only. A *bent* CSV polyline is **detected and disclosed** by the straightness check (§ 4), never projected |
| 6 | **Rider dynamics, speed and braking** (§12) | follows from the position-only slider. Rules out drag, trolley friction, eddy brakes, terminal velocity |
| 7 | **Thermal change of state, creep, and §8.3's load combination** (§8, §9) | the engine parameterises on `L₀`, so these can be added later as `ΔL₀` without redesign — but not in this effort. **Read this entry with care: the single §8.2 dynamic amplification factor `DAF` *is* in scope** as a labelled `Passager` field at 1,2 `[FIELD:SSF]`, because the verdict has to judge an amplified envelope (§ 6). What stays out is §8.3's γ-factor **combination**, which is disclosed and unapplied, and §9's thermal and creep terms |
| 8 | **Product identity fields** — rope/cable désignation, model name, manufacturer | purely decorative, no consumer in the v1 physics, and every such field still costs URL surface and a first-run default. The app describes a rope by its **numbers**, not its name |
| 9 | **A print or report view, and any print stylesheet** | refused on the **verdict grammar**, not on effort (ADR-0003): a printed page is precisely what gets read as an attestation, and a printed « Indéterminé » — or a printed « Choisissez un régime » where a verdict should be — is a document that **outlives its context**. *Not* ruled out: the client-side SVG export of the chart already in the page, which needs no route and carries no verdict. v1 does not build it either |
| 10 | **A service worker, a PWA manifest, any installable packaging** | « genuinely offline » is a **data** claim — no network call for terrain or coefficients, no runtime network dependency after load — not a packaging one. Left undeclared it reads as installable-PWA, which adds a caching layer that can serve a **stale build of a safety tool** |
| 11 | **Resolving the §16 gap register** | buying paywalled standards, contacting SSF for the Vercors dataset, re-pulling product datasheets. The binding provenance rule is how v1 copes with the gaps instead |

Two further boundaries stated because they are easy to mistake for gaps: **no worked example, no
named scenario and no bundled trace ship** (the first-run scenario *is* the worked example —
§ 9), and **the prototype at `src/routes/prototype.profile-view.tsx` is not part of v1** — its
deletion is *sequenced*, not performed at assembly time (§ 10).

## § 2 — The engine contract

The engine is the whole of `src/lib/sim/`. It is the boundary between settled physics
(`docs/simulation-theory.md`) and everything the rest of this spec decides. Nothing below
re-derives a formula and nothing below introduces a constant: every equation is cited by § and
quoted from the theory doc, every number comes from a resolved ticket or from that doc.

Source: [ticket 03](./issues/03-engine-api-execution-model-and-module-layout.md) (types,
functions, failure taxonomy, module layout, execution model), amended 2026-08-12 by [ticket
11](./issues/11-validation-and-error-state-ux.md) §1/§7 (the `invalid-input` payload and
`resolveL0`'s re-report); [ticket 12](./issues/12-sweep-performance-and-worker-decision.md) (no
Web Worker, measured; the guard rail re-expressed on 260 × 60).

### The seven choices that generated this contract

An implementer who does not know what was killed re-opens it, or "fixes" a deliberate choice.
Each line below is a decision with its rejected alternative.

| Decision | Rejected alternative, and why |
| --- | --- |
| One `solveSpan`, loads as an array | Not an unloaded/loaded split: §6.1 keeps the unknowns at `(H, V₀)` and the equations at `x(L₀)=L`, `z(L₀)=h` whatever `n` is, so the unloaded case is `loads: []`. One Newton, one Jacobian assembler, one convergence path to fixture. |
| §7 modes resolve to `L₀` **above** the solver, in `resolveL0` | Not three engine entry points (`solveForTension`, `solveForSag`, …). `L₀` becomes materialised first-class state: mode switching pre-fills the length just achieved, the §15 fixture surface stays small, and §9's `ΔL₀` story composes later only because `L₀` is a value. |
| Scalars only in results; cable regenerated by `sampleCable` | Not a polyline field on any result object: given `(H, V₀)` §4.1's `x(s)`/`z(s)` are closed-form, and 240 steps × ~200 render points is ~50 k points churned per parameter change to display one. |
| §11 clearance is a **reducer over the sweep**, not a sweep field | Keeps statics terrain-free: the §15 fixtures need no terrain stub, terrain enters the engine at exactly one point, and anchor-tension / loaded-sag envelopes are likewise reductions, never stored fields. |
| Sweep **precomputed**, slider is an index lookup | Not solve-on-demand per slider position: §11's clearance minimum is global over rider positions, §10's verdict needs the worst tension over the traverse, and §6.2's warm start is inherently sequential. On demand pays a cold start per frame *and* still precomputes the traverse for the verdict — it solves the traverse twice. |
| Failure is a **value** | Not a throw: if step 137 of the sweep fails you want the other 259 and you want to know where the failing band is. Throwing means `try/catch` in the hot loop and discards partial results. |
| Terrain crosses as **data** (`Terrain` union), never a closure | A closure cannot be structured-cloned; data also beats a megamorphic callback in the clearance inner loop. Chosen to keep the worker door open, and it keeps that property for free now that the door is shut. |

Two framings that this contract depends on and that read like opinions until they are stated:

- **Most of §5.4 is internal remedies, not failures.** Damping an oscillating residual (§5.1),
  the straight-bar fallback at `w·L₀/H < 10⁻⁴` (§4.3), the `H → 0` stiffness floor, clamping a
  negative `H` iterate — the solver does these and then *succeeds*. They are `SolveNote`s, not
  errors.
- **Exceeding `MBF` is a safety verdict, not a solve failure.** A line strung too tight solves
  perfectly and returns a huge `H`; §10 then says unsafe. Physics succeeding while safety fails
  is the correct split, and it is what keeps `MBF`, `η_t` and the regime's `k` out of the solver
  entirely. (Read with the amendment in *Failure taxonomy* below: an over-`MBF` **target** never
  reaches a solve, because it maps to a sub-floor `L₀` the pre-check refuses.)

### Units: the engine is SI, the UI is not

| Quantity | Engine unit | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `L`, `h`, `L0`, `s`, `x`, `z` | m | `s` is the **unstretched material coordinate** (§6.2's primary variable) |
| `w` | N/m | `w = μ·g`, `g = 9,81` fixed and unexposed |
| `EA`, `P`, `H`, `V`, `T`, `strength` | N | |
| `theta` | rad | signed; negative = descending |

Forces cross the engine boundary **in newtons, always**. The global force-unit selector (daN
default / kN / kgf) and the `EA`-in-MN carve-out are display concerns — « voir § 3 — le modèle
de paramètres » — and the URL serialises daN — « voir § 8 — état, URL et coquille applicative ».
The engine never sees a display unit, which is what makes ticket 04's «a display control must be
provably physics-neutral» claim cheap to keep: there is nothing to keep it in.

### Public types — `src/lib/sim/types.ts`

```ts
type PointLoad = { P: number; s: number }        // s = material coordinate, §6.2's variable

type SpanInput = { L: number; h: number; L0: number; w: number; EA: number; loads: PointLoad[] }

type SpanState = {
  H: number; V0: number; VB: number                // §4.1 unknowns + the far-end vertical
  TA: number; TB: number; Tmax: number
  L0: number; w: number; EA: number                // inputs echoed — see below
  loads: PointLoad[]                               // echoed for the same reason
  iterations: number
}

type SweepStep = {                                 // §6.2 step 3, exactly
  sD: number; xD: number; zD: number
  H: number; V0: number; TA: number; TB: number; theta: number
}

type SweepResult = {
  grid: Float64Array                               // swept s_D values; length 260 — see below
  steps: SweepStep[]
  failures: { index: number; sD: number; error: SolveError }[]
  coverage: number                                 // solved / grid.length
}

type SolveResult =
  | { ok: true; state: SpanState; notes: SolveNote[] }
  | { ok: false; error: SolveError }

type SolveError =
  | { kind: "invalid-input"; field: string; value: number
      bound: number; boundKind: "min" | "max" }    // pre-check: non-finite/non-positive
                                                   // L₀,w,EA,L, and the §5.4 chord bound
  | { kind: "no-convergence"; attempts: number; residual: number }   // after §5.2's ≤ 5 re-seeds
  | { kind: "slack-limit"; hFloored: number }                        // §4.3 H→0: the line is down

type SolveNote = "near-taut-fallback" | "damped" | "reseeded"

type Terrain =
  | { kind: "flat"; z: number }
  | { kind: "profile"; xs: Float64Array; zs: Float64Array }

type MaterialSpec =
  | { family: "steel";   d: number; R0: 1770 | 1960; construction: "fibre-core" | "iwrc"
      overrides?: { MBF?: number; EA?: number; mu?: number } }
  | { family: "textile"; ropeClass: "hmpe" | "pa-en1891a" | "polyester"
      MBL: number; mu: number; k: number }

type DerivedMaterial = { w: number; EA: number; strength: number }   // N/m, N, N
```

**`bound` and `boundKind` are an amendment, not decoration** (ticket 11 §7, ELECTIONS §0.11).
Ticket 03's original payload was `{ kind, field, value }`, and « Tension impossible »'s only
actionable copy — « longueur non tendue minimale pour ce câble : 197,40 m » — was unwritable
from it. Same shape of hole as ticket 08's missing `DAF` field: the state was named, its payload
could not say what the user needs.

**Why `SpanState` echoes its inputs.** Ticket 03: *"Inputs echoed so it is self-sufficient for
sampling; kills the 'sampled with the wrong `w`' bug class."* `loads` is echoed for exactly that
reason — `sampleCable` on a loaded state must know where the kink is, and the clearance reducer
rebuilds one `SpanState` per sweep step. See the note under `evaluateClearance`.

**`MaterialSpec` is a discriminated union on `family`** and it is the *input* spec, not the
derived numbers: steel derives from geometry, textile transcribes from a datasheet.
`DerivedMaterial` is what the solver consumes. Overrides are steel-only and per value — « voir
§ 3 — le modèle de paramètres » for the field tables, the provenance chips and the
stale-Override badge. Termination efficiency `η_t` is **not** in `MaterialSpec`: it is one
picker per end and it is consumed by the verdict only, because ticket 03 already reports `T_A`
and `T_B` separately.

**`{ kind: "flat" }` stays in the union although v1's UI never emits it.** Manual-mode ground is
the straight line between the two anchor bases, which emits the **2-point case** of `profile`
(`xs: [0, L]`, `zs: [zBaseA, zBaseB]`) — ticket 04 refined the seam without changing the type.
`flat` remains useful for fixtures. Rejected: extending `Terrain` with an `x0` offset field — it
changes a type ticket 03 fixed and puts an offset in the clearance inner loop (« voir § 4 —
terrain et import CSV »).

### Function surface

```ts
// solve.ts — §5 damped Newton, §5.2 seeds + retries, §4.3 degenerate branches
solveSpan(input: SpanInput, warmStart?: { H: number; V0: number }): SolveResult

// catenary.ts — §4.1 closed form; pure, analytic, no solve
sampleCable(state: SpanState, n: number): Float64Array          // 2·(n+1) interleaved x,z
levelSpanShortcut(...)                                          // §4.1 h=0 fast path, below

// jacobian.ts — §4.2 analytic partials (own file: §15 asserts ∂z/∂H = ∂x/∂V₀ and FD agreement)

// loads.ts — §6.1
solveAtHorizontalPosition(input: SpanInput, P: number, xTarget: number): SolveResult

// sweep.ts — §6.2 grid + march + warm start
sweepGrid(L0: number, n?: number, tailSteps?: number): Float64Array
sweep(input: SpanInput, riderP: number, n?: number): SweepResult   // input.loads must be []

// terrain.ts — the engine's only terrain seam
zGroundAt(terrain: Terrain, x: number): number                    // pure, linear interpolation

// clearance.ts — §11
evaluateClearance(
  input: SpanInput, riderP: number, steps: SweepStep[],
  terrain: Terrain, hRider: number, samplesPerStep?: number,
): {
  hMin: number; atX: number; atStepIndex: number
  contact: "none" | "rider" | "cable"
  perStep: Float64Array; perStepX: Float64Array; perStepCable: Uint8Array
}

// parameterise.ts — §7
resolveL0(spec: TightnessSpec, span: SpanGeometry): Result<number, SolveError>

// verdict.ts — §10
evaluateVerdict(v: VerdictInput): VerdictResult
```

Notes on four of them:

- **`levelSpanShortcut` is internal API with a public name.** §4.1's `V₀ = −w·L₀/2` is exact for
  `h = 0` (fixtures Z1/Z2 return it to 10 digits); it is a fast path guarded on `h === 0 &&
  loads.length === 0`, not part of `index.ts`, but a **named export from its module** because
  §15 requires tests to reach it directly. Same for `sweepGrid` and the Jacobian.
- **`solveAtHorizontalPosition` is separate, not a mode of `solveSpan`.** §6.1 case 2 is a
  different problem — extra unknown `s_P`, extra equation `x(s_P) = x_D` — resolved by outer 1-D
  bisection on the monotone `s_P`, calling `solveSpan` in its loop. It is what ticket 07's typed
  `x` uses (exact, ~40 µs) instead of snapping and rewriting the user's number, and what a typed
  `x` landing in an `Indéterminé` gap uses — « voir § 5 — visualisation et mise en page ».
- **`evaluateClearance` takes the span, not only the steps.** Ticket 03 sketched
  `evaluateClearance(steps, terrain, hRider)`; that argument list cannot rebuild the `SpanState`
  a step's cable must be sampled from (`L0`, `w`, `EA`, the rider's `P`), which is what the
  reducer does 260 times. The signature above is the sketch completed, on ticket 03's own stated
  ground ("self-sufficient for sampling"); the reducer's shape is otherwise unchanged.
- **`loads` stays an array although the UI exposes exactly one rider.** §6.1's `n`-load
  generality costs nothing; the multi-rider *UI* is out of scope.

`resolveL0`'s two collaborating types belong to the adapter above the solver:

```ts
type TightnessSpec =
  | { mode: "sag";     D_target: number }   // §7 mode 3 — flèche à vide cible
  | { mode: "tension"; H_target: number }   // §7 mode 2 — prétension cible
  | { mode: "length";  L0: number }         // §7 mode 1 — L₀ direct, no inversion to run
type SpanGeometry = { L: number; h: number; w: number; EA: number; strength: number }
```

`H` is **strictly monotone decreasing in `L₀`** (§7, `[VERIFIED-NUM]` on fixture Z6's bracket),
which is what makes mode 2 a safe bisection: lower bound `L₀ = chord`, upper bound grown by
doubling the slack until `H < H_target`. Mode 3 seeds `H ≈ wL²/(8·D_target)`, refines `H`
against the exact catenary sag, then runs mode 2. Both quoted from §7; neither is re-derived
here.

### The verdict entry point

`verdict.ts` is engine code — it holds §10's regimes, `T_allow = η_t·MBF/k` and the §10.4
use-class gate — but every number it *reports* is fixed by « voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et
provenance ». The engine-side contract is:

```ts
type VerdictInput = {
  steps: SweepStep[]; coverage: number
  Tvide: { TA: number; TB: number }        // ONE unloaded solve, not a per-step quantity
  DAF: number
  regime: RegimeId                          // absent regime never reaches here — see below
  eta: { A: number; B: number }             // per end, ticket 05 §6
  strength: number                          // MBF | MBL, in N
  usage: "PERMANENTE" | "TEMPORAIRE"        // §10.4 gate
  terminations: { A: TerminationId; B: TerminationId }
}
```

Four engine-side obligations, each of which is a place a reader assumes more than was decided:

1. **`T_DAF = T_vide + DAF·(T_chargé − T_vide)`**, per sweep step, per end — exactly §8.2's
   `pretension + DAF × suspended load`, and needing nothing from §8.3's paywalled combination.
   This is a **reporting** requirement, not an engine change: `solveSpan` already produces
   `T_vide` and the amplification is one multiply-add per step per end. `T_vide` is **one**
   unloaded solve, which is why ticket 12's measurement survived ticket 08's later DAF decision.
2. **DAF does not touch clearance.** §11's criterion is geometric; a heavier momentary load does
   not move the quasi-static sag the rider traverses at.
3. **WorkSafe QLD's suspended-load criterion is one cell, not per end**, and its numerator is
   `min(η_A, η_B)·MBF` over the denominator `DAF·(T_chargé − T_vide)`. Bare `MBF` was rejected —
   §7 credits `η_t·MBF/k` everywhere else, so bare `MBF` would be the only place in the app that
   credits an unterminated rope, an optimistic outlier in the direction that kills. `min()`
   rather than a per-end pair because a line fails at its **weakest** termination. Write the
   denominator down or the criterion is unimplementable: read as `MBF/P` it scores **77** on the
   shipped line and can never bind.
4. **`coverage < 1` suppresses the verdict** (`Indéterminé`), it does not qualify it: the steps
   that failed are the ill-conditioned ones, so a verdict on the solved fraction is biased
   optimistic *by construction*. This is why ticket 03 introduced `coverage` at all.

**An absent regime is not a verdict state.** The regime field has no default, and « régime
choisi » is struck from the Solve Gate because it never blocks a solve. With no regime the
verdict is **not attempted** — `evaluateVerdict` is not called, the banner slot is empty and the
rail shows « Résultats sans régime ». Nothing was attempted, so this is emphatically *not*
`Indéterminé`, and « — » stays reserved to `Indéterminé` alone.

### Execution model: synchronous and pure forever

The engine core is **plain sync pure functions with no knowledge that workers exist**. That is
what makes §15 a set of one-line Vitest assertions with no async and no worker harness in jsdom.

The app's only access to it is one hook:

```ts
useSweep(params) → { status: "ready" | "computing", steps, envelopes, stale }
```

**No Web Worker** — measured, not felt. Ticket 03 fixed the criterion *before* the numbers
existed: move the sweep off-thread iff p95 wall time of one full `sweep()` (240 steps plus end
tails, 1000 m span, worst-conditioned case) exceeds **8 ms**, half a 60 Hz frame.

| | 240 steps | 500 steps |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Worst measured p95 (`sweep()` + `evaluateClearance`) | **1,30 ms** | **2,30 ms** |
| Criterion | 8 ms | 8 ms |
| Margin | **≈ 6,2×** | ≈ 3,5× |

`sweep()` alone is p95 ≤ 0,6 ms in every cell; the **clearance reducer is the larger half** (p95
≤ 1,7 ms) and scales with `steps × samplesPerStep`, not with the solver. Dragging the rider is
**vsync-bound, not compute-bound** (frame p50 16,70 ms / p95 18,40 ms against a 16,7 ms vsync
interval), because the slider is an index lookup plus one `sampleCable(200)` — which settles the
worker question from a second direction: off-threading would not have made dragging smoother.
Caveat to keep attached to these numbers: one machine, one browser, one fast desktop CPU, and
Chromium clamps `performance.now()` to ~0,1 ms, which is why several cells read equal. The
*conclusion* survives a considerably slower device on a ≈6× margin; the *measurement* is not a
portable benchmark.

Three inferences the measurement does **not** license:

- **The `useSweep` seam stays.** "No worker, therefore drop the async seam" is the obvious wrong
  inference. Ticket 03 justified the seam independently: stale-while-recomputing rendering is
  needed anyway — a pretension drag must keep drawing the last good profile, not blank the chart
  — and that is the same machinery a worker would need. `status` simply never reports
  `"computing"` in v1. Retrofitting async later would touch every call site.
- **The coarse-live / fine-on-release sweep stays unbuilt**, and its hazard is why: a coarse
  sweep can miss the worst clearance point and **flash a green verdict**. That is a safety
  failure, not a fidelity one. It is the conventional answer to a slow sweep, so anyone
  reintroducing it "for smoothness" is trading a verdict's correctness for an animation.
- **Neither `steps` nor `samplesPerStep` goes up.** Vertex-only clearance evaluation is safe
  because the convexity miss is the cable's own sag across one terrain segment, **≈ 5 mm at
  1500 m** — densifying the scan buys millimetres and spends the half of the budget that
  actually scales. Also rejected, from ticket 03: adaptive refinement around the discovered
  minimum (`h_clear` is flat near its minimum, so the value is already right at 2 m granularity,
  and adaptivity makes runs non-reproducible), and a time-sliced main-thread sweep (it makes the
  sweep interruptible-and-partial, and the safety verdict cannot consume a partial envelope).

**Guard rail and tripwire.** Re-run the bench if anything raises **`260 × 60`** — the grid
length times `samplesPerStep`, *not* `240 × 60` — or adds per-step work heavier than arithmetic
on values the sweep already has. The bench to re-run is the **ported Vitest bench**, not the
prototype route: the harness and `check.ts`'s §15 fixture assertions are ported into the real
build before the prototype is deleted, or this tripwire becomes unreproducible — « voir § 10 —
ordre d'implémentation ».

### The sweep grid, and why the denominator is 260

`SWEEP_STEPS = 240` names the **uniform** step count only. `sweepGrid` appends **10 geometric
tail steps per end**, inside the outer 2 % at each end, honouring §6.2's *refine near ends*
where `θ` and `T_A` change fastest. **Endpoints are excluded** — `s_D = 0` is the rider standing
on the platform, not a state worth a slider stop.

```
 s_D = 0                                                                    s_D = L₀
   ╷                                                                            ╷
   ╷ ·· · ·  ·   ·    ·     |    ·    ·    ·    ·    ·    ·    |     ·    ·  · ··╷
   ╷ └─────────────────┘    └──────────────────────────────────┘   └────────────┘
   ╷   10 tail steps            240 uniform inner steps            10 tail steps
   ╷   in 0 … 0,02·L₀           i·L₀/241, i = 1…240               in 0,98·L₀ … L₀
   endpoints excluded                                             endpoints excluded

   grid.length === 240 + 2×10 === 260
```

| Constant | Value | Provenance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `SWEEP_STEPS` | `240` uniform | §6.2's 100–500 range; confirmed by measurement, not inherited — 240 costs the same as 100 inside Chromium's ~0,1 ms clamp while buying 2,4× the resolution, and 500 also passes the criterion, so there is no reason to spend the margin |
| `TAIL_STEPS` | `10` per end | ticket 03 |
| tail width | `0,02·L₀` per end | ticket 03 ("the outer 2 % at each end") |
| tail spacing | geometric, `0,02·L₀ · 2^−(TAIL_STEPS−i)`, `i = 1…10`, densest at the anchor | the §15-verified prototype solver (`src/routes/-prototype-07/solver.ts:235`), on which the 260 count, the first-run figures and the whole performance table were measured — reproduce it exactly or those numbers stop meaning anything |
| `SAMPLES_PER_STEP` | `60` | `evaluateClearance` default; the second factor in the `260 × 60` guard rail |

**The grid is deterministic from `(L₀, n)`.** A step array that depends on the results is one
you cannot fixture.

**`coverage = solved / grid.length`, and 260 — not 240 — is the denominator the UI prints.**
Verified: `sweepGrid(L0, 240).length === 260`. This was a live bug in two tickets' copy: ticket
08 §2 printed « Couverture 240 / 240 pas » and its §6 banner « Indéterminé — 209 des 240 pas
résolus », so a fully solved sweep would have read **260 / 240**. Correct copy is « Couverture
260 / 260 pas » and « Indéterminé — 209 des 260 pas résolus », matching the first-run record's
couverture **260/260**. The UI renders `résolus / grid.length`, never a literal.

### Failure taxonomy

Three error kinds, no more, because most of §5.4 succeeds. Ground contact is **not** an error:
it surfaces in `evaluateClearance` as `contact: "rider"` (the rider envelope is violated) or
`contact: "cable"` (the cable is in the dirt), which satisfies §4.3's *detect and report, don't
iterate into it* without the solver ever seeing terrain.

| `SolveError` | Cause | User surface (French copy in « voir § 3 — le modèle de paramètres et § 7 — validation et états d'erreur ») |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `invalid-input{field:"L0"}` | §5.4's `L₀ < chord/(1+T_break/EA)` pre-check | **« Tension impossible »** — carries the attainable bound, no Fix Action |
| `invalid-input{field:…}` | non-finite / non-positive `L₀`, `w`, `EA`, `L` | per-field, tier one |
| `no-convergence` | after §5.2's ≤ 5 re-seeds; also `resolveL0` bracket failure for any reason other than rupture | **« Calcul impossible »** / **« Cible inatteignable »** respectively |
| `slack-limit` | §4.3 `H → 0`: the line is lying down | **« Calcul impossible »** |

**Deep slack is not a failure — it converges.** Measured: `L₀ = 400 m` on a 200 m span returns
`h_min = −169,67 m` with `contact: "cable"`, coverage 1,000. A line lying down is therefore the
« Câble au sol » **verdict**, not a solver error; `slack-limit` remains a guard for the `H → 0`
singular Jacobian, unreachable in the admissible envelope.

**The §5.4 chord bound *is* the breaking load**, and that fact is the hardest refusal in the
app. Measured on the §15-verified solver (12 mm IWRC 1770: `w = 0,5465·9,81 N/m`, `EA =
6,90 MN`, `MBF = 90 740 N`): floor `L₀ = chord/(1 + MBF/EA) = 197,404 m` on a 200 m level span
yields `T_max = 100,0 % MBF`, and 987,02 m on a 1000 m span yields 101,1 %. Two consequences,
both amendments to earlier claims:

- `invalid-input{field:"L0"}` **never** means « nombre invalide ». The generic-typo copy would
  be a lie about the one physics this app exists to report.
- The bound is violable in **all three tightness modes**, not only in direct-`L₀` mode, and
  `H_target ≥ MBF` is **not** "solves perfectly, verdict says rupture": any over-`MBF` target
  maps through `resolveL0` to a sub-floor `L₀` that the same pre-check refuses, so **no
  user-facing rupture verdict is reachable that way**. Ticket 04 §7's two claims to the contrary
  are struck.

#### `resolveL0` re-emits the bound in the **driving quantity**

`solveSpan` reports `field: "L0"` with the §5.4 floor. `resolveL0` catches that and **re-emits**
`field: "D_target" | "H_target"` with the bound mapped forward through the same inversion that
failed. This is free — `resolveL0` already owns the mode↔`L₀` map — and it is what makes
« Tension impossible » and « Cible inatteignable » writable at all.

| Active mode | `field` re-emitted | `boundKind` | The copy this enables |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `length` (`L₀` direct) | `L0` | `min` | « Longueur non tendue minimale pour ce câble : 197,40 m » |
| `tension` (`H_target`) | `H_target` | `max` | « Prétension maximale atteignable : 9 074 daN » |
| `sag` (`D_target`) | `D_target` | `min` | « Flèche minimale atteignable : 0,29 m » |

The bound is quoted **in the driving field's own quantity and unit, and never twice**: tension
mode states 9 074 daN and *not* « soit L₀ ≥ 197,40 m ». A second equivalent invites the user to
type a length into a tension field — the eyeball-confusion class ticket 05 refused a `kN`
carve-out to avoid. Quoting an `L₀` bound beside a tension field is the failure this amendment
exists to prevent. (0,29 m is the measured minimum attainable flèche at the floor on a 200 m
span, 0,15 % of span; 7,21 m / 0,72 % at 1000 m.)

#### No `SolveNote` ever reaches the UI

Measured across **349 440** instrumented solves: `reseeded` fired **0** times, §4.3's near-taut
condition `w·L₀/H < 10⁻⁴` fired **0** times (minimum observed ratio **3,51e-4**, needing ≈3,5×
more tension than the §5.4 floor admits), and damping halved `α` in 2 114 iterations (0,6 %).
**3 792** sweeps returned coverage **1,000** in every case. So: no residual on screen, no
`SolveNote` surface, no dev panel. `SolveNote` exists for diagnostics and for the `[ Copier le
diagnostic ]` payload, which is plain text, English keys, full float precision, decimal point —
it is evidence, not UI copy — and which contains **no terrain**, because `solveSpan` is
terrain-free and a solve failure is fully reproducible without the 200-row trace.

**Test-plan obligation, stated here because it follows from the engine's own numbers**: all
three error kinds, all three `SolveNote`s and `Indéterminé` are **unreachable inside the
admissible envelope** and must be **injected** — a forced iteration cap or a stub — not hunted
for with pathological inputs. « voir § 11 — plan de test ».

### Solver constants, quoted from the theory doc

| Constant | Value | § |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Convergence residual | absolute anchor-position error `‖(Δx, Δz)‖` in **metres** | §5.3 |
| Tolerance | `1×10⁻⁶ m` for spans ≲ 1000 m, `1×10⁻⁶·L` scaled beyond | §5.3 |
| Damping | halve `α` until the residual norm decreases; cap step growth (`a_max = 1.2`); keep `H > 0` | §5.1 |
| Retry policy | re-seed and retry, **≤ 5 attempts** | §5.1 |
| Seeds | parabolic (`H ≈ w·L²/(8·D_est)`, `D_est = √(3·L·(L₀−chord)/8)`, `V₀ ≈ −w·L₀/2 + h·H/L`) and Peyrot–Goulois | §5.2 |
| Near-taut branch | `w·L₀/H < 10⁻⁴` ⇒ Taylor / straight elastic bar `T₀ = (d/L₀ − 1)·EA`, `d = chord` | §4.3 |
| Slack limit | floor the horizontal stiffness (`slack_kx = 0.01·|w|`) | §4.3 |
| Negative `H` iterate | clamp `H > 0` during Newton | §4.3 |

Never transcribe a value from `docs/references/` (§14): the theory doc is the only source.

### Module layout — `src/lib/sim/`

```
src/lib/sim/
  index.ts          public barrel — the only path app code imports from
  types.ts          SpanInput SpanState PointLoad SweepStep SweepResult SolveResult
                    SolveError SolveNote Terrain MaterialSpec
  catenary.ts       §4.1 x(s) z(s), level-span shortcut, sampleCable
  jacobian.ts       §4.2 analytic partials (own file: §15 asserts ∂z/∂H = ∂x/∂V₀ and FD)
  solve.ts          §5 damped Newton, §5.2 seeds + retries, §4.3 degenerate branches
  loads.ts          §6.1 multi-segment V(s), solveAtHorizontalPosition
  sweep.ts          §6.2 grid + march + warm-start
  parameterise.ts   §7 resolveL0
  terrain.ts        Terrain union + zGroundAt
  clearance.ts      §11 evaluateClearance
  verdict.ts        §10 regimes, T_allow = η_t·MBF/k, use-class gate
  materials/
    steel.ts        §13.1 K-factors, EA = E_app·C·d², mass factor
    textile.ts      §13.2 EA = k·MBL
  __fixtures__/
    theory-15.ts    F1, Z1–Z6, each with a `source` field   ← « voir § 11 — plan de test »
```

Files are named by concept, one per §-section, each carrying a header comment citing its
`simulation-theory.md` § **plus** the underlying reference report, per
`docs/conventions/simulation.md`. Colocated `*.test.ts`, one per source file.

**Where the A/B separation actually goes.** `docs/conventions/simulation.md`'s "families must
not share a code path unchecked" cannot mean two catenary solvers: the elastic catenary sees
`(w, EA)` as numbers and §15's fixtures are material-free. The separation belongs where the
physics genuinely differs.

- `materials/steel.ts` and `materials/textile.ts` share **no base module, no common helper and
  no `materials/index.ts` barrel**. Each derives `{ w, EA, strength }` independently from its
  own spec. Adding a barrel is the specific "tidying" that undoes this decision.
- `MaterialSpec` is a **discriminated union on `family`**; both specs are retained across a
  family flip (which is what makes textile→steel able to raise `invalid-input{field:"L0"}`,
  since `T_break/EA` is ≈ 0,013 steel against ≈ 0,67 PA).
- The §10.4 use-class gate (`PERMANENTE ⇒ steel only`) is **hard validation in `verdict.ts`**,
  not a branch in either material module.
- Everything downstream of derivation is legitimately shared.

**Do not lift `src/routes/-prototype-07/solver.ts` into `src/lib/sim/`.** Ticket 07 §12 laid it
out so it could not be mistaken for a head start; the engine is rebuilt TDD from the §15
fixtures — « voir § 10 — ordre d'implémentation », which also sequences the prototype's deletion
(after the bench and fixtures are ported, not now).


---

## § 3 — The parameter model — fields, units, ranges, gates

This is the input contract. Every other part of the spec reads its fields from here: the URL
schema serialises exactly these quantities, the error surfaces render exactly these checks, the
rail's input half lays out exactly these groups, and the engine receives nothing that is not
derived from them.

Source tickets: [04 — Span and rigging parameter
model](./issues/04-span-and-rigging-parameter-model.md) and [05 — Material spec
model](./issues/05-material-spec-model.md), both **as amended** 2026-08-11/12 by tickets 08, 11,
13 and 14. Where an amendment struck an original claim, the struck claim is recorded below with
the word **struck** so nobody restores it from the un-amended ticket.

Two conventions hold everywhere in this section. **French UI copy** sits in « guillemets » with
the comma as decimal mark; **English identifiers** sit in `backticks`. And the map's binding
provenance rule applies to every coefficient below: anything tagged `[PAYWALLED]`,
`[CONVENTION]` or `[UNVERIFIED:R1]` appears only as a labelled, configurable default carrying a
warning — the tags travel with the values into the UI, they are not bookkeeping for this
document.

### Two geometry framings, deliberately not one

Manual mode types **`L` and `h` directly**. Imported mode places anchors as (Station along the
Trace, Anchor Height above the profile there) and shows `L` and `h` as read-outs under **Valeurs
déduites**.

Two framings rather than one, because `solveSpan` takes exactly `(L, h)` and typing those two
numbers beats backing them out of a terrain that may not exist: forcing the imported framing
onto manual mode would make every metre of `h` come from tower height — a 200 m line dropping
30 m would need a 30 m tower at A. Numerically identical, a lie about the installation.

**Manual-mode ground** is the straight line joining the two **anchor bases**, from the two
`hauteur d'ancrage` fields. That is why garde au sol is live from first launch rather than only
after an import, and why the first parameter a user touches moves the app's central output.

```
h_clear(x) = z_cable(x) − z_sol(x) − h_rider

  A ─────────────────
  │▲       cable      ╲
  │4m       ╲──────── ╲ B
  base╲                 │▲2.5m
       ╲── sol ──────────base
```

Manual terrain emits `{ kind: "profile", xs: [0, L], zs: [zBaseA, zBaseB] }` — the two-point
case of a union the engine already has, resolved by the same linear interpolation, so no engine
change. `{ kind: "flat" }` stays in the union for fixtures and v1's UI never emits it. Anchor
heights feed **Terrain only**; they never reach `solveSpan`. The import pipeline itself is
« voir § 4 — terrain et import CSV ».

`h < 0` means B below A. The label and its helper text must not muddle that: a downhill line has
a **negative** dénivelé.

### The field table

Hard = blocks before the engine (which gate, below). Warning = non-blocking, needs no solve.
`Initial` is the shipped first-run value; the whole opening screen is « voir § 9 — premier
lancement ».

#### Usage

| Groupe | Champ (FR) | Symbole | Unité | Dur | Avertissement | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Usage | Type d'installation | — | — | grille §10.4 | — | `PERMANENTE` |
| | Régime de sécurité | — | — | requis (**Verdict Gate**) | — | *aucun* — « — choisir — » |

`PERMANENTE` is the restrictive branch, so the app never opens in the permissive state by
accident. `Régime de sécurité` has **no default** and its absence is legitimate state, not a
hole to fill — the five regimes and the verdict grammar are « voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et
provenance ». It is a rail field under Usage next to `Type d'installation`: splitting the user's
controls across two regions for a value they must actively choose was rejected.

#### Géométrie

| Groupe | Champ (FR) | Symbole | Unité | Dur | Avertissement | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Géométrie | Portée | `L` | m | `> 0`, fini | ∉ [50, 2000] | **150,0** |
| | Dénivelé | `h` | m | fini | `\|h\| > L` (> 45°) | **−18,0** |
| | Hauteur d'ancrage A | — | m | `≥ 0` | `> 30` | **12,0** |
| | Hauteur d'ancrage B | — | m | `≥ 0` | `> 30` | **12,0** |
| | Station A | — | m | dans le tracé | — | *mode importé seul* |
| | Station B | — | m | dans le tracé, `L > 0` | — | *mode importé seul* |
| | Importer un profil… | — | — | **bouton** | — | *aucun tracé* |
| | Retirer le tracé | — | — | bouton, actif si tracé chargé | — | — |
| | *identité du fichier* | — | — | lecture seule, 4 états | — | *absent* |

In manual mode `Portée` and `Dénivelé` are the editable pair and the Station rows are absent; in
imported mode the Stations are editable and `L`/`h` move to **Valeurs déduites**. Both modes
keep the two `hauteur d'ancrage` fields — that is the one framing they share.

The last three rows are an **amendment to ticket 04's table**, which listed none of them
(ELECTIONS §0.5c). « Importer un profil… » is **a button, not a drop zone**, because the chart
already carries three drag targets and a fourth would compete with them. Parse failures render
**inline beneath the control**, never in the banner slot — « voir § 7 — validation et états
d'erreur ». The file-identity line has four states: *absent* · « nom.csv » · « tracé reçu par
lien » (a link carries the payload but not the filename) · « tracé en mémoire … (non utilisé) »
with `[ Réutiliser ce tracé ]`, the state a « Réinitialiser le scénario » leaves behind.

#### Ligne

The family selector plus one Material Spec per family. **The app holds both Material Specs at
once**: the selector chooses which is active and the inactive one — overrides and terminations
included — is retained, so flipping back restores what you built. Retention is Session state,
never a URL guarantee: the URL carries the active configuration and never a retained alternate.

| Groupe | Champ (FR) | Symbole | Unité | Dur | Avertissement | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Ligne | Famille de ligne | — | — | requis, grille §10.4 | — | **Câble acier** |
| | Terminaison A | `η_A` | — | requis (**Verdict Gate**), `η` éditable | ⚠ selon terminaison | **Cosse à sertir / œil Flamand**, `η_t` 0,90 |
| | Terminaison B | `η_B` | — | requis (**Verdict Gate**), `η` éditable | ⚠ selon terminaison | **Cosse à sertir / œil Flamand**, `η_t` 0,90 |

**The governing asymmetry: steel derives from geometry, textile transcribes from a datasheet.**
§13.1 gives a closed formula chain from `d`, so the steel block has three inputs and three
derived values; §13.2 gives no `MBL(d)` and no `μ(d)` — only the stiffness convention `EA =
k·MBL` — so the textile block has four inputs and exactly one derived value. Every decision
below follows from that split, including why override is a steel-only concept.

##### Ligne › Câble acier

| Champ (FR) | Symbole | Unité | Affordance | Dur | Avertissement | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Diamètre nominal | `d` | mm | numérique | `> 0` | ∉ [6, 26] mm `[CONVENTION]` | **12,0** |
| Grade | `R₀` | N/mm² | select : 1770 / 1960 | requis | — | **1770** |
| Construction | — | — | select : 6×19 âme textile (`K = 0.330`) / 6×19 âme métallique (`K = 0.356`) | requis | — | **âme métallique** |

The derivation chain, quoted from `docs/simulation-theory.md` §13.1 and never re-derived:

```
MBF [kN] = K · d² · R₀ / 1000     K = 0.330 (fibre core) / 0.356 (steel core)  [STD-ATTESTED]
EA       = E_app · C · d²         E_app ≈ 100–130 GPa,  C = 0.384–0.449
μ        = W · d² / 100           W = 0.359–0.400                             [STD-ATTESTED]
w        = μ · g                  (g = 9.81, below)
```

| Valeur déduite | Formule | Défaut à 12 mm / 1770 / âme métallique |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MBF` | `K·d²·R₀/1000` | **9074 daN** (90,74 kN) |
| `EA` | `E_app·C·d²` | **6,90 MN** (6,897 MN) |
| `μ` | `W·d²/100` | **0,5465 kg/m** |
| `w` | `μ·g` | **0,536 daN/m** (5,361 N/m) |

**`E_app` and `C` are pinned in prose, not fields**: `E_app = 115 GPa` and `C = 0.4165`, the
midpoints of §13.1's ranges, stated in the provenance popover so the number is reproducible,
never as a magic `EA` coefficient. `W` likewise, midpoint **0.3795**. Pinned midpoints in a
popover rather than two exposed knobs, because `E_app` and `C` only ever appear as a product:
two fields means two ways to be wrong about one quantity, with no independent knowledge of
either — a user who nudges `E_app` to 130 *and* `C` to 0.449 has taken the corner of a box
rather than a value they can defend. Overriding `EA` directly is strictly more expressive.
Because the midpoints are pinned and not construction-indexed, `EA` and `μ` depend on **`d`
alone** while `MBF` depends on all three inputs — which is what the culprit table below encodes.

**Also rejected: a free `K` field, or a free-numeric grade**, for ropes outside 6×19 /
1770–1960. `K` is `[STD-ATTESTED]` for exactly those two constructions and a free `K` is the
invented constant `docs/conventions/simulation.md` forbids. The escape hatch is the
derived-value override: a 6×36 user types their datasheet's `MBF` and `μ`, which is better
grounded than any extension of the formula.

**Ranges produce point values, never bands.** `MBF` has no range at all (`K` is a discrete
pick); the whole §13.1 range problem collapses onto `EA`, which spans 5,53–8,41 MN (**×1,52**)
at 12 mm and moves `H` by ~5 % and sag by **~0,3 m** on a 200 m span at 3 % flèche — 0,3 m of
garde au sol, and it grows as the line tightens. `EA` is nonetheless **a single derived scalar**
at a documented default carrying the « non confirmé » chip, with the ranges stated in the
popover prose. One Solve, one Sweep, one Verdict; `SpanState` stays scalar and the engine
contract is untouched.

A point value rather than propagated `EA_min`/`EA_max` intervals, because an interval
re-litigates the engine contract (two Sweeps, and the whole output half becomes interval
arithmetic), `[5,53 ; 8,41] MN` is **not** a confidence interval but the union of two handbook
ranges whose joint distribution nobody published, and `MBF` — the quantity the Verdict turns on
— has no range at all, so the band would decorate the sag numbers while the safety factor stayed
a point value regardless. If that uncertainty needs to escape the popover, the cheap honest form
is a sensitivity read-out (« garde au sol 5,9 m à `EA` bas »), not an engine change.

`d`'s warning band [6, 26] mm is `[CONVENTION]` — **ours, not §13.1's**: the attested examples
sit at 6 mm, the paywalled EN 12385-4 tables run 10–20 mm, and 26 mm is where zip-line practice
stops. Label it as convention in the UI so it is not mistaken for a standards range.

##### Ligne › Corde textile

| Champ (FR) | Symbole | Unité | Affordance | Dur | Avertissement | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Classe de corde | — | — | select : HMPE 12 torons / Polyamide EN 1891 type A / Polyester semi-statique | requis | — | **HMPE** |
| Charge de rupture | `MBL` | daN *(sélecteur global)* | numérique | `> 0` | — | ***vide, requis*** |
| Masse linéique | `μ` | kg/m | numérique | `> 0` | — | ***vide, requis*** |
| Raideur relative `EA/MBL` | `k` | — | numérique | `> 0`, requis | ⚠ non confirmé + hors bande | **22,0** / **1,5** / *aucun* |

Derived: **`EA = k·MBL`** — the §13.2 convention, never a fibre modulus — plus `w = μ·g`.

**No rope ships.** §13.2 compromises every product row in both research runs, the map's
zero-shipped-presets decision is explicit, and — measured — that one invented `MBL` decides the
verdict: 100 kN ⇒ FS 11,94 « dans les limites »; 60 kN ⇒ 7,63 « hors limites » on the same line.
So the retained inactive textile spec ships classe HMPE with `k` 22 and `Charge de rupture` /
`Masse linéique` **blank and required**. Flipping to `Corde textile` under `TEMPORAIRE`
therefore lands on two blank required fields, which is **not** « Saisie en cours » — a field the
user never filled is not mid-edit, so it raises blocking errors with the culprit table's
attribution and keeps no stale picture.

**Polyester ships `k` blank and `requis`.** §13.2 places it only « between the two » and R1's `k
= 25–35` is refuted. A class whose stiffness field is mandatory and empty states the truth:
nobody published the number, and this is the one class unusable without a datasheet in hand. Its
empty-state message carries that reason rather than the generic one.

**Diamètre is dropped from the textile block.** Nothing in v1 consumes it: §13.2 has no
`MBL(d)`, sheave `D/d` is §13.1 and unused by the Verdict, and §12 dynamics are out of scope. A
decorative field would still reach the URL schema and the first-run defaults while affecting
nothing. Dropped rather than kept-and-ignored, because a field that changes no number teaches
that some fields do not matter. `Classe de corde` drives exactly two things: the `k` default and
the termination whitelist. Not creep grade — §9 is out of scope.

**`k` is a plain editable numeric with a per-class default, and its warning is always on.**
§13.2 indexes `k` by working load (22 @ 10 % MBL, 31 @ 30 % MBL) and **a zip line never enters
that band** — measured 0,6 % MBL standing and 6,3 % loaded on a 200 m HMPE line. HMPE is
insensitive across the whole published range (loaded flèche moves 6 %); **PA is brutally
sensitive** (a 2× swing in garde au sol between `k` 1,5 and the refuted 18); and since §13.2's
elongation rows put the bedding-in toe inside the first 10 % MBL, the true secant `k` below 10 %
is **lower than 22** — the mildest attested value is already optimistic here. So the honesty
mechanism is a result-level warning driven by the computed `% MBL`, ambient rather than an
alarm, worded and tiered in « voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et provenance ».

A plain field rather than a band selector (`10 % / 20 % / 30 % MBL → k`), because the menu
offers three choices **none of which describe the operative regime** and picking from a menu
makes an extrapolation feel like a calibrated selection. And a plain field rather than
auto-iteration on `k(%MBL)`, because iterating extrapolates a two-point table backwards past its
lowest point and dresses the result as convergence — the fabricated-precision failure the theory
doc rules against — and has no defensible answer to *which* tension selects `k`, since the rider
swings tension ~10× between standing and loaded.

##### Terminaisons — one picker per end

`T_allow = η_t·MBF/k` is written with a single `η_t` in §10.1, but a real rig is asymmetric —
socket at the tree, grips at the tensioner — and the engine already reports `T_A` and `T_B`
separately per Sweep Step. **Terminaison A and Terminaison B are separate pickers**, each
setting an **editable** `η_t` (mandatory: every §10.2/§10.3 value is `[CONVENTION]` or a range,
so the binding rule requires it configurable). Two pickers rather than one `min(η)` against
`max(T)`, because per-end is strictly more correct and free.

| Famille | Terminaison | `η_t` défaut | Tag |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Acier | Manchon métal/résine (EN 13411-4) | 1,00 | `[STD-ATTESTED]` |
| | **Cosse à sertir / œil Flamand** (−3) | **0,90** | `[STD-ATTESTED]` |
| | Épissure (−2) | 0,80 | `[STD-ATTESTED]` |
| | Manchon à coin (−6) | 0,80 | `[CONVENTION]` |
| | Serre-câbles étrier (−5) | 0,80 | `[CONVENTION]` + refus `[REGULATOR]` |
| Textile | Œil cousu / épissuré | 0,90 | `[CONVENTION]` |
| | Épissure longue HMPE | 0,90 | `[DATASHEET/CONVENTION]` |
| | Nœud de huit (PA) | 0,60 | `[CONVENTION]`, plage 0,60–0,80 |
| | Nœud de chaise (PA) | 0,55 | `[CONVENTION]`, plage 0,55–0,70 |
| | Nœuds sur HMPE | — | **désactivé** |

Every range takes its conservative end: 0,90 not 0,95; 0,60 not 0,80.

**Provenance: this table's tags govern.** Ticket 05 §5's provenance mapping said the « non
confirmé » chip fires on « toutes sauf manchon », which contradicts the tags above; §6's tags
were elected as the governing ones, so **only Manchon à coin, Serre-câbles étrier and every
textile termination carry the chip**. The consequence is arithmetic and load-bearing: the
first-run aggregate is **4 valeurs** (`m_p`, `h_rider`, `EA` acier, `μ` acier), not 6 — « voir
§ 6 — sorties, verdict et provenance ».

**One rule for prohibitions**, inherited by every later surface:

> If a defensible number exists, let the user compute and let the **Verdict** judge.
> If no number exists, **disable** the option and show the reason.

- **Serre-câbles étrier — selectable, Verdict refuses.** `η_t = 0,80` is attested; « shall not
  be used where the rope supports persons » is `[REGULATOR]`, regulatory rather than physical.
  This is what lets a user model the non-compliant installation they were sent to inspect and be
  told exactly why it fails.
- **Nœuds sur HMPE — disabled with the reason shown**, not because it is forbidden but because
  §10.3's 0,25–0,50 describes a knot that *slips*: there is no efficiency to multiply by, so no
  honest number to compute with.

```
Terminaison B
 (●) Épissure longue
 ( ) Nœud de huit — indisponible
     ℹ §10.3 : sur HMPE le nœud glisse (0,25–0,50).
       Aucun rendement défendable ⇒ épissure obligatoire.
```

Accepted cost, stated so the verdict surfaces inherit it knowingly: the app will render a
computed, plausible-looking result for an illegal rig, relying on the Verdict to carry the
refusal.

##### Override — steel only, per value, stale rather than silent

Textile has **no override affordance**: `MBL` and `μ` are already direct inputs, `EA` is a
read-out, and `k` is the sole source of truth for stiffness. `EA` stays read-only rather than
writable-and-back-solving `k`, because a writable `EA` creates a real ambiguity the moment `MBL`
is edited — « my rope has `k = 22` » and « my rope has `EA = 2200 kN` » disagree about what
survives — which is the same semantic-flip objection that killed three editable tightness
fields.

Steel's three derived values override **independently and persistently**:

```
Valeurs déduites
Charge de rupture  [ 9074 ] daN                    ⟳
Raideur axiale     [ 6,90 ] MN     ⚠ non confirmé  ⟳
Masse linéique     [ 0,5465 ] kg/m ⚠ non confirmé  ⟳

— après passage de d = 12,0 à 16,0 mm —
Charge de rupture  [ 9074 ] daN   valeur saisie
  ⚠ la formule donne 16 129 daN                      [ Recalculer ]
```

- Typing into a derived field flips it to a « valeur saisie » pastille with a reset control
  restoring the formula.
- On a change to `d`, `R₀` or construction, overrides **survive** and each gains a **stale
  badge** carrying the new formula value and a one-click « Recalculer ». **Non-blocking.**
- The badge copy carries **no « maintenant »**: messages state, buttons act, and the reset is a
  button rather than a clause in the sentence.
- **The stale badge is derived, never carried as state.** The URL has no staleness key, so a
  link from a sender whose override was stale would arrive as a plain override and the recipient
  would see a 12 mm breaking force on a 16 mm rope with no badge — the worst thing to hide,
  since `MBF` drives the Verdict. **On every load and every link arrival, each override is
  compared against the formula for the current `(d, R₀, construction)` and badged if it
  differs.**
- **That comparison runs at the field's display precision, never on floats.** A prefill writes
  the *displayed* value into state, so a freshly created override holds `9074` daN against a
  formula value of 9073,98: float equality would badge every override on every load. This is the
  display-precision table's **consumer**, not merely a reader of it.

Survive-with-a-badge rather than either alternative, because both lose information silently:
clearing the override discards datasheet numbers, and keeping it quietly leaves a 16 mm rope
reporting a 12 mm breaking force.

`MBF` is the only derived value shipping **attested**, so overriding it **downgrades**
provenance: a typed `MBF` gains a « valeur saisie » pastille and **enters** the aggregate list —
the mirror image of the rule that editing an Unconfirmed Default never removes it from that
list. The aggregate tracks « not standards-backed » whichever direction you arrived from.

A textile→steel flip can raise a sub-floor `L₀` refusal: the §5.4 bound is `L₀ > chord/(1 +
T_break/EA)`, and `T_break/EA` is ≈ 0,013 for 12 mm steel (bound ≈ 0,987·corde) but ≈ 0,67 for
the PA rope (bound ≈ 0,600·corde). Steel→textile never invalidates `L₀`; textile→steel routinely
can. In direct-`L₀` mode the flip keeps `L₀` and the line changes character completely — same
`L₀`, `EA` 6,90 MN versus 45 kN — which is correct and dramatic enough to look like a broken
chart to someone not expecting it.

#### Tension de la ligne

| Groupe | Champ (FR) | Symbole | Unité | Dur | Avertissement | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tension de la ligne | Flèche à vide cible | `D_target` | m | `> 0` | — | **6,00** *(segment actif)* |
| | Prétension cible | `H_target` | daN | `> 0` | — | *lecture seule* — 253,5 |
| | Longueur non tendue | `L₀` | m | `> 0` | — | *lecture seule* — 151,64 |

Exactly one of the three is state; the other two are read-outs. Which one is editable is the
Tightness Spec, below.

#### Passager

| Groupe | Champ (FR) | Symbole | Unité | Dur | Avertissement | Initial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Passager | Masse | `m_p` | kg | `> 0` | ⚠ non confirmé `[UNVERIFIED:R1]` | **120** |
| | Enveloppe passager | `h_rider` | m | `> 0` | ⚠ non confirmé `[CONVENTION]` | **2,1** |
| | Coefficient dynamique | `DAF` | — | `> 0` | flèche < 3 % (§8.2) | **1,2** |
| | Position du passager | `x_D` | m | `0 ≤ x ≤ L` | — | **milieu de portée** (75,0) |

`m_p` 120 kg is attributed to EN 15567-1 §4.1.2 but not found in the primary text
(`[UNVERIFIED:R1]`); `h_rider` 2,1 m is `[CONVENTION]`. Both are **Unconfirmed Defaults**: a
« non confirmé » chip labels the field and clicking it discloses the provenance and the
§ reference.

```
Passager
Masse              [ 120 ] kg  ⚠ non confirmé
Enveloppe passager [ 2,1 ] m   ⚠ non confirmé

⚠ Masse de calcul non confirmée
  120 kg attribué à EN 15567-1 §4.1.2 mais introuvable dans
  le texte primaire [UNVERIFIED:R1]. Acheter la norme.
  §8.1 · docs/simulation-theory.md
```

A chip plus popover rather than always-visible warning paragraphs under each field, because with
the rider group, the safety regime and several material coefficients all unconfirmed the input
half becomes mostly warning text and users learn to scroll past it — which defeats the rule it
implements. **Editing an Unconfirmed Default flips its chip to « valeur utilisateur » but does
not remove it from the aggregate list**: « no normative value exists » stays true whatever the
user types, and a user-supplied 100 kg is no more standards-backed than the shipped 120 kg.

**`DAF` is a fourth provenance case: well-attested *and* configurable.** `[FIELD:SSF]` is the
highest-trust tag in the reference, so `DAF` is editable with a provenance popover and **no
« non confirmé » chip**. It was added because the verdict has to judge an amplified envelope
`T_vide + DAF·(T_chargé − T_vide)` (§8.2) and no ticket owned the field; it deliberately does
**not** touch clearance.

**`x_D` is a view parameter and it clamps on every path** — geometry change, link arrival and
typed input — because `x` selects a view, not a design input, and refusing to draw a valid span
because the viewpoint is stale is worse than moving the viewpoint. This is a deliberate
exception to the typed-blocks / dragged-clamps rule that governs stations, and the exception is
**not silent**:

```
Position du passager  [ 150,0 ] m
  position ramenée à la portée : 0 – 150,0 m
```

A transient inline note rather than a blocking error, because that satisfies
never-discard-silently without inventing a blocking error for a viewpoint. The clamped value is
what the debounced URL write publishes. `x_D` sits in **no gate**.

#### Valeurs déduites

Read-outs, never editable. The split against the rail's `Résultats` section is **whether the
rider is involved**: everything rider-free is a deduced value, everything traverse-dependent is
a result. Order as shipped:

| Valeur déduite | Symbole | Unité | Présent quand | Premier lancement |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Charge de rupture | `MBF` / `MBL` | daN | toujours | 9074 daN |
| Raideur axiale | `EA` | **MN** *(dérogation déclarée)* | toujours | 6,90 MN |
| Masse linéique | `μ` | kg/m | acier (textile : c'est un champ) | 0,5465 kg/m |
| Poids linéique | `w` | daN/m | toujours | 0,536 daN/m |
| Corde | `√(L²+h²)` | m | toujours | 151,08 m |
| Longueur non tendue | `L₀` | m | hors mode Longueur | 151,64 m |
| Prétension | `H à vide` | daN | hors mode Tension | 253,5 daN |
| Flèche à vide | — | m | hors mode Flèche | 6,00 m *(à x 74,8 m)* |
| Flèche / portée | — | % | toujours | 4,00 % |
| Tension permanente | — | % MBF | toujours | 2,90 % MBF |
| Portée | `L` | m | **mode importé seul** | — |
| Dénivelé | `h` | m | **mode importé seul** | — |

`corde` and `w` are the only two rows independent of a solve, which is why they survive « Aucun
résultat » while every other row is replaced by the cause line (« voir § 7 — validation et états
d'erreur »).

One list rather than two competing ones: an earlier draft had a deduced-values block *and* a
results block both claiming the same read-outs, and the rider test is what separates them — both
the §8.2 stiffening rule and the §9.2 creep cap are about the **standing** line, so `flèche /
portée` and `tension permanente` belong here and not beside `T max`.

### Tightness Spec — a segmented control with exactly one editable field

```
Tension de la ligne
┌────────┬──────────┬──────────┐
│ Flèche │ Tension  │ Longueur │
└────────┴──────────┴──────────┘
Flèche à vide cible   [ 6,00 ] m

── Valeurs déduites ─────────────
Prétension           253,5 daN
Longueur non tendue  151,64 m
```

A segmented control (`Flèche` / `Tension` / `Longueur`) picks the driving quantity — the
**Tightness Driver**. Exactly one field is editable; the other two appear under `Valeurs
déduites` as unambiguous outputs. Switching mode **pre-fills the new field from its read-out**,
which is why « cible 250 daN » → « longueur directe » lands on the length just achieved.

A segmented control with one editable field rather than three always-visible editable fields
with a radio marking the driver, because with three fields every keystroke can flip which
quantity is the model's meaning, and the two passive fields would need stale-then-fresh states
during every recompute. Also rejected: **hiding direct `L₀`** — practitioners rarely think in
unstretched length, but `L₀` is the internal state variable and the `ΔL₀` story of §9 needs it
typeable.

**`Flèche` ships as the active segment.** Sag is the quantity a rigger can verify on site with a
tape, and it is the same quantity promoted to a *validity* read-out, so the driving input and
the warning that governs it are one number on the first screen. It is also the only mode whose
shipped value is **exact at display precision**: a direct-`L₀` default would have to ship a
rounded value, and 1 decimal there costs 0,21 m of flèche and 0,20 m of garde — 8 % of the
shipped margin. Rejected `Tension` (a force in the driver field on first paint reads as an
arbitrary constant until the user knows the cable, and the flèche/portée validity read-out
becomes a consequence the user cannot steer) and rejected `Longueur` (it ships exactly that
rounding problem).

The **prefill writes the displayed value into state, never the underlying float** — 201,84
enters state, 201,8412773 never does. That is what makes the URL exact and short by
construction, and it is what makes the table under *Display precision per quantity* load-bearing
state rather than cosmetics.

### The §10.4 use-class gate — disabled with the reason shown

`docs/simulation-theory.md` §10.4: « `installation_type = PERMANENT` ⇒ steel only (hard
validation); `TEMPORARY_RESCUE` ⇒ textile allowed with the B-family warnings ». The boundary is
a **use-class boundary, not a physical ban**.

```
Type d'installation
 (●) Permanente (public)
 ( ) Temporaire — secours & expédition

Famille de ligne
 (●) Câble acier
 ( ) Corde textile  — indisponible
     ℹ §10.4 : fluage, UV/abrasion et fusion par friction
       (HMPE ~144–152 °C) ⇒ acier seul en installation permanente
```

Textile stays **visible but disabled** under `PERMANENTE` with the reason inline, rather than
hidden: the rule is a fact worth teaching, and hiding the option leaves a user who came to
compare families with no explanation.

The transition path — switching **to** `PERMANENTE` while textile is already configured — is a
**blocking error with Fix Actions, never a silent auto-switch**, because an auto-switch would
discard the Material Spec the user just built. It offers **two** Fix Actions, `[ Passer à
l'acier ]` first (the conservative branch) and `[ Passer en usage temporaire ]` second: for an
arrived link neither repair is privileged, and withholding the second forces a recipient to
discard the Material Spec the link was sent to show them. `[ Passer à l'acier ]` restores a
prior steel spec if one exists and defaults otherwise. Copy and precedence are « voir § 7 —
validation et états d'erreur ».

### Units, the force selector, and the two constants

- **SI internally, always.** Canonical storage is **newtons**, so unit round-trips are lossless.
- A **global force-unit selector** — `daN` (default) / `kN` / `kgf` — applies to every force in
  the app; **never per-field**. It lives in the Rail Header and persists in both the URL (`u`)
  and the Session as a display preference.
- **The unit invariant: a unit switch never writes state.** `daN → X → daN` is the identity.
  State stays canonical newtons, each unit carries its own display precision, and the URL keeps
  serialising **daN**. The rule that a prefill writes the displayed value into state governs
  prefills **from computed read-outs only** (the tightness mode switch, « Recalculer » on a
  stale override); a unit switch writes **nothing**.

  « Le changement d'unité n'écrit jamais dans l'état ; daN→X→daN est l'identité. »

  Without this invariant, MBF 9074 daN → 90,7 kN → 9070 daN moves the quantity the Verdict
  divides by, by 4 daN per toggle, drifting on repeat — and the claim that a display control is
  provably physics-neutral, which is what kills the 10× input-error class, quietly stops
  holding.
- **Forces in the URL are always daN and `u` is display-only.** If the URL's numbers followed
  the selector, hand-editing `u=daN`→`u=kN` would make the line 100× tighter. Physics-neutrality
  has to hold at the serialisation boundary too.
- **Strict global, one declared carve-out.** A `kN` carve-out for breaking loads was rejected:
  it would put « MBF 90,7 kN » above « tension 437 daN » and a user eyeballing whether 437 sits
  safely under 90,7 is wrong by a factor of 100 — a worse error class than the typo it fixes,
  and unavoidable, whereas the typo has a clean escape (switch the selector to kN and every
  field converts losslessly, including the one about to be typed).

  **The carve-out that survives, declared: `EA` renders in MN.** The argument above is about
  *eyeball comparability*, and `EA` is never compared to a tension by eye — it is three orders
  of magnitude larger and consumed only inside ratios. It renders as « 6,90 MN » under every
  setting of the selector, and it serialises in **daN** in the URL like every other force.
  Declared as a carve-out with that reason, not left as an inconsistency.
- **`w` follows the force unit**: daN/m, kN/m, kgf/m.
- **`g` is fixed at `G = 9.81` and not exposed.** It varies ≤ 0,3 % over the Earth, immaterial
  beside the ±15 % spread in `EA` and `MBF`, and exposing it would add a fourth way to get a
  wrong answer plus URL state and fixture surface. 9.81 is what §8.1 (`120 kg → 1.177 kN`) and
  fixture Z8 use; nothing in §15's statics fixtures constrains it, since they pin `w` directly
  (`w = 5 N/m`).
- **`kgf` uses `KGF = 9.80665`**, its strict definition. Two constants, each correct in its
  role: `G` for physics, `KGF` for the unit. A 120 kg mass therefore reads as **120,04 kgf**;
  the 0,03 % discrepancy is disclosed in the kgf note rather than papered over.

### Numeric input — the widget is a correctness question

Every numeric field is **`<input type="text" inputMode="decimal">`**.

| Boundary | Decimal mark | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Field display | **`,`** | French copy, `<html lang="fr">` |
| Field input | `,` **and** `.` both accepted | a keypad may offer either |
| URL (`?L=150&h=-18`) | `.` | the schema is fixed and hand-editable |
| `[ Copier le diagnostic ]` payload | `.` | evidence, full float precision, English keys |

**`type="number"` is rejected.** Its acceptance of `,` is browser- and locale-dependent and the
failure is **silent**: the element reports an empty value, so a rigger who types « 6,5 » loses
the field with no message. Losing `type=number`'s free min/max is no loss — this section owns
the ranges, and the URL *requires* out-of-range values to be representable so a link can restore
them faithfully.

The widget creates one message that `type=number` had no place for: since unparseable text is
now a reachable state, « nombre illisible » exists and **quotes the raw text back**, because
what the user typed is the evidence. Full message table: « voir § 7 — validation et états
d'erreur ».

### Display precision per quantity

**This table is a measured sensitivity decision, not a mechanical derivation.** An earlier
handoff called it « derivable from units and hard ranges, nothing left to decide, only to
tabulate » and that is **false**: `L₀` is the sharp case, and at the first-run line 1 decimal
(151,6 against 151,644 m) shifts flèche by 0,21 m, `H` by 3,5 % and garde by **0,20 m — 8 % of
the shipped clearance margin**, where 2 decimals costs 0,02 m. Each row therefore states a
*minimum*, with its reason.

The table has three consumers, and two of them are not readers:

1. **The prefill rule.** A prefill from a read-out writes the *displayed* value into state, so a
   coarse row silently moves the line.
2. **The stale Override badge**, which compares override against formula **at display
   precision**. A missing row there is not a formatting gap — it is a badge that fires on every
   load for every override.
3. **The first-run regression lock**, which asserts **formatted strings** rather than floats, so
   every row below is frozen by a test.

Two formatting rules, then the rows.

- **Fixed decimals, padded.** A field or read-out at 2 decimals renders « 6,00 », never « 6 ».
  Padding is display-only: state holds a *number* whose value equals the displayed value, so the
  URL serialises `6` and the field still shows « 6,00 ».
- **Rounding is always visible**, because the field shows exactly what state holds. A user who
  types 82,5 kg sees « 83 kg » commit; nothing is rounded behind the user's back. That property
  is what makes coarse rows on insensitive quantities acceptable.
- **Force units derive from the daN column**: `kN` = daN decimals **+ 2** (1 kN = 100 daN),
  `kgf` = daN decimals (1 kgf ≈ 0,98 daN). So `MBF` reads 9074 daN / 90,74 kN, and a tension
  reads 253,5 daN / 2,535 kN. No per-unit row is needed and the resolution never coarsens on a
  switch.

#### Inputs and deduced values

| Grandeur | Symbole | Unité affichée | Décimales | Marque | Premier lancement | Minimum justifié par |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Portée | `L` | m | 1 | `,` | 150,0 | 0,1 m sur 50–2000 m ; jamais préremplie |
| Dénivelé | `h` | m | 1 | `,` | −18,0 | idem ; 44 m de dénivelé ne valent que 0,21 m de garde |
| Hauteur d'ancrage A / B | — | m | 1 | `,` | 12,0 | garde bouge mètre pour mètre ⇒ 0,1 m suffit |
| Station A / B | — | m | 1 | `,` | — | grain du fichier (`Distance` écrite à 0,1 m) |
| Flèche à vide cible | `D_target` | m | **2** | `,` | 6,00 | **préremplie depuis un read-out** ; 1 déc. ⇒ ±0,05 m de garde par aller-retour de mode |
| Prétension cible | `H_target` | daN | 1 | `,` | 253,5 | préremplie ; 0,05 daN est sans effet |
| Longueur non tendue | `L₀` | m | **2** | `,` | 151,64 | **mesuré** : 1 déc. = 0,21 m de flèche, 0,20 m de garde, 8 % de la marge |
| Diamètre nominal | `d` | mm | **1** | `,` | 12,0 | `MBF ∝ d²` : arrondir 12,7 → 13 gonfle `MBF` de 4,8 % du mauvais côté |
| Raideur relative | `k` | — | 1 | `,` | 22,0 / 1,5 | les deux défauts publiés (22 et 1,5) doivent s'afficher tels quels |
| Charge de rupture | `MBL` | daN | 0 | `,` | *vide* | saisie depuis une fiche technique, toujours entière en daN |
| Masse linéique | `μ` | kg/m | 4 | `,` | 0,5465 | `μ` acier est déduit à 4 déc. ; entre dans `w` linéairement |
| Rendement de terminaison | `η_A`, `η_B` | — | 2 | `,` | 0,90 | les valeurs §10.2/§10.3 sont publiées à 2 déc. |
| Masse du passager | `m_p` | kg | 0 | `,` | 120 | 1 kg = 0,8 % de `P`, un ordre sous le ±15 % de `EA`/`MBF` |
| Enveloppe passager | `h_rider` | m | 1 | `,` | 2,1 | soustraite directement de la garde ; 0,1 m = 4 % de la marge livrée |
| Coefficient dynamique | `DAF` | — | 1 | `,` | 1,2 | multiplie un écart de tension ; 0,05 sur 664 daN = 33 daN |
| Position du passager | `x_D` | m | 1 | `,` | 75,0 | 1 px ≈ 1,2 m à 1500 m ⇒ 0,1 m est déjà sous le pointeur |
| Charge de rupture | `MBF` | daN | 0 | `,` | 9074 | **consommé par le badge d'override** : la formule donne 9073,98 |
| Raideur axiale | `EA` | **MN** | 2 | `,` | 6,90 | idem badge ; 0,01 MN = 0,15 % sur une plage de ×1,52 |
| Poids linéique | `w` | daN/m | 3 | `,` | 0,536 | `w = μ·g` avec `μ` à 4 déc. |
| Corde | — | m | 2 | `,` | 151,08 | comparée à `L₀` (2 déc.) dans la borne §5.4 |
| Flèche à vide | — | m | 2 | `,` | 6,00 | même grandeur que `D_target`, même grain |
| Flèche / portée | — | % | 2 | `,` | 4,00 | le seuil §8.2 est à 3 % ; 4,00 vs 3,99 est lisible |
| Tension permanente | — | % MBF | 2 | `,` | 2,90 | le plafond §9.2 est à 30 % ; le régime utile est sous 7 % |

#### Results and read-out cells

Consumed by the rail's output half and the five read-out cells (« voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et
provenance »); rows live here because the table is one table and the first-run lock freezes
these strings.

| Grandeur | Unité | Décimales | Marque | Premier lancement |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `T_A` / `T_B` max statiques | daN | 1 | `,` | 817,1 / 793,5 |
| Enveloppe DAF | daN | 1 | `,` | 927,9 / 901,5 |
| Flèche chargée max | m | 2 | `,` | 7,48 |
| Garde min | m | 2 | `,` | 2,44 |
| Position d'un extremum (`x`) | m | 1 | `,` | 74,1 |
| `FS effectif` | — | 2 | `,` | 8,80 / 9,06 |
| `T adm` par extrémité | daN | 0 | `,` | 2722 |
| Réserve de tension | daN | 0 | `,` | −111 |
| Pente locale | % | 1 | `,` | −11,1 |
| Couverture | pas | 0 | — | 260 / 260 |

Three of those rows — `FS effectif`, `T adm` and `Réserve de tension` — are régime-dependent and
therefore **absent** on the first screen (« Résultats sans régime »); the figures quoted are
what they read once a régime is chosen — 2722 daN and +1794 daN under EN 15567-1, −111 daN under
ACCT 10:1.

One collision to settle at review, recorded rather than hidden: the first-run regression lock
freezes « 817,1 daN » (1 decimal) while the rail and read-out-cell **sketches** print « T max
817 daN ». The frozen strings govern — they are the thing asserted by a test — so tension
read-outs carry 1 decimal everywhere and the sketches are the loose rendering. Anyone who
prefers 0 decimals for the five cells must change the lock's expectations in the same commit.

### The three validation gates

There is no single form tier. Ticket 04 §7's FORM tier **splits into three named gates with
distinct consequences**, and « régime choisi » is **struck** from the solve tier: it never
blocks a solve.

**The table itself is written once, in « voir § 7 — validation et états d'erreur », together
with the evidence that forced the split** — the three fields of the `Passager` group land in
three *different* gates, which is itself why the split was right. Read the « Dur » column of
every table above against these input lists: **Solve Gate** = `L`, `h`, the driving tightness
field, family + Material Spec, §10.4 family × usage, `m_p` · **Clearance Gate** = hauteurs
