undeclared it reads as installable-PWA, which adds a caching layer that can serve a **stale
build of a safety tool**: the one failure mode a provenance-obsessed app must not acquire by
accident.* No terrain ever crosses a network boundary, which the client-side decode already
guarantees structurally.

Static hosting is not a v1 requirement and is not decided here. Recorded for whoever asks later:
`spa: {}` (`start-plugin-core/dist/esm/schema.js:109-119`, prerendering the shell to `/_shell`
with `maskPath: '/'`) is the migration path, it changes no route code, and it **must not be
enabled speculatively** — under `ssr: false` it buys only a build-time prerender of a frame the
server already returns in 200.

#### `docs/conventions/routing.md` is amended

The doc asserted plain SSR and now asserts « SSR is on for the document and off for the app »,
the `defaultSsr` no-op, the 3×-per-request purity rule, the omit-never-`undefined` rule, the
`useSearch` trap and the offline declaration. **The amendment is already applied** — treat
`docs/conventions/routing.md` as binding and keep it in sync if any of the above moves.

### Implementation trap: `Route.useSearch()` is raw ∪ validated

Record this beside the key table, because the type system hides it:

> `Route.useSearch()` returns **raw ∪ validated** at runtime
> (`router-core/dist/esm/router.js:870`, `:918`; `react-router/dist/esm/useSearch.js:22`) while
> its TypeScript type is **validated-only**.

**App code reads one derived Scenario object built from the validated keys, never the raw bag.**
This is why « clé ignorée » is an **app-level guarantee the router does not enforce**, and why
the rewrite must build a plain object rather than spread `useSearch()`.

### Contracts

Declarations only — these are fixed by the key table and by the Session's content, and are
contract, not implementation.

```ts
// src/lib/url/schema.ts — the validated search object, one per key-table row.
// Optionality mirrors the "Présence" column: an absent key is absent, never `undefined`.
type ForceDaN = number; // every force in the URL is daN, always
type TerminationToken =                     // `fam` fixes which set is legal
  | "manchon" | "cosse" | "coin" | "serre"  // acier
  | "oeil" | "huit" | "chaise"              // textile
  | "epissure";                             // both families — listed once, deliberately
type ValidatedSearch = {
  v?: 1;
  u?: "daN" | "kN" | "kgf";
  use?: "perm" | "tempo";
  reg?: "en15567" | "wsqld" | "acct10" | "ansi5" | "secours10";
  L?: number; h?: number;                     // manual terrain only
  sa?: number; sb?: number; tr?: string;      // imported terrain only; `tr` verbatim `p1.…`
  za?: number; zb?: number;
  fl?: number; te?: ForceDaN; lo?: number;    // exactly one — the Tightness Driver
  fam?: "ac" | "tx";
  d?: number; r0?: 1770 | 1960; cst?: "tc" | "iwrc";
  mbf?: ForceDaN; ea?: ForceDaN; mu?: number; // steel overrides; absent = non surchargée
  cls?: "hmpe" | "pa" | "pes"; mbl?: ForceDaN; k?: number;
  // termination tokens: the union of both families; `fam` fixes which set is legal
  ta?: TerminationToken; tb?: TerminationToken; na?: number; nb?: number;
  mp?: number; hr?: number; daf?: number;
  x?: number;
};

// src/lib/url/p1.ts — synchronous, isomorphic, no streams.
declare function encodeP1(trace: { d: Float64Array; z: Float64Array; dev: number }): string;
declare function validateP1(raw: string): string;        // structural only; verbatim, or throws
declare function decodeP1(raw: string): { d: Float64Array; z: Float64Array; dev: number };

// src/lib/session/session.ts — `scoutech:session:v1`, version in the key.
declare function readSession(): Session | null;          // synchronous, lazy-initialiser only
declare function writeSession(s: Session): void;         // debounced ≈1 s by the caller
declare function clearSession(): void;                   // « Effacer la session », confirmed
```

`Session` is the Scenario plus both Material Specs, the retained manual `L`/`h`, the cached
Trace and its filename, and the display preferences — strictly larger than what a link carries.


---

## § 8 — State, the URL schema, persistence, and the app shell

This is the app's complete error vocabulary. It is a **contract**, not a detail: every other
section of this spec refers to it, because in a tool whose output is a safety margin the rules
governing *when a number may not appear* are as load-bearing as the formulas that produce it.

Source: [Validation and error-state UX](./issues/11-validation-and-error-state-ux.md) in full,
with the cross-ticket rulings of ELECTIONS §0.1–§0.4 folded in. Two of ticket 11's own eight
opening items were **misfiled** and the spec must not reproduce them:

- `slack-limit` and « la ligne est beaucoup trop molle » are **not refusals**. Deep slack
  *converges*: on a 200 m span with anchor bases at −4 / −2,5 m, `L₀ = 200,5 m` gives coverage
  1,000 and `h_min = −7,59 m` with `contact: "cable"`; `L₀ = 400 m` gives `h_min = −169,67 m`,
  still `contact: "cable"`. A line lying down is therefore the **« Câble au sol »** verdict
  (voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et provenance), not a solver error.
- `L₀` below the §5.4 floor is **not** a safety verdict but the hardest refusal in the app,
  because the chord bound *is* the breaking load (§ Tension impossible below). Ticket 11's own
  framing — « the solve succeeds and returns an enormous `H`, so this surfaces as a safety
  verdict » — is measurably false.

### Les quatre invariants

Each is stated as an invariant because each is violated by the obvious implementation, and each
carries its reason inline — without the reason it reads like a style rule and gets
« simplified » later.

| # | Invariant | Le mécanisme qu'il protège |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Aucun contrôle dur ne s'affiche uniquement sur un read-out.** | Every hard check is declared on a **quantity** and attributed to an editable field by the culprit table below. Ticket 04 hung its ranges on *fields*, but `L`/`h` are derived in imported mode and `L₀` is a read-out in two of the three tightness modes — so `invalid-input{field:"L0"}` already pointed at an uneditable field before terrain existed. |
| 2 | **Aucun message d'erreur n'affirme un acte.** | Every state here is reachable **on the first frame of a link** (§ État d'URL et persistance: the URL restores state faithfully, including invalid state), so « vous avez saisi » / « maintenant » are lies half the time. The rule is narrow: *messages* state a property of the world; **button labels describe the effect of pressing them**, which is accurate whether the state was entered or arrived. |
| 3 | **Aucun verdict ne repose sur des nombres dont il n'a pas été calculé.** | This is what forces the « Saisie en cours » / « Aucun résultat » split below. It also narrows `stale` to exactly one meaning: **un recalcul en vol sur des entrées encore valides**. Nothing else in the app may be called stale. |
| 4 | **Le bandeau n'a qu'un seul occupant à la fois.** | Ticket 07's spine bought exactly one banner band and ticket 08 refused stacking; a stack would let a dismissible link notice sit above a « Hors limites ». Precedence in § Le bandeau below. |

A fifth, weaker, but load-bearing three times: **un refus ne détruit jamais une valeur que
l'utilisateur possède encore** — a rejected import keeps the current terrain, an out-of-range
`x_D` clamps rather than blocking the span, a family flip keeps both Material Specs.

### Les trois grilles de validation

Ticket 04 §7's single FORM tier is **struck** and replaced by three named gates with distinct
consequences (ELECTIONS §0.1), completed by ticket 11 §1 with the three Passager fields that no
gate claimed:

| Grille | Entrées | Ce que l'échec supprime |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Solve Gate** | `L`, `h`, le champ de tension directeur, famille + Material Spec, famille × usage (§10.4), `m_p` | tout l'aval : ni `SpanState`, ni câble, ni read-out |
| **Clearance Gate** | hauteur d'ancrage A / B, terrain (stations), `h_rider` | la garde au sol et la courbe de garde **seules** — le câble se dessine |
| **Verdict Gate** | régime, les deux terminaisons, `DAF` | le bandeau et la grille critères × extrémités **seuls** |

The Passager assignments are **forced by decisions already made, not chosen** — and they land in
three *different* gates, which is itself the evidence the split was right:

- `m_p` is the magnitude of the single `PointLoad` handed to `solveSpan`. With no rider there is
  no loaded `SpanState`: no cable, no `T_A`/`T_B`, nothing in the five read-out cells. **Solve
  Gate.**
- `h_rider` enters only `evaluateClearance`'s envelope, and the « Enveloppe passager traversée »
  verdict is true *only given* `h_rider`. **Clearance Gate** — exactly the row ELECTIONS §0.1
  wrote for the anchor heights.
- `DAF` enters only `T_vide + DAF·(T_chargé − T_vide)` and **does not touch clearance**.
  **Verdict Gate** — and its failure additionally removes the « ↳ majoration dynamique » detail
  row, which is the DAF decomposition and nothing else.

Three consequences, each of which is a rejection worth keeping:

- **« Régime choisi » is struck from the FORM tier.** It never blocks a solve. That is what
  makes the verdict-less first run coherent: as ticket 04 wrote it, first run blocked the engine
  and showed an empty chart, destroying ticket 04 §1's own reason for inventing manual-mode
  ground (« la garde au sol est vivante dès le premier lancement »).
- **Trois grilles plutôt qu'une quatrième grille « sans passager »**, qui garderait les `Valeurs
  déduites` vivantes en n'effaçant que l'aval du passager: `m_p` ships at 120 kg and an
  incomplete link completes it from defaults, so an empty `m_p` is reachable only by a user
  clearing the field — which « Saisie en cours » already routes to a state that keeps **all**
  the numbers. A fourth gate would buy a worse version of a state that already exists.
- **`x_D` sits in no gate**: it clamps (§ Le clamp de `x_D`).

**When the Clearance Gate fails, the strip keeps its axes and stays a scrub track.** `x` maps to
rider position whether or not clearance data exists; ticket 07 §2 made the strip the app's only
pointer input path and it may never stop being one. The single exception is written down under
« Aucun résultat » below — implement the keep-the-scrub-track rule unconditionally and someone
computes a scale over a NaN domain.

### Deux états par champ — et un troisième message que le widget élu crée

The split is on whether a value was **committed** (ELECTIONS §0.3), so every field carries two
typographically distinct messages: the empty state is **neutral, lowercase, no icon**; the
committed-invalid state is **⛔ and blocking**. Calling both « erreur » would make ordinary
typing feel like a fault, which is the whole point of the split.

**The elected input widget creates a third message.** Every numeric field is `<input type="text"
inputMode="decimal">` accepting both `,` and `.` and always displaying `,` (ELECTIONS §0.6, voir
§ 3 — le modèle de paramètres, *Précision d'affichage par grandeur*). With that widget
**unparseable text is a reachable state** — `type="number"` hid it by reporting an *empty*
value, precisely the silent failure it was rejected for: a rigger who types « 6,5 » loses the
field with no message. So **« nombre illisible » is a message this app needs and `type=number`
had no place for**, and it **quotes the raw text back**, because what the user typed is the
evidence.

| Champ | Contrôle dur | Vide (neutre) | Valeur engagée invalide (⛔) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Portée `L` | `> 0`, fini | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Portée — une longueur strictement positive est requise. Valeur : −5,0 m. » |
| Dénivelé `h` | fini | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Dénivelé — nombre illisible. Saisie : « 12,3,4 ». » |
| Hauteur d'ancrage A / B | `≥ 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Hauteur d'ancrage A — une hauteur négative n'existe pas. Valeur : −2,0 m. » |
| Flèche à vide cible | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Flèche à vide cible — une flèche strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0 m. » |
| Prétension cible | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Prétension cible — une tension strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0 daN. » |
| Longueur non tendue | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Longueur non tendue — une longueur strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0 m. » |
| Masse `m_p` | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Masse du passager — une masse strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0 kg. » |
| Enveloppe passager `h_rider` | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Enveloppe passager — une hauteur strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0 m. » |
| Coefficient dynamique `DAF` | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Coefficient dynamique — une valeur strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0. » |
| Diamètre nominal `d` | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Diamètre nominal — un diamètre strictement positif est requis. Valeur : 0 mm. » |
| Charge de rupture `MBL` | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Charge de rupture — une charge strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0 daN. » |
| Masse linéique `μ` | `> 0` | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Masse linéique — une valeur strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0 kg/m. » |
| Raideur relative `k` | `> 0`, requis | « valeur requise — aucune valeur publiée pour le polyester » | « ⛔ Raideur relative k — une valeur strictement positive est requise. Valeur : 0. » |
| Station A / B | dans le tracé | « valeur requise » | « ⛔ Station hors du tracé (0 – 224,0 m). Valeur : 260,0 m. » |
| Station B ≤ Station A | `L > 0` | — | « ⛔ Portée nulle ou négative — l'ancrage B est avant l'ancrage A. » `[ Inverser A et B ]` |
| Type d'installation × famille | §10.4 | — | « ⛔ Corde textile impossible en installation permanente (§10.4). » `[ Passer à l'acier ]` `[ Passer en usage temporaire ]` |
| Régime × famille / usage | ticket 08 §5 | — | « ⛔ Régime « Secours textile 10:1 » indisponible avec un câble acier. » `[ Choisir un autre régime ]` |

**Template, fixed:** `⛔ <Champ> — <la règle, énoncée>. Valeur : <v>.` The rule is stated as a
property of the world, **never as a reproach** (invariant 2), and the offending value is
**always echoed** — on an arrived link the user has not seen it yet.

Notes that are decisions, not detail:

- `k`'s *empty* message carries its reason, because for polyester the blank is not an omission:
  **nobody published the number.**
- `Grade`, `Construction` and `Classe de corde` are selects that ship with a value and cannot be
  emptied, so their `requis` needs no message. **Terminations are handled by disabling with the
  reason, never by an error** — no `η_t` exists for knots on HMPE, so there is no number to
  judge.
- **Warning-tier copy is deliberately absent from this table.** `L ∉ [50, 2000]`, `|h| > L`,
  `hauteur > 30`, `d ∉ [6, 26]` are non-blocking form warnings (voir § 3 — le modèle de
  paramètres) and result-level warnings are the three-tier ladder (voir § 6 — sorties, verdict
  et provenance). Two idioms, two owners.

### Le bandeau : un occupant, quatre rangs

Precedence (ELECTIONS §0.4, completed by ticket 11 §3 with the state ELECTIONS §0.3 created):

1. **Blocking errors**, any gate — **count + jump link only**.
2. **Les trois états de refus** (§ Les trois refus) — same visual class as blocking.
3. **« Saisie en cours »** — neutral.
4. **The verdict** (five states, voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et provenance) **or** the no-regime
   prompt.

Ranks 3 and 4 are mutually exclusive by construction: an empty required field suppresses the
verdict, so nothing at rank 4 can coexist with rank 3. Ranks 4's two occupants are mutually
exclusive too, so their relative order is moot.

**Not slot occupants:** the ignored-keys strip (it is about the link, not the line, and must
never be able to hide a verdict), import parse failures (they render in the import block), the
coarse-terrain ⚠ (Provenance — it never promotes). The refusal page pre-empts everything by
being the index route's `errorComponent`.

#### Le résumé bloquant nomme la pire grille franchie

ELECTIONS §0.4 fixed the summary as « ⛔ 2 erreurs bloquantes — aucun résultat ». That copy is
true **only for a Solve Gate breach**: under the three-gate split a bad `DAF` blocks the verdict
while `T max` and `Garde min` are printed on screen, and « aucun résultat » beside a printed
clearance is the app contradicting itself. The summary therefore names the **worst gate
breached**:

```
Solve Gate      ⛔ 2 erreurs bloquantes — aucun résultat              [ voir la première ]
Clearance Gate  ⛔ 1 erreur bloquante — garde au sol indisponible      [ voir la première ]
Verdict Gate    ⛔ 1 erreur bloquante — aucun verdict                  [ voir la première ]
```

The slot carries **only** the count and the jump link; the actionable message and its Fix Action
live **inline on the culpable field**.

**Blocking errors are a list, not a singleton.** `use=perm & fam=tx & reg=secours10` raises two
at once, **family first**, and applying one Fix Action leaves the other standing rather than
cascading a silent second repair.

Sizing, inherited from § Mise en page responsive: the banner slot is **44–76 px of content
height** and reduces to a single line while the soft keyboard is open. The Skeleton **reserves
the band's height and paints nothing in it** — no border, no fill, no text — which is what makes
rank 1's first real occupant land with no reflow on hydration. Reserving height is layout;
painting a band is a statement.

### « Saisie en cours » — le dernier Sweep valide, marqué, écritures suspendues

**Firing rule: a required field is empty *and holds focus*.** Mid-edit, not an error.

The focus clause is what keeps the state bounded — mid-edit lasts as long as the caret is in the
field. A required field that is empty and **blurred**, or that was never filled, is not mid-edit
at all and falls to « Aucun résultat » **with no stale picture**. Without the clause, walking
away from a half-typed form leaves a stale Sweep on screen indefinitely, which is the one thing
the split exists to prevent.

```
[ bandeau ]  Saisie en cours — ces valeurs correspondent à Flèche à vide cible 6,00 m.
```

- The chart and both read-out blocks keep the **last valid Sweep**, and the chart **freezes
  rather than dims** — **one worded mark for the whole output half**, never a per-row or
  per-gate stale matrix. The mark **names the field and its last committed value**, which
  attributes every number on screen unambiguously, where opacity only says « something here is
  provisional » while degrading legibility of exactly the quantity the user is steering toward.
  *Freezing rather than dimming, because at 390 px the collapsed two-row safety header has no
  space for a decorative state* — and freezing makes an earlier draft's exemption list (danger
  bands and the red cable/rider held at full strength inside a dimmed chart) unnecessary:
  nothing fades in the first place.
- **Scrubbing goes inert**, pointer and typed `x` alike. This is the app's second inert scrub
  track, and it is a different case from the first: the Clearance-Gate protection above keeps
  the track live because the Sweep is still *valid*, whereas here the Sweep is stale by
  definition, so scrubbing would let the user **generate new stale numbers** rather than read
  the ones already on screen. The exact typed-`x` solve cannot run on an incomplete input set
  anyway.
- **The verdict banner is suppressed** (rank 3) — the same slot behaviour as no-regime, **not a
  sixth verdict state**.
- Every solve-dependent number keeps its value, so « la garde au sol est vivante dès le premier
  lancement » survives ordinary typing.
- **The debounced URL write and the Session write are suspended**, so the address bar keeps the
  last complete Scenario. **« Copier le lien » is disabled** with « Complétez les champs vides
  pour partager ce lien. »
- The empty field carries its neutral message from the field table.

Two rejections, in opposite directions. *Freezing the marked output rather than blanking the
output half on every keystroke that momentarily empties a field*, because blanking is visually
violent and punishes correct behaviour — clearing a field to retype it is how numeric input
works. And *suspending the URL write rather than keeping it live*, which would publish a
half-typed scenario into the address bar and into « Copier le lien ».

### « Aucun résultat » — une cause, trois échos, et la seule piste de scrub inerte

Trigger: a **committed** invalid value (typed `−5`, or arrived in a link) **or** any of the
three refusals below.

**No stale numbers, ever.** The alternative breaks invariant 3: a rigger reading `FS effectif
4,12` beside a blocking error has been told the line is fine **by the largest number on screen**
— and a link makes this reachable on a first frame where there is no « last good » to fall back
on, so « keep the last good » has no value to offer in the case that matters.

The cause sentence is written **once** and **echoed in each suppressed region** — not per row,
which is the warning wall ticket 04 rejected, and not once only, because the Rail is one
scrolling column (voir § 8 — état, URL et coquille applicative) so a rail reader may never have
the banner in view:

```
[ bandeau ]        ⛔ 1 erreur bloquante — aucun résultat        [ voir la première ]
[ 5 cellules ]     Aucun résultat — portée invalide.
── RÉSULTATS ──    Aucun résultat — portée invalide.
── VALEURS DÉDUITES ──
  corde · w                   (présents — indépendants de la résolution)
  L₀ · H à vide · flèche à vide · flèche/portée · tension permanente
                              Aucun résultat — portée invalide.
```

**Inputs still draw**: terrain, both anchors, the span markers, the 5 % margin. **Absent**: the
cable, the unloaded line, the clearance curve, the danger bands, the rider marker.

The five read-out cells are **replaced by one line spanning the row**, keeping the row's height
(the two-row ≈104 px band, so nothing jumps). *One spanning line rather than « — » per cell*,
because that glyph is spent on `Indéterminé` — *attempted, unavailable* — and reusing it for
*nothing attempted* collides with the distinction ticket 08 bought five verdict states to
preserve. *And not empty frames either*: a blank value cell reads as a rendering fault.

#### L'exception géométrique — le seul état où la piste n'est pas un chemin de pointeur

When `L` itself is invalid there is **no shared x domain at all** — it is defined as span + 5 %
— so `x_D`, defined on `[0, L]`, has no interval to live in and the clamp has nothing to clamp
into. Both plots then render an empty frame with **no axis ticks** and one centred line « Portée
invalide — aucun tracé possible. », and **pointer input is inert**.

This is the **only** state in the app where the strip is not a pointer path. It is scoped to `L`
/ `h` / station failures, and **it has to be written down as an exception** or someone
implements the keep-the-scrub-track rule unconditionally and computes a scale over a NaN domain.

### Les trois refus

`no-convergence` has **two origins needing two surfaces**, because `resolveL0` shares the
engine's error union — hence three states, not the two a naive collapse gives (ELECTIONS §0.2).

| État | Cause | Copie |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **Tension impossible** | §5.4 chord bound ; toute cible de tension au-delà de la charge de rupture | « ⛔ Tension impossible — la ligne casserait avant d'atteindre cette tension. Longueur non tendue minimale pour ce câble : 197,40 m · valeur : 190,00 m. » |
| | mode Tension | « ⛔ Tension impossible — … Prétension maximale atteignable : 9 074 daN. » |
| | mode Flèche | « ⛔ Tension impossible — … Flèche minimale atteignable : 0,29 m. » |
| **Cible inatteignable** | échec d'encadrement de `resolveL0` pour toute raison autre que la rupture | « ⛔ Cible inatteignable — aucune longueur non tendue ne donne cette flèche à vide (6,00 m). » |
| **Calcul impossible** | `solveSpan` `no-convergence` **ou** `slack-limit` | « ⛔ Calcul impossible pour cette configuration. Le solveur n'a pas convergé — ce cas n'est pas censé se produire. Modifiez légèrement la tension ou la géométrie. » `[ Copier le diagnostic ]` |

`Indéterminé` is **not** one of these: it is a complete solve of an incomplete traverse, and it
stays a *verdict* state (voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et provenance, and § `Indéterminé` below).

#### « Tension impossible » — la borne §5.4 *est* la charge de rupture

Measured, on the §15-verified prototype solver, 12 mm IWRC 1770 (`w = 0,5465·9,81 N/m`, `EA =
6,90 MN`, `MBF = 90 740 N`), against `docs/simulation-theory.md` §5.4
(`docs/simulation-theory.md:254-266`, row « No convergence after retries — bad parameters (e.g.
`L₀ < chord/(1+T_break/EA)`) »):

| Portée | Plancher `L₀ = chord/(1 + MBF/EA)` | `T_max` au plancher | Flèche minimale atteignable |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 200 m (de niveau) | **197,404 m** | **100,0 % MBF** | 0,29 m (0,15 % de la portée) |
| 1000 m | **987,02 m** | **101,1 % MBF** | 7,21 m (0,72 % de la portée) |

Three consequences, all of them corrections to earlier tickets:

- `invalid-input{field:"L0"}` **never** means « nombre invalide ». Ticket 11's item 1 framing
  (« presumably prevented rather than reported ») is wrong for the one field where it matters:
  the generic-typo copy would be a lie about the one physics this app exists to report.
- Ticket 04 §7's line 264 — `H_target ≥ MBF` « is not an input error. It solves perfectly and
  the verdict says rupture » — is **struck**: any over-MBF target maps through `resolveL0` to a
  sub-floor `L₀` that the same pre-check refuses, so **no user-facing rupture verdict is
  reachable that way**.
- Ticket 04 §7's line 261 is **struck** too: the bound is violable in **all three** tightness
  modes, not only in direct-`L₀` mode.

**No Fix Action, and the reason is not restraint: no unambiguous repair exists, because the
bound *is* a rupture design.** Ticket 05 §6's rule — « defensible number ⇒ compute and let the
Verdict judge » — does not reach here: that rule governs *regulatory* refusals with a real
installation behind them (grips at `FS 8,0`), and here there is no installed state to compute.
You cannot tension a rope past its breaking load, so a rupture verdict would report a line that
cannot exist.

**The bound is quoted in the driving field's own quantity and unit, and never twice.** In
tension mode the copy states 9 074 daN and *not* « soit L₀ ≥ 197,40 m »: a second equivalent
invites the user to type a length into a tension field, the eyeball-confusion class the global
force-unit decision refused a `kN` carve-out to avoid.

That makes two **engine obligations**, free at a seam that already exists (voir § 2 — le contrat
du moteur):

1. `invalid-input` carries its violated bound — `{ kind: "invalid-input"; field: string; value:
   number; bound: number; boundKind: "min" | "max" }` (ELECTIONS §0.11). Without the bound,
   « Tension impossible »'s only actionable copy — « longueur non tendue minimale : 197,40 m » —
   is **unwritable**. Same shape of hole as the missing DAF field: the state was named, its
   payload could not say what the user needs.
2. `resolveL0` **re-reports the bound in the driving quantity**: it catches `solveSpan`'s
   `field: "L0"` floor and re-emits `field: "D_target" | "H_target"` with the bound mapped
   forward through the same inversion that failed.

#### « Calcul impossible » — aucun résidu à l'écran, aucun `SolveNote`, aucun panneau dev

Two engine kinds share **one** surface because a state earns its own presentation when it maps
to a *different user action*; these two map to the same non-action.

The evidence, and it is the reason the surface is this thin — measured across **349 440
instrumented solves** and **3 792 sweeps** (steel / PA / HMPE × 50–2000 m × `h` to ±0,99 L ×
`L₀` from 0,2× to 50× the floor × riders 0–1000 kg):

| Instrument | Mesure |
| --- | --- |
| `reseeded` | **0 fois** |
| §4.3 near-taut condition `w·L₀/H < 1e-4` | **0 fois** (ratio minimal observé **3,51e-4**, soit ≈3,5× plus de tension que le plancher §5.4 n'en admet) |
| `damped` (α halved) | 2 114 itérations, **0,6 %** |
| Couverture des sweeps | **1,000 dans tous les cas** |

So **no `SolveNote` ever reaches the UI and no dev panel ships.** *No residual on screen rather
than the rigger-legible « 3e-4 m, il en fallait 1e-6 »*: a residual read-out would document a
number **nobody has ever seen**, and §5.3's tolerance is itself L-scaled beyond 1000 m
(`docs/simulation-theory.md:243-252`), so it could not be interpreted without printing its own
tolerance beside it.

The diagnostic goes to the clipboard instead. Payload is **plain text, English keys, full float
precision, decimal point** — it is **evidence, not UI copy**, and rounding it to the display
grain would destroy the thing it exists to carry:

```
scoutech diagnostic — 2026-08-12T14:22:09.114Z — build <version>
kind        no-convergence
attempts    5
residual    3.4170e-4
tolerance   1e-6
notes       damped×12  reseeded×0  near-taut-fallback×0
[resolveL0] mode=sag  target=6
[solveSpan] L=150  h=-18  L0=151.6440231  w=5.361164  EA=6900000
            loads=[{x:75,P:1177.2}]
[material]  family=steel  d=12  R0=1770  cst=iwrc  MBF=90740  overrides=none
[address]   /?v=1&u=daN&use=perm&reg=en15567&L=150&h=-18&...
```

- `[address]` reads « (non partageable — tracé hors budget) » where the link would be refused,
  so the payload **never silently omits a field**.
- It contains **no terrain**, and that is not an omission: `solveSpan` is terrain-free and
  clearance is a reducer above it, so a solve failure is fully reproducible without the 200-row
  trace.

#### Obligation sur le plan de test

**All three engine kinds stay as guards, and the test plan must *inject* them** — a forced
iteration cap or a stub — **not hunt for them with pathological inputs**. Measured, they are
unreachable inside the admissible envelope, so no fixture finds them naturally. **The same
obligation covers `Indéterminé`.** Voir § 11 — plan de test.

### La table des champs responsables — statique, indexée par (grandeur × mode)

Invariant 1's mechanism. The message and its Fix Action anchor to the **first** culpable field
in table order; other culprits get the **error outline only**; the `Valeurs déduites` read-out
shows the offending value **marked**.

| Grandeur | Mode | Champs responsables (dans l'ordre) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `L` | manuel | Portée |
| `L` | importé | Station A, Station B |
| `h` | manuel | Dénivelé |
| `h` | importé | Station A, Station B, Hauteur d'ancrage A, Hauteur d'ancrage B |
| `L₀` | mode Longueur | Longueur non tendue |
| `L₀` | mode Flèche | Flèche à vide cible |
| `L₀` | mode Tension | Prétension cible |
| `MBF` | acier, non surchargé | Diamètre nominal, Grade, Construction |
| `MBF` | acier, surchargé | Charge de rupture *(le champ lui-même)* |
| `EA` | acier, non surchargé | Diamètre nominal |
| `EA` | acier, surchargé | Raideur axiale *(le champ lui-même)* |
| `EA` | textile | Raideur relative `k`, Charge de rupture |
| `μ`, `w` | acier, non surchargé | Diamètre nominal |
| `μ`, `w` | acier, surchargé | Masse linéique *(le champ lui-même)* |
| `μ`, `w` | textile | Masse linéique |

Two rules the material rows establish, which is why the Material Spec fields need no new
machinery:

- **An overridden derived value is its own culprit.** Override turns the read-out into an
  editable field, so attribution collapses to identity — the row exists only to *say* so, and to
  keep the two modes visibly parallel in review.
- **The steel rows follow §13.1's formulas, not the field group.** `MBF = K·d²·R₀/1000` depends
  on all three inputs, but `EA = E_app·C·d²` and `μ = W·d²/100` depend on **`d` alone**, because
  `E_app`, `C` and `W` are pinned midpoints and not construction-indexed. Listing Grade and
  Construction as culprits for `EA` would send a user to two fields that cannot move the number.

The table carries rows **only for quantities with a hard check**. `flèche à vide`, `H à vide`,
`tension permanente % MBL`, `corde` and `FS effectif` have none — they are solve outputs judged
by warnings and by the verdict, so they never need a culprit.

*A static table rather than a dependency graph*: the mapping is tiny, mode-indexed and has to be
reviewable beside the field table (voir § 3 — le modèle de paramètres). Exactly one tightness
quantity is state per mode, so the table has one row per mode **by construction**.

### Les sept échecs d'import — en ligne sous le contrôle de fichier

The seven blocking kinds render **inline under the file control** in `Paramètres › Géométrie`
(ELECTIONS §0.5c) with `[ Choisir un autre fichier ]`, and **never in the banner slot** —
nothing about the current line changed.

**A rejected file leaves the current terrain untouched**: manual geometry or a previously loaded
trace both survive. Destroying it would turn a rejected import into **data loss** — the
retain-and-restore rule read one step further, and invariant 5.

Every message is **prefixed with the file name** — the user may have picked the wrong file of
several — and `colonnes-manquantes` **names what it did read**:

| Kind | Copie |
| --- | --- |
| `colonnes-manquantes` | « profil-2.csv — colonnes Distance et Altitude introuvables (colonnes lues : D, Z). » |
| `aucune-donnée` | « profil-2.csv — le fichier ne contient aucune ligne de données. » |
| `trop-peu-de-points` | « profil-2.csv — au moins 2 points sont nécessaires. » |
| `valeur-non-numérique` | « profil-2.csv — valeur illisible ligne 47, colonne Altitude. » |
| `distance-non-croissante` | « profil-2.csv — la distance décroît à la ligne 112. » |
| `distance-dupliquée` | « profil-2.csv — deux altitudes à la même distance, ligne 88. » |
| `lecture-impossible` | « profil-2.csv — impossible de lire le fichier. » |

The non-blocking « ⚠ Distances peut-être en km plutôt qu'en mètres ? » belongs to the **terrain
provenance block** instead (voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et provenance), since that file *was*
accepted and the hint qualifies terrain now in use. The split across two surfaces is structural,
not cosmetic: **a blocking failure has no scenario to disclose.**

Recorded because it will otherwise read as dead code: **the km hint is reachable only for
hand-authored files.** The sub-20 m duplicate-`Distance` rule (`Distance` written to 0,1 m,
spacing `length/199`) rejects any geoportal trace short enough to trigger it as
`distance-dupliquée` first. It is kept for exactly that case, which is the denser-terrain escape
hatch (voir § 4 — terrain et import CSV).

**The typed-blocks / dragged-clamps asymmetry needs no machinery in the error model at all**:
clamping produces no error state, so the asymmetry lives entirely in the input handler. `x_D`'s
note is the one place a clamp speaks, and it speaks as a **note**, not an error.

#### Terrain grossier : ⚠ confirmé, et la fréquence est arithmétique, pas observée

Confirmed at **⚠**, inside the always-visible terrain block. *⚠ rather than the ambient tier*:
the ambient tier is defined by a specific property — it fires **always**, so an alarm would mean
nothing (textile `k`, on 100 % of zip lines by construction) — whereas this one is **conditional
on a design choice the user controls** (spacing `length/199` crosses 5 m at ≈995 m of span) and
it names a **blind spot in garde au sol**, the app's primary output, not a coefficient whose
uncertainty is already visible in another read-out. Demoting it would put the only disclosure of
an unseen-terrain risk in plain text beside a green verdict.

**State explicitly that the frequency claim is arithmetic, not observed.** All three repo traces
are ≤ 224 m (`docs/references/example-profile-trace{1,2,3}.csv` — 183,4 / 224,0 / 188,4 m, 200
rows each), so **nobody has ever seen a coarse file**: the ≈995 m threshold is division, not
field experience, and the spec must say so or the claim reads as measurement.

The counter-argument — a warning firing on half the stated range trains the blindness ambient
exists to avoid — is answered by the frequency being over the *range*, not over one user's work:
an operator building 200 m lines never sees it, and the one building 1500 m lines should see it
every time, **because it is true every time.**

### Les surfaces du lien

#### Le bandeau des clés ignorées

A dismissible **full-width strip above rail and charts** — *not the banner slot*, because it is
about the **link** and not the line, and it must never be able to hide a verdict. Shown once at
load, naming **each dropped key with its reason** and stating the rewrite: « ignoré » alone
cannot distinguish the sender's typo from a contradiction, so each key says **which** it was.

```
Une partie du lien a été ignorée : clé inconnue « xy » ; « za » n'était pas
un nombre (hauteur d'ancrage A ignorée).
Champs absents du lien, complétés depuis le scénario par défaut :
masse du passager 120 kg · enveloppe passager 2,1 m · coefficient dynamique 1,2.
L'adresse a été corrigée pour indiquer les valeurs utilisées.          [ ✕ ]
```

- The two salvageable kinds are **an unknown key** and **an unrepresentable value**. A
  contradictory *pair* of keys is not salvageable and refuses the link whole (below).
- The second sentence is the completion-from-defaults rule, and it **names the values, not just
  the fields**, so the recipient can read every number they got — which is the whole answer to
  the fourth-meaning-of-absence objection.
- **Manual dismissal only, no auto-hide, and dismissal lasts for the page load and nothing
  longer**, in component state. The strip is computed client-side on first mount from the **raw
  query string, before the rewrite `replace` fires**, so after the rewrite a reload of the
  now-clean URL produces no strip at all — there is nothing left for persistence to suppress,
  and a per-URL dismissal map would be Session surface nobody owns.
- **Mount order is settled and has no race** (voir § 8 — état, URL et coquille applicative): (1)
  the store's lazy initialiser reads the Session or the first-run defaults, (2) any recognised
  key wins wholesale, (3) the mount effect diffs raw keys and produces both the strip and the
  rewrite.
- **A successful Session restore announces nothing** — bare `/` *means* « restore the session »,
  and a notice for the defined normal case is noise.
- **The strip can only ever name keys the client received.** A validator that returns `{ key:
  undefined }`, or that runs under `search.strict`, makes the **server** 307 the offending key
  away before any client code runs — even under `ssr: false`. There is no other transport, so a
  malformed key of that shape is never reported at all; it is simply gone from the URL the
  client ever sees.
- Cost, from § Mise en page responsive: the strip spends **60 of the 124 px of resting rail at
  390 px**. That arithmetic *is* the justification for manual-dismissal-only — the strip is
  affordable exactly because it can be removed and because the Chart Block collapses on the
  first scroll.

#### Un lien à deux directeurs de tension refuse en entier

Two or more tightness drivers present ⇒ **refuse the link whole**, through the same
`errorComponent` path as an unknown `v`. This **amends** the URL schema's « `fl` et `te` tous
deux présents ⇒ les deux ignorés, bandeau » row.

Composed with completion-from-defaults, the elected drop-and-report does this: both drivers are
dropped, no driver key remains, completion supplies the **default Flèche 6,0 m** — and the
recipient reads a line whose *tension came from our defaults*, in the one quantity that scales
every tension in the criteria grid. This is not an override of the whole-or-nothing rule but an
**application** of it: individual keys are dropped « only when the remainder still describes a
complete line », and with no driver the remainder does not describe a line at all.

The rule generalises past the `fl`/`lo` pair: `fl`+`te`, `te`+`lo` and all three together are
the same fault, because the driver is encoded **by which key is present**, so two present keys
are not a bad value but **two contradictory models of the same line**. The same argument does
*not* reach the other salvage rows — an unknown key removes nothing the line needs.

Consequence for the strip copy: the `fl`/`lo` example is **gone from it**, because that case no
longer reaches the strip at all.

#### La page de refus — HTTP 200 après une frame de Skeleton

The refusal page is the **index** route's `errorComponent`, full-viewport inside the shell, and
it arrives at **HTTP 200 after a Skeleton frame** (measured: `ssr: false` returns 200 where a
thrown `validateSearch` under SSR renders the same refusal at **500**). Two consequences for the
copy:

- It **names the link, never the app or the network** — no « erreur de chargement », no
  « échec » : nothing failed to load and the status line agrees.
- It renders **in the frame the Skeleton occupied**, so the transition reads as the page
  *resolving* rather than as an error interrupting a load.

```
Ce lien ne peut pas être ouvert.
Version de schéma inconnue (v = 3).
                                        [ Ouvrir l'application sans ce lien ]
```

Reason lines, one per refusal cause:

| Cause | Ligne de motif |
| --- | --- |
| version de schéma inconnue | « version de schéma inconnue (v = 3) » |
| tracé indécodable | « tracé illisible » |
| tracé hors budget d'adresse | « tracé trop volumineux pour cette adresse » |
| deux directeurs de tension | « flèche, prétension et longueur non tendue : deux valeurs de tension dans le même lien » |

**No « ouvrir malgré tout ».** The whole-or-nothing rule forbids offering a partial read, and
`L`/`h` are derived from the terrain, so a trace-less application of an imported link would
**fabricate geometry the sender never entered**.

The action is labelled by its **effect** (invariant 2): *« Ouvrir l'application sans ce lien »
rather than « Ouvrir l'application vide », because the app is not empty* — it opens on the
first-run scenario, and promising emptiness would be the one wrong thing to say about the screen
that follows.

#### La ligne `<noscript>` du Skeleton

One line, worded here: **« Ce simulateur nécessite JavaScript pour fonctionner. »** With
JavaScript disabled the app is a Skeleton forever and otherwise says nothing.

### Le badge Override obsolète est dérivé au chargement

The URL schema has **no staleness key**, so a link from a sender whose override was stale
arrives as a plain override: the recipient would see a 12 mm breaking force on a 16 mm rope
**with no badge**, which is the worst thing to hide, since `MBF` drives the Verdict. So the
badge is **derived on every load and every link arrival, never carried** — which generalises
« invalid state can arrive » into « **stale** state can arrive » — and the comparison runs **at
the field's display precision, never on floats**, because a freshly created override holds
9074 daN against a formula value of 9073,98 and float equality would badge every override on
every load. Non-blocking. That is what makes the per-quantity display precision table a
**correctness** dependency rather than formatting: a missing row there is a badge that fires on
every load for every override. Mechanism, sketch and copy — including that « maintenant » is
dropped from the badge sentence, invariant 2 again: voir § 3 — le modèle de paramètres.

### `Indéterminé` — le dénominateur 260, les trous dans la piste, et le seul « — » légal

**Coverage renders as `résolus / grid.length`, and the denominator is 260, not 240.** Verified:
`sweepGrid(151.644, 240).length === 260` and `sweepGrid(1000, 240).length === 260` — **240
uniform steps plus 10 geometric tail steps per end, endpoints excluded**. So:

```
SWEEP_STEPS = 240 uniform + 10 tail steps per end  ⇒  260 pas
```

Ticket 08's « Couverture 240 / 240 pas » and « Indéterminé — 209 des 240 pas résolus » are wrong
by construction — a fully solved sweep would read 260/240. **Amended copy: « Couverture 260 /
260 pas » and « Indéterminé — 209 des 260 pas résolus ».** The performance guard rail is
re-expressed on **260 × 60**, not 240 × 60 (voir § 2 — le contrat du moteur, *Exécution :
synchrone et pure*).

Because the clearance curve **is** the scrub track and failed steps are a sparse `failures[]`,
under coverage < 1 the track has **gaps** and the rider can be parked in one:

- **Gaps are drawn as an explicit discontinuity** — the curve is **broken, never interpolated
  across**, with the gap's x-interval hatched in the axis gutter. *Hatching rather than a tint*,
  because tint is reserved for danger and a second tint meaning « inconnu » would compete with
  the one meaning « dangereux ».
- **Pointer input skips them**: a drag crossing a gap snaps to the nearest *solved* step, so
  **no pointer path can leave the rider in a hole**.
- A **typed `x` inside a gap gets the exact §6.1-case-2 solve** (~40 µs), which may succeed
  where the swept step failed — different conditioning, different seed.
- If that solve **also** fails, the five cells read **« — »**, and **this is the only place that
  glyph is legal**: *attempted and unavailable*, exactly what it was minted for.

**The « — » discipline, stated once because three tickets spent effort keeping it unambiguous:**

| Situation | Rendu |
| --- | --- |
| tenté, indisponible (`Indéterminé`, gap non résolu) | **« — »** |
| rien n'a été tenté (pas de régime choisi) | ligne **absente**, pas de tiret |
| bloqué (« Aucun résultat ») | une ligne de cause qui traverse la rangée |

A later « consistency » pass that dashes empty cells would undo all three decisions at once.

The no-regime case gets a **neutral strip: no colour, no icon**, deliberately **not** the
verdict's chrome — « Choisissez un régime de sécurité pour obtenir un verdict. » `[ Choisir un
régime ]` focusing the picker. *Neutral rather than coloured, because colouring the absence of a
judgement would be a judgement*, and three of the five verdict states are not-pass, so a
coloured band reading anything at all invites a wrong read of a screenshot. Row treatment is in
§ Sorties, verdict et provenance (« Résultats sans régime »).

The objection is real and answered rather than dismissed: a neutral strip is easy to ignore, and
the first run opens in exactly this state, so a user may never discover why no verdict appears.
**The button in the strip is the discovery path**, and it is a better one than a coloured
warning about a line that has nothing wrong with it.

### Le clamp de `x_D`

`x_D` has its own field-table row and **clamps on every path** — geometry change, link arrival,
typed input — because `x` selects a **view**, not a design input (voir § 3 — le modèle de
paramètres for the row, the note and the reason). What belongs to *this* contract is that the
clamp is **a note, not an error**: the typed-blocks / dragged-clamps asymmetry therefore needs
no machinery in the error model at all, and `x_D`'s note is the one place a clamp speaks. The
clamped value is what the debounced write puts in the URL.

### Fix Actions, lien de saut, et le repli en écran étroit

**Fix Actions are a general affordance of the error component**, not an ad-hoc addition per
error — four instances settle it: the §10.4 family gate, `Inverser A et B`, the regime gate, and
§10.4's second repair.

**Their labels describe the effect of pressing them** (invariant 2): « Passer à l'acier »,
« Passer en usage temporaire », « Inverser A et B », « Choisir un autre régime »,
« Recalculer ». *Verbs naming the effect rather than the audit's destination nouns (« Ligne en
acier »), because the rule that matters constrains **messages**, and a verb naming the effect is
accurate whether the state was entered or arrived.*

- **The §10.4 gate offers two Fix Actions**, `[ Passer à l'acier ]` first (the conservative
  branch) and `[ Passer en usage temporaire ]` second. For an arrived link **neither repair is
  privileged**, and withholding the second forces a recipient to **discard the Material Spec the
  link was sent to show them**.
- The regime gate's fix is `[ Choisir un autre régime ]` — a **focus** action, not a
  value-setting one, since **no single correct regime exists**.
- « Tension impossible » carries **no** Fix Action (above).

**One jump primitive serves all of them**: scroll the target's rail section into view,
**force-open it if collapsed**, then move DOM focus to the field or control, with
`scroll-margin-top` reserving the pinned header's height so the target never lands underneath
it.

- `[ Choisir un régime ]` focuses the picker **without opening it** — opening a popup from a
  remote button covers the context the user has just travelled to.
- **The banner's jump link is a `<button>`, not an `<a href="#…">`**: the URL is write-only
  after load, and a hash would put a fragment into an address bar the app rewrites.

**Narrow screens.** The error surface's placement is **layout-independent**, and two rules make
it so:

1. **A blocking error force-opens and pins its rail section**, and its Fix Action is reachable
   at **every** width — otherwise the app's only blocking states can be invisible while the
   chart draws, including on the first frame after following a link.
2. The pinned two-row safety header **carries the banner slot under the four-rank precedence**,
   so it may hold a blocking-error summary or « Saisie en cours » rather than only a verdict.

The two mechanisms compose with **no special case**: the jump link scrolls into the rail, which
is itself the Chart Block's collapse trigger, so pressing it produces exactly the layout the
message needs. Voir § 5 — visualisation et mise en page.

### Vocabulaire produit par ce contrat

Terms coined here and added to `CONTEXT.md`: **Blocking Error** · **Fix Action** · **Champ
responsable / Culpable Field** · **Solve / Clearance / Verdict Gate** · **Tension impossible** ·
**Cible inatteignable** · **Calcul impossible** · **Saisie en cours** · **Aucun résultat** ·
**Résultats sans régime**.


---

## § 9 — Le premier lancement — first-run scenario and shipped defaults

Provenance: [First-run scenario and defaults](./issues/14-first-run-scenario-and-defaults.md),
whose every figure was **measured, not estimated**, on ticket 07's §15-verified solver;
cross-ticket rulings from the final election round (three gates, three refusal states, banner
precedence, the `x_D` clamp, the 260 denominator).

This section is the **opening-screen record**: the shipped inputs, the state they compute to,
the two measured findings that make them non-obvious, and the regression lock that freezes them.
It is normative — an implementer builds these values, and a change to any of them breaks a test
on purpose.

### How every number in this section was produced

Not one figure here may be re-derived or "cleaned up". All statics come from the **§15-verified
prototype solver** (`src/routes/-prototype-07/solver.ts`, §4.1/§4.2/§5/§6.1/§6.2/§11, fixtures
F1 and Z1–Z5 asserted in `src/routes/-prototype-07/check.ts`), driven under `bun` from throwaway
scripts. That route is scheduled for deletion **after** its fixtures and bench are ported into
Vitest, and its solver must **not** be lifted into `src/lib/sim/` — voir § 10 — ordre
d'implémentation.

Fixed run parameters for every measurement below:

| Paramètre de mesure | Valeur |
| --- | --- |
| Sweep | `sweep(input, P, 240)` ⇒ `SWEEP_STEPS = 240 uniform + 10 tail steps per end` ⇒ **260 pas** |
| Clearance | `evaluateClearance(…, 60)` — 60 samples per step |
| Charge | `m_p` 120 kg ⇒ `P` = 1177,2 N · `h_rider` 2,1 m · `DAF` 1,2 |
| Terminaisons | `η_t` 0,90 at both ends |
| Terrain | ticket 04 §1's two-point profile: `xs = [0, L]`, `zs = [−za, h − zb]`, anchor-A-top frame |

Re-verified here: `coverage = 1` over **260 pas**, with the first and last step at `x` 0,005 and
149,995 m.

The steel chain was **recomputed from `docs/simulation-theory.md` §13.1, never transcribed**
(ledger #6's rule: never transcribe fixtures, regenerate them):

```
MBF = K · d² · R₀ / 1000   K = 0.356 (6×19, âme métallique)
                           ⇒ 0,356·144·1770/1000 = 90,74 kN = 9074 daN
EA  = E_app · C · d²       E_app 115 GPa, C 0,4165  ⇒ 6,897 MN  (affiché 6,90 MN)
μ   = W · d² / 100         W 0,3795                 ⇒ 0,5465 kg/m
w   = μ · g                g = 9,81                 ⇒ 5,361 N/m = 0,536 daN/m
```

`E_app` 115 GPa, `C` 0,4165 and `W` 0,3795 are pinned midpoints of §13.1's ranges — popover
prose, never fields (voir § 3 — le modèle de paramètres). Shipping this exact chain is
deliberate: it makes the popover's numbers reproducible from the first screen instead of
describing a rope nobody can see.

### `FIRST_RUN_SCENARIO` — the shipped inputs

One constant serves three consumers — the opening state, the reset target, and the completion
values for a partial link. **Not three copies.**

```ts
// src/lib/scenario/first-run.ts
export const FIRST_RUN_SCENARIO: Scenario;
export function firstRunDefaults(): Scenario;
```

| Champ (FR) | Identifier | Valeur de départ | Clé URL | Note |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Type d'installation | `use` | **`PERMANENTE`** | `use=perm` | branche restrictive (ticket 04) |
| Régime de sécurité | `regime` | **non défini** — « — choisir — » | *absente* | jamais défaulté par défaut |
| Portée | `L` | **150,0 m** | `L=150` | |
| Dénivelé | `h` | **−18,0 m** | `h=-18` | |
| Hauteur d'ancrage A | `z_A` | **12,0 m** | `za=12` | |
| Hauteur d'ancrage B | `z_B` | **12,0 m** | `zb=12` | |
| Tightness Spec — segment actif | — | **Flèche** | *implicite via `fl`* | voir plus bas |
| Flèche à vide cible | `D_target` | **6,0 m** | `fl=6.0` | seul champ éditable du segment |
| Famille | `family` | **Acier** | `fam=ac` | |
| Diamètre nominal | `d` | **12 mm** | `d=12` | |
| Grade | `R₀` | **1770** | `r0=1770` | |
| Construction | `construction` | **6×19 âme métallique** | `cst=iwrc` | ⇒ `K` = 0,356 |
| `MBF` / `EA` / `μ` acier | — | **aucune surcharge** | *absentes* | absence = non surchargée |
| Terminaison A | `η_A` | **Cosse à sertir / œil Flamand**, `η_t` **0,90** | `ta=cosse&na=0.9` | `[STD-ATTESTED]` |
| Terminaison B | `η_B` | **Cosse à sertir / œil Flamand**, `η_t` **0,90** | `tb=cosse&nb=0.9` | `[STD-ATTESTED]` |
| Masse passager | `m_p` | **120 kg** | `mp=120` | ⚠ « non confirmé » |
| Enveloppe passager | `h_rider` | **2,1 m** | `hr=2.1` | ⚠ « non confirmé » |
| Coefficient dynamique | `DAF` | **1,2** | `daf=1.2` | `[FIELD:SSF]` — popover, **pas de pastille** |
| Position du passager | `x_D` | **75,0 m** = `L/2` | `x=75.0` | clampé sur tous les chemins |
| Unité de force | `unit` | **daN** | `u=daN` | |
| Source de terrain | — | **géométrie manuelle** (aucun tracé) | `tr` absente | |
| Material Spec textile retenue | — | classe **HMPE**, `k` **22**, `MBL` et `μ` **vides et requis** | *absentes* | famille inactive |

Three of these are **not** this scenario's to choose and must not be re-litigated: `m_p` 120 kg
and `h_rider` 2,1 m are Unconfirmed Defaults *pre-filled precisely so they are
labelled-and-configurable rather than blank*, and `DAF` 1,2 is §8.2's `[FIELD:SSF]` measured
ceiling for a controlled step-off — the best-attested constant in the whole reference, so it
carries a provenance popover and **no « non confirmé » chip**. `m_p` is additionally a **Solve
Gate** input: with no rider mass there is no loaded `SpanState` at all, so 120 kg is what keeps
the opening screen solvable (voir § 7 — validation et états d'erreur, *Les trois grilles de
validation*).

`Régime de sécurité` at « — choisir — » is **not** an empty required field. It is struck from
the form tier, its absence is legitimate state, and the first screen is verdict-less by design.

### The computed opening state (measured)

| Grandeur | Valeur affichée |
| --- | --- |
| Corde A–B | 151,08 m (151,076) |
| Longueur non tendue `L₀` | **151,64 m** (151,644 — **2 décimales en état**, voir § 3 — le modèle de paramètres, *Précision d'affichage par grandeur*) |
| Prétension `H` à vide | **253,5 daN** |
| `T_A` / `T_B` à vide | 263,4 / 253,7 daN |
| Flèche à vide | **6,00 m** à `x` 74,8 m |
| Flèche / portée | **4,00 %** |
| Tension permanente | **2,90 % MBF** |
| `T_A` / `T_B` max statiques | **817,1 / 793,5 daN** |
| Enveloppe DAF `T_vide + DAF·(T_chargé − T_vide)` | **927,9 / 901,5 daN** |
| Flèche chargée max | **7,48 m** |
| **Garde min (traversée)** | **2,44 m à `x` 74,1 m** (passager à `x` 73,8), `contact: "none"`, 0 pas en violation |
| Couverture | **260 / 260 pas** |
| FS effectif | **8,80 (A) / 9,06 (B)** |

**No ticket-04 warning fires on this line**: 50 ≤ 150 ≤ 2000 · |h| ≪ L · hauteurs < 30 m ·
flèche/portée 4,00 % > 3 % · `d` 12 ∈ [6, 26]. That is a property of the chosen numbers, not
luck — a first screen that opens on its own warning would spend the app's warning vocabulary
before the user has typed anything.

### Finding 1 — dénivelé buys no clearance, so the geometry is forced by arithmetic

The first-run scenario is **not a taste question**. On manual terrain the app's central output
reduces to

```
garde ≈ lerp(hauteurs d'ancrage) − flèche chargée − enveloppe passager
```

because dénivelé tilts the chord *and* the ground plane by the same amount, so it cancels out of
the difference. Only the anchor heights and the loaded sag survive. Measured, at fixed `L`,
flèche and hauteurs:

| `h` (m) | 0 | **−18** | −40 | −60 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| garde min (m) | 2,46 | **2,44** | 2,37 | 2,25 |
| `L₀` (m) | 150,583 | **151,644** | 155,757 | 161,998 |

| flèche à vide cible (m) | 5 | **6** | 7 | 8 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| garde min (m) | 3,39 | **2,44** | 1,44 | 0,40 |
| flèche chargée (m) | 6,53 | **7,48** | 8,48 | 9,52 |

**44 metres of dénivelé move garde by 21 cm; one metre of flèche moves it by a metre.** That is
why 2,44 m is the figure worth shipping — near the practical minimum *and* visibly elastic, so
the first parameter a user touches moves the app's central output metre for metre. Equal 12 m
heights also put the minimum at `x` 74,1, mid-span, where the flèche explains it; asymmetric
hauteurs 12/10 shove it to `x` 84,2 and down to 1,39 m, because the minimum migrates toward the
low end as the ground plane tilts against the sag, and the user loses the visual explanation.

Two consequences leave this section:

- **Terrain-provenance copy must state the measured consequence** — on a straight ground line no
  metre of dénivelé buys clearance (2,46 → 2,25 m across `h` 0 → −60) — or a user modelling a
  gorge crossing manually reads a false failure (measured −62,8 m on a 1109 m line). Voir § 6 —
  sorties, verdict et provenance. - The precision table needs **`L₀` at 2 decimals**; the
  evidence is in the next subsection.

#### The recorded objection: 12 m platforms at both ends, and the arithmetic that forces them

Record this or somebody "fixes" it. 12 m platforms at *both* ends read as an adventure-park
tower-to-tower line, whereas the repo's real traces are gorge crossings (trace1 dips 66,3 m
below its own ground chord). The intuitively realistic pair is A 12 m on a platform, B 6 m at
the arrival — and it does not work:

| Scénario | garde min | contact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 150 / −18, hauteurs 12/6, flèche 6,0 | **−1,04 m** | `rider` |
| 150 / −18, hauteurs 10/4, flèche 6,0 | **−3,04 m** | `cable` |
| 200 / −25, hauteurs 12/6, flèche 6,0 | **−1,53 m** | `rider` |
| 200 / −25, hauteurs 12/6, flèche 8,0 | **−3,33 m** | `cable` |
| 180 / −20, hauteurs 10/4, flèche 6,0 | **−3,32 m** | `cable` |

**Every intuitively realistic anchor pair opens the app in « Câble au sol »** — the one opening
state forbidden outright. The cause is not the solver: manual terrain **is** a plane, so a plane
is what the defaults must suit. A gorge-shaped scenario needs a Trace, and no Trace ships. The
honest reading of the shipped hauteurs is *« 12 m of clear air beneath each anchor »*, not
*« build two towers »*, and the terrain-provenance line is where that gets said.

Rejections recorded, one line each:

- **12,0 / 12,0 m rather than the realistic 12 / 6 m**, because the realistic pair measures
  garde −1,04 m and opens on « Câble au sol ». - **`h` −18,0 m rather than a steeper descent
  that "looks like a ride"**, because the clearance cost of going further is 21 cm — i.e.
  nothing — and −18 m (12 % of span) sits in the family of the real traces (183,4 / −2,7 · 224,0
  / −38,6 · 188,4 / −5,1 m) instead of buying margin it cannot buy. - **A clearance near the
  practical minimum rather than a comfortable one**, because a garde that is comfortably huge
  (or already violated) wastes the app's central read-out on first contact.

### Finding 2 — Flèche is the active Tightness segment because its value is exact

`Tension de la ligne` opens on **Flèche**. « Flèche à vide cible » **6,0 m** is the one editable
field; « Prétension » 253,5 daN and « Longueur non tendue » 151,64 m appear as `Valeurs
déduites` read-outs.

Two arguments, one of them measured. Sag is the quantity a rigger can verify on site with a
tape, and it is the same quantity promoted to a **validity** read-out (flèche/portée gates the
parabola rule and fires §8.2's stiffening warning) — so the driving input and the warning that
governs it are the *same number* on the first screen. And it is **the only mode whose shipped
value is exact at display precision**. A prefill writes the *displayed* value into state, never
the underlying float, so a direct-`L₀` default would have to ship a rounded 151,64 m or 151,6 m:

| `L₀` en état (m) | flèche à vide | `H` à vide | garde min |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 151,6 | 5,79 m | 262,6 daN | 2,64 m |
| 151,64 | 5,98 m | 254,4 daN | 2,46 m |
| **151,644** | **6,00 m** | **253,6 daN** | **2,44 m** |

One decimal costs **0,21 m of flèche, 3,5 % of `H` and 0,20 m of garde — 8 % of the shipped
margin**; two decimals cost 0,02 m. Shipping `Flèche` sidesteps it entirely: 6,0 m is exact, and
`L₀` stays a read-out where rounding is cosmetic rather than state. This is also the measurement
that promotes the display-precision table from « mechanically derivable » to a **sensitivity
decision** — voir § 3 — le modèle de paramètres, *Précision d'affichage par grandeur*.

- **Rejected: `Tension` as the shipped segment**, although it is the professional framing (a
  tensioner reads force, and the only instrumented field data in the reference is quoted as
  pretension, `[FIELD:SSF]` ≤ 220/145 daN), because a force on first paint (253 daN) is
  meaningless until the user knows the cable — the driver would read as an arbitrary constant
  instead of a measurable geometric target, and the flèche/portée validity read-out would become
  a consequence the user cannot steer. - **Rejected: `Longueur`**, because it ships the rounding
  problem above and practitioners rarely think in unstretched length.

### The shipped Material Spec — steel complete, textile blank on purpose

Active family **Acier**, `d` 12 mm / `R₀` 1770 / 6×19 âme métallique ⇒ MBF 9074 daN, `EA`
6,90 MN, `μ` 0,5465 kg/m, **no override on any derived value** — absence means « non
surchargée », and an override on first run would ship the one `[STD-ATTESTED]` derived value
**pre-downgraded** into the provenance aggregate.

Terminaison A **and** B: **« Cosse à sertir / œil Flamand », `η_t` 0,90** — `[STD-ATTESTED]`
(§6's tags govern over §5's chip mapping, which wrongly lumped it with the conventioned rows),
the commonest real zip-line termination, and the conservative choice against the 1,00 socket.
Both ends identical **deliberately**: `T_A > T_B` here, so **FS_A 8,80 governs**, and the
shipped scenario demonstrates the higher-anchor rule without needing asymmetric hardware to do
it.

Retained **inactive** textile spec: classe **HMPE**, `k` **22** (`[DATASHEET]`, Samson 872,
arithmetic verified), and **`Charge de rupture` (`MBL`) and `Masse linéique` (`μ`) blank and
required**. **No rope ships.**

- **Blank-and-required rather than an illustrative MBL**, because — measured — that one invented
  number *decides the verdict*: MBL 100 kN ⇒ FS 11,94 « dans les limites »; 60 kN ⇒ 7,63 « hors
  limites » **on the same line**. Every product row in both research runs is compromised and the
  zero-shipped-presets rule is explicit, so the blank field is the honest statement that nobody
  published the number — the same treatment already chosen for polyester's `k`. - **The
  consequence is specified, not papered over.** Flipping to `Corde textile` under `TEMPORAIRE`
  lands on two blank required fields. That is **not** « Saisie en cours »: a field the user
  never filled is not mid-edit, so it raises blocking errors attributed through the culprit
  table, and **no stale picture is kept** (voir § 7 — validation et états d'erreur).

### The shipped line is allowed to read « Hors limites » under ACCT 10:1

FS effectif **8,80** against `k` = 10. **Do not up-size the cable and do not slacken the line to
buy a green under the strictest entry.**

| Régime | `k` | `T adm` (ancrage A) | FS effectif | État |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| EN 15567-1 | 3,0 | 2722 daN | 8,80 | Dans les limites |
| WorkSafe QLD — tension totale | 3,0 | 2722 daN | 8,80 | Dans les limites |
| WorkSafe QLD — charge suspendue | 5,0 | — (une seule cellule) | **12,29** | Dans les limites |
| ANSI/ACCT 5:1 | 5,0 | 1633 daN | 8,80 | Dans les limites |
| ACCT 10:1 `[CONVENTION]` | 10,0 | 817 daN | 8,80 | **Hors limites** (réserve −111 daN) |
| Secours textile 10:1 | 10,0 | — | — | indisponible (acier + `PERMANENTE`) |

The suspended-load figure uses the only denominator that makes that criterion bindable —
`min(η_A, η_B)·MBF / [DAF·(T_chargé − T_vide)]` = 0,9 · 9074 / 664,5 = **12,29** — against 77,
which can never bind, if read as `MBF/P`. So **total tension governs on the shipped line** at
8,80. The two `η` values coincide here (0,90 / 0,90) and diverge only on a mixed-termination
line, which is exactly what the `min` exists for; the criterion is **one cell, not per end**
(voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et provenance, *La grille critères × extrémités*).

Three reasons the red entry ships as-is:

1. The app opens **verdict-less**, so nothing fails on first contact — the state appears only
   after a deliberate pick of the `[CONVENTION]` entry that sits adjacent to its contradictory
   `[UNVERIFIED:R1]` 5:1 twin. 2. Choosing hardware so that a `[CONVENTION]` passes turns the
   shipped Material Spec into an **implicit safety recommendation**, which « regulatory — not
   tunable » and the zero-preset rule both refuse. 3. **The spread is the teaching**: one
   unchanged line reads « dans les limites » at `k` = 3 and « hors limites » at `k` = 10.

**Measured cost of the alternative, which is why it is not close:** `d` 14 mm gives FS 10,79 —
still **« Marge faible »** at `FS_eff < 1,1·k` — so up-sizing one step **does not even work**.
Green everywhere needs `d` ≥ 16 mm (12,71) or flèche ≥ 5 % of span, i.e. the first screen would
teach an oversized cable or a slack line as normal practice, and 16 mm would contradict the
worked steel chain the popover prose pins.

### The opening screen, region by region

The Rail is **one scrolling column with five sections** plus the Rail Header. This is the record
of what each holds on first paint.

```
┌ En-tête du rail ────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Scoutech    [daN ▾]  [ Copier le lien ]                    [⋯]  │
├ PARAMÈTRES ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Type d'installation      PERMANENTE                             │
│ Régime de sécurité       — choisir —                            │
│ Portée                   150,0  m                               │
│ Dénivelé                 −18,0  m                               │
│ Hauteur d'ancrage A       12,0  m                               │
│ Hauteur d'ancrage B       12,0  m                               │
│ [ Importer un profil… ]   (aucun tracé)                         │
│ Famille                  Acier                                  │
│ Diamètre nominal          12    mm      Grade  1770             │
│ Construction             6×19 âme métallique                    │
│ Terminaison A            Cosse à sertir / œil Flamand  (η 0,90) │
│ Terminaison B            Cosse à sertir / œil Flamand  (η 0,90) │
│ [Flèche|Tension|Longueur]  Flèche à vide cible   6,0  m         │
│ Masse passager           120    kg    ⚠ non confirmé            │
│ Enveloppe passager         2,1  m     ⚠ non confirmé            │
│ Coefficient dynamique      1,2                                  │
│ Position du passager      75,0  m                               │
├ VALEURS DÉDUITES ───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Charge de rupture MBF    9074 daN                               │
│ Rigidité EA              6,90 MN            ⚠ non confirmé      │
│ Masse linéique μ         0,5465 kg/m        ⚠ non confirmé      │
│ Poids linéique w         0,536 daN/m                            │
│ Corde                    151,08 m                               │
│ Longueur non tendue L₀   151,64 m                               │
│ Prétension H à vide      253,5 daN                              │
│ Flèche à vide            6,00 m   (à x 74,8 m)                   │
│ Flèche / portée          4,00 %                                 │
│ Tension permanente       2,90 % MBF                             │
├ RÉSULTATS ── sur toute la traversée ── aucun régime choisi ──────┤
│ (le jeu de lignes réduit : voir « Résultats sans régime »)       │
├ PROVENANCE ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ℹ Terrain — ligne droite entre les pieds d'ancrage              │
│   (aucun profil importé)                                        │
│ ⚠ 4 valeurs sans référence normative confirmée           [ ▾ ]  │
├ CE QUE CET OUTIL NE CALCULE PAS ────────────────────────────────┤
│ (les cinq lignes statiques, inchangées)                          │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**The aggregate count is 4** — `m_p`, `h_rider`, `EA` acier, `μ` acier — with **no regime line
pinned yet**, since the regime is pinned first only once one is chosen. `EA` gets a chip because
`E_app` and `C` are `[CONVENTION]` ranges; `μ` because `W` is a range whose midpoint we chose;
`MBF` gets **none** (`[STD-ATTESTED]` `K` factors, unoverridden); `DAF` gets **none**
(well-attested *and* editable).

**Chart Block on first paint.** Cable drawn; « corde A–B » **on** and « ligne à vide » **off**;
`x` domain **[−3,75 ; 153,75] m** (span + 5 %); `z` extent 30 m (anchor A top at 0 down to base
B at −30) with 12 % margins ⇒ 37,2 m over 157,5 m, i.e. **auto-fit VE ≈ ×1,5** measured at a
1080×420 px plot box — inside the **Plot Aspect Band [0.35, 0.50]** and clear of the **×1
floor**, so the badge reads a plain exaggeration rather than the « vraie échelle » clamp state
(voir § 5 — visualisation et mise en page, *Exagération verticale*). No danger band, no
violation marker, and the **handle lane is reserved but empty** in manual mode.

**Read-out strip at `x` = 75,0 m** (the rider snaps to sweep step `x` 75,06): garde **2,49 m**
(à `x` 74,1) · `T_A` **817 daN** · `T_B` **794 daN** · pente locale **−11,1 %** · flèche chargée
**7,48 m**.

**The rider ships at `x` = `L/2` = 75,0 m**, and that pays a dividend. The traverse minimum sits
at rider `x` 73,8 — **0,9 m away**, inside the `max(1 m, 1 % de la portée)` = 1,5 m rule — so
the clearance cell reads plainly as « Garde min » on first paint instead of relabelling itself.
And the cell says **2,49 m** while the Rail says **2,44 m**: the first screen *is* the worked
example of Position scope versus Traverse scope, a 5 cm gap that reads as a rounding bug unless
both scope labels are present. They are — « à la position du passager » on the read-out row and
« sur toute la traversée » on the `Résultats` header — and that label is what keeps this true
when the two blocks become adjacent at 390 px.

- **Rejected: parking the rider on the traverse minimum** so the two numbers agree. That is a
  curated first screen hiding the very distinction the app spent belt-and-braces redundancy on;
  `L/2` is the only neutral choice, and mid-span is `x_D`'s fixed initial value on every path
  anyway.

**The address bar stays bare.** A bare `/` means « restore the Session », so it is never
rewritten on load; the defaults become a URL only through « Copier le lien »:

```
/?v=1&u=daN&use=perm&L=150&h=-18&za=12&zb=12&fl=6.0&fam=ac&d=12&r0=1770
 &cst=iwrc&ta=cosse&tb=cosse&na=0.9&nb=0.9&mp=120&hr=2.1&daf=1.2&x=75.0
```

**141 characters, measured** — `reg` absent (« — choisir — ») and `mbf`/`ea`/`mu` absent (non
surchargées), the two absence meanings the schema protects. This **discharges** the
≈200-character estimate for the readable keys; keep it as a re-check in the real build, not as a
risk. Keys are emitted in the schema's key-table order, so two links completed from the same
partial input are string-identical and « Copier le lien » stays diffable.

**The defaults are never server-painted.** The Session is read synchronously in the store's lazy
initialiser (`readSession() ?? firstRunDefaults()`) at the client-only subtree's first render,
so a returning user never sees this scenario flash before their own, and the Skeleton carries no
field values at all.

### « Résultats sans régime » — the reduced row set

The first screen opens **verdict-less** and that is confirmed, not merely inherited: **the two
numbers the app exists to produce are both live without a regime.** Garde min 2,44 m is
geometric fact — no `k` appears anywhere in §11 — and the tension envelope is physics. All a
regime adds is a *divisor*, and « régime choisi » is struck from the form tier precisely so that
divisor cannot gate the engine. A default regime would pick the user's safety framework (three
of five regimes are `[CONVENTION]`/`[UNVERIFIED:R1]`); a blocked solve would ship an empty chart
and destroy manual-mode ground's own reason for existing.

> **Résultats sans régime** — the state of the Rail's output half while `Régime de sécurité` is
> « — choisir — ». Every rider-free and Traverse-scope value is **present and complete**; every
> `k`-dependent value is **absent — not dashed, not blank, not zero**; the banner slot carries
> the neutral prompt.

| Ligne de `Résultats` | Sans régime |
| --- | --- |
| Verdict (bandeau) | **absent** → prompt neutre |
| FS effectif | **absent** |
| Réserve de tension | **absent** |
| `T adm` ancrage A | **absent** |
| `T adm` ancrage B | **absent** |
| grille critères × extrémités | **absent** (Verdict Gate suppresses banner and grid together) |
| `T max` (traversée) | **928 daN — ancrage A, passager à x 74 m** (enveloppe majorée) |
| Garde min (traversée) | **2,44 m — passager à x 74 m** |
| `T max` ancrage A / B | présents |
| `T max` statique · majoration dynamique | présents |
| Flèche chargée max | 7,48 m |
| Couverture | 260 / 260 pas |
| Provenance · Ce que cet outil ne calcule pas | présents, complets |

**Five things absent, not four**: the criteria × ends grid goes with the rows, because one gate
suppresses both. In place of the four rows, one plain-text line — no second button, because `[
Choisir un régime ]` already lives in the banner and two identical buttons 200 px apart is the
warning wall the parameter model refused:

> « FS effectif, réserve de tension et T admissible aux ancrages A et B dépendent du régime —
> choisissez un **régime de sécurité**. »   (the emphasised phrase focuses the picker)

The block header gains **« aucun régime choisi »** beside « sur toute la traversée », so the
scope label and the reduction are read in one glance.

- **Absent rather than dashed, and this overrides the general dash treatment.** « — » is spent
  on `Indéterminé` — *attempted, unavailable* — and reusing it for *nothing attempted* collides
  with the distinction five verdict states were bought to preserve. A first screen full of
  dashes on a scenario that solved perfectly (couverture 260/260, `contact: "none"`, 0 pas en
  violation) reads as a failed computation: the exact misread `Indéterminé` exists to prevent,
  firing on a healthy line. Three tickets have now spent effort keeping that one glyph
  unambiguous; a later "consistency" pass that dashes empty cells would undo all three. -
  **Rejected: printing a bare `FS effectif 8,80` with no requirement beside it.** Worse than
  hiding it — §10 forbids blending, no free `k` exists anywhere in the app, and a lone safety
  factor invites the reader to supply their own. - **Accepted cost: the block grows when a
  regime is picked.** That is honest feedback on a deliberate act, and it grows in the output
  half, not under the user's cursor.

#### The headline `T max` is the **amplified** envelope, not the static one

Corrected here against this scenario's own round-1 wording, which read « T max 817 daN ».
817,1 daN is the **static** envelope; the headline `T max` is the **DAF-amplified** one, because
it is the quantity `FS effectif` divides by:

```
FS effectif 8,80 = η_A · MBF / T_DAF,A = 0,9 · 9074 / 927,9
T_DAF = T_vide + DAF · (T_chargé − T_vide)      ← needs T_vide alongside the loaded sweep
```

Settled: the headline reads **« T max 928 daN — ancrage A, passager à x 74 m »**, with detail
rows **« T max statique 817 daN »** and **« ↳ majoration dynamique +111 daN (DAF 1,2) »**.
`T_vide` is already produced by `solveSpan`, so this is a **reporting** requirement, not an
engine change.

The failure avoided is a *reconciliation* failure and it is invisible in exactly the state that
ships: with no regime chosen `FS effectif` is absent, so a headline of 817 beside a hidden 8,80
contradicts nothing — and then contradicts itself the moment the user picks a regime, when a
reader who divides 0,9 · 9074 / 817 gets **10,00** against a printed 8,80. It also *strengthens*
the scope finding: the strip's Position-scope `T_A` cell reads **817 daN** at `x` 75 while the
Rail's traverse headline reads **928 daN**, so the two scopes are visibly **different numbers**
on the first frame instead of the same number printed twice. The strip stays static because DAF
is a verdict-side amplification that never touches a drawn or instantaneous quantity.

### No worked example ships — no named scenario, no bundled trace, no « charger un exemple »

**The first-run scenario *is* the worked example.** The three CSVs stay in `docs/references/` as
fixtures.

**The SSF Vercors 1109 m line cannot ship, and the reason is measured twice over.** Ledger #7
rules R1's Vercors numeric table fabricated — only the 1109 m span, ≤ 220/145 daN and ~33 m/s
are attested — so dénivelé, MBL, `μ`, `k`, hauteurs and terrain would all be **ours**. And the
invented half decides the answer:

| Variante inventée (L 1109 m, HMPE illustratif) | flèche à vide | garde min | FS effectif | Verdict (secours 10:1) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MBL 100 kN, `μ` 0,075, H cible 220 daN | 52,0 m | **−62,8 m** `cable` | 11,94 | dans les limites |
| MBL 60 kN | 51,9 m | −67,2 m | **7,63** | **hors limites** |
| `k` 31 | 52,0 m | −60,5 m | 11,53 | dans les limites |
| H cible 145 daN (l'autre chiffre SSF) | 79,2 m | −89,2 m | 16,13 | dans les limites |
| `h` −60 au lieu de −150 | 51,6 m | −62,2 m | 12,08 | dans les limites |

Two failures at once: **the verdict flips on a number nobody published**, and **every variant
opens in « Câble au sol » on straight ground** at any plausible anchor height — because what
makes such a line safe is a gorge, i.e. a Trace nobody has. Shipping that under the reference's
highest-trust tag is the fabricated-precision failure the provenance rule exists to prevent.
