**Rejected: bundling a real trace as a demo**, the closer call because the files exist. Measured
at the shipped tightness (flèche à vide 6,0 m, hauteurs 12/12) — and note there are **three**
example traces, not two, contrary to three tickets' prose:

| Fichier | points | `L` | `h` | pas max | garde min |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `example-profile-trace1.csv` | 200 | 183,4 m | −2,7 m | 1,00 m | 6,83 m à x 21,0 |
| `example-profile-trace2.csv` | 200 | 224,0 m | −38,6 m | 1,20 m | 9,90 m à **x 224,0** |
| `example-profile-trace3.csv` | 200 | 188,4 m | −5,1 m | 1,00 m | 8,80 m à x 6,0 |

All three show the **uninteresting** clearance case — a gorge gives metres of air, and on trace2
the minimum sits *at the arrival anchor*, i.e. it is merely `hauteur B − enveloppe` and teaches
nothing about sag. A demo file would also buy a third state in the "which file" identity line
(« tracé d'exemple » beside « tracé reçu par lien » and a real filename) and hand some users an
unattested Swiss profile they will treat as a reference line.

### « Réinitialiser le scénario » versus « Effacer la session »

Reset returns the **active configuration** to the first-run defaults, terrain source included:

| Revient aux valeurs de départ | Survit intact |
| --- | --- |
| `L` 150,0 · `h` −18,0 · hauteurs 12,0 / 12,0 | la Material Spec inactive conservée |
| Flèche à vide cible 6,0 m, segment `Flèche` actif | le **tracé, mis en cache** dans la Session |
| Spec acier 12 / 1770 / 6×19, aucune surcharge | l'unité de force et les bascules de calques |
| Terminaisons cosse / cosse · passager 120 kg / 2,1 m · DAF 1,2 | |
| `x` → `L/2` = 75,0 m · régime → « — choisir — » | |
| **la source de terrain** → géométrie manuelle | |

It removes the Trace **as terrain source** through the existing remove path — which restores the
retained manual `L`/`h` — while **keeping the profile cached**, and navigates to bare `/` with
`replace: true`.

- **Rejected: letting the Trace survive as terrain.** With a trace loaded `L` and `h` are
  *derived read-outs*, so the post-reset state would not be the first-run scenario at all —
  **Portée would read the trace's 183,4 m** after a control that promised a return to the
  shipped state. The terrain source *is* part of the active configuration. - **Keeping it cached
  forces a re-add path, or the cache is dead weight.** With nothing able to read the cached
  profile the reset would be destructive in effect. So the file-identity line in `Paramètres ›
  Géométrie` gains a **fourth state** — « Tracé en mémoire : *pont-du-diable.csv* (non
  utilisé) » with **`[ Réutiliser ce tracé ]`**, which re-places A at the first station and B at
  the last exactly as an import does. For a link-received trace the line reads « tracé reçu par
  lien (non utilisé) », since the filename does not travel. - **No confirmation dialog on
  reset**, and that is a decision. Nothing is destroyed — Trace, retained spec and display
  preferences all survive — and a confirmation on a non-destructive action **trains the user to
  click through confirmations**, which is what would make the one dialog guarding real data loss
  worthless. The control already costs two clicks (Rail-header overflow menu) and the menu item
  states its own consequence:

  > **Réinitialiser le scénario**
  > Revient aux valeurs de départ. Le tracé importé est retiré du terrain mais reste en mémoire.

- **Rejected: `push` instead of `replace`, to make Back an undo of a reset.** Tempting, since
  reset keeps everything the URL does not carry — but only *nearly* true: writes are debounced
  ≈400 ms and suspended under « Saisie en cours », so the previous entry is sometimes not the
  pre-reset state, and an undo that is right 99 % of the time contradicts the flat rule that
  **Back is not undo**. Accepted cost, recorded: a reset discards typed numbers with no undo;
  the mitigation is « Copier le lien », which exists.

**« Effacer la session » stays the only destructive control**: it deletes `scoutech:session:v1`
entirely — cached Trace, filename, retained inactive spec, display preferences — **behind a
confirmation**, with an extra warning when the Trace arrived by link (« Ce tracé est arrivé par
lien : il ne pourra pas être réimporté depuis un fichier. »). Keep the two controls distinct:
collapsing them either leaves an imported trace standing behind a word that promised a reset, or
deletes user data behind a word that promised only numbers.

### A recognised link that omits keys is completed from these defaults — literally

`?L=250&h=-40` loads. Missing fields are filled from `FIRST_RUN_SCENARIO`, the address bar is
rewritten to the **completed** Scenario (`navigate({ to: '/', search: <plain object>, replace:
true })` from the mount effect, which fires here precisely because something was completed), and
the ignored-keys strip gains a **second sentence** — same component, same manual dismissal,
shown whenever *either* sentence has content:

```
Clés ignorées : « junk » (clé inconnue).
Valeurs absentes complétées avec les valeurs de départ : hauteur d'ancrage A
12,0 m · hauteur d'ancrage B 12,0 m · flèche à vide cible 6,0 m · diamètre
12 mm · … · position du passager 125,0 m. L'adresse a été réécrite avec le
scénario complet.
```

One strip and not two, because both sentences answer the same question — « what did this link
actually give me? » — and the banner slot holds exactly one occupant. Each sentence names
**field and value**, which is what defeats the scenario-nobody-saw hazard: the recipient can
read every value they got.

**The completion set is conditional, and stating it is the whole implementation.** Keys whose
absence already carries meaning are exempt and keep it:

| Absence | Sens conservé |
| --- | --- |
| `reg` | « — choisir — » |
| `mbf` `ea` `mu` (acier) | non surchargée |
| `tr` `sa` `sb` | source de terrain manuelle — et si `tr` **est** présent, `L`/`h` ne sont **pas** complétés, ils sont dérivés |
| clés de la famille inactive | famille inactive |

Everything else completes. Two consequences worth writing down:

- **`x` completes to `L/2` of the *completed* span**, not to the constant 75,0 — 125,0 m for
  `?L=250&h=-40` — and then clamps, with the transient inline note. - **`fam=tx` cannot be
  completed into a solvable line**, because two of its required keys have no shipped default.
  Such a link arrives at « Aucun résultat » with two blocking errors, which is correct.

**The literal rule beats the clever one, and the measurement is decisive.** `fl` completes to
the absolute **6,0 m**, never to 4 % of the arriving span:

| `?L=250&h=-40` complété par | flèche/portée | garde min | FS eff | résultat |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fl=6.0` (littéral) | 2,40 % ⚠ | **1,25 m**, `contact: none` | 5,41 | solvable, avertissement §8.2 honnête |
| `fl=10.0` (4 % de la portée) | 4,00 % | **−2,39 m**, `contact: cable` | 7,49 | **« Câble au sol »** |

The "smart" completion puts a hand-authored link **on the ground**; the literal one lands on a
clear line with a ⚠ that is simply true — a 250 m span at 6 m of sag really is stiff, and firing
an honest warning beats inventing a sag the sender never wrote. Literal also keeps « les valeurs
de départ » meaning **one** thing in both places the phrase is used, here and in reset.

- **Rejected: refusing partial links.** Hand-authored `?L=250&h=-40` was deliberately kept
  working, which is only coherent if the rest comes from these defaults; refusing would make the
  readable-keys schema pay its cost and buy nothing. The fourth-meaning-of-absence objection is
  answered by *stating* the completion in both the address bar and the strip.

Refusals are unchanged: unknown `v`, undecodable or oversize `tr`, `fl` and `lo` both present
(voir § 8 — état, URL et coquille applicative).

### The opening screen is frozen as a Vitest fixture, asserted at display precision

A defaults change, a precision-table change or a solver regression must not be able to move the
first screen silently.

| Élément | Décision |
| --- | --- |
| Constante | **`FIRST_RUN_SCENARIO`** in `src/lib/scenario/first-run.ts` — one object serving the opening state, the reset target and the completion values |
| Test | **`src/lib/scenario/first-run.test.ts`**, colocated per `docs/conventions/testing.md` |
| Nature | **regression lock, not a physics fixture** — §15's `F1`/`Z1`–`Z6` in `src/lib/sim/__fixtures__/theory-15.ts` keep that job and keep their `1e-3` tolerance |

**Assert the formatted strings, not the floats.** `1e-3` relative on `L₀` is ±0,15 m, and 0,15 m
of `L₀` moves garde by ≈0,15 m — a tolerance that would let the opening screen drift by **6 % of
its safety margin and stay green**. What is being frozen is what the user reads, so the assertion
is the rendered string.

The eleven frozen strings, and the three structural assertions no string can carry (`contact ===
"none"`, `coverage === 1` over `grid.length === 260`, and zero steps in violation), are listed
once in « voir § 11 — plan de test ».

Side effect worth having: the same test locks the **French decimal comma** and the per-quantity
display-precision table, since both live inside the rendered string.


---

## § 10 — Implementation order

Provenance: `docs/simulation-theory.md` §17's staged roadmap (the physics order, not
negotiable), [ticket 03](./issues/03-engine-api-execution-model-and-module-layout.md) (module
layout and the fixture surface), [ticket 07](./issues/07-profile-visualisation-prototype.md) §12
(the prototype is a primary source, not a head start), [ticket
12](./issues/12-sweep-performance-and-worker-decision.md) (the bench is a tripwire and must
survive the prototype), [ticket 15](./issues/15-responsive-and-narrow-screen-layout.md) §5
(three pixel budgets to re-check before deletion) and the final election round (injected-only
failure states).

Two things in this order are owned by nobody else and are the reason it is written down: **the
bench and the §15 fixtures are ported into the real build before the prototype is deleted**, and
**`src/routes/-prototype-07/solver.ts` is never lifted into `src/lib/sim/`**. Everything else is
dependency arithmetic.

The rule that governs every engine stage: **the fixture is written first and fails, then the
module makes it pass.** §15 is the TDD seed set, one group of it per stage, and no stage is
finished with a red case from an earlier one.

### The order at a glance

| # | Stage | Delivers | Gate to pass |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **P0.1** | Test harness | `vitest` env config, `bun run bench`, `docs/conventions/testing.md` amended | `bun run test` runs, 0 tests |
| **P0.2** | `__fixtures__/theory-15.ts` | `F1`, `Z1`–`Z6` transcribed from §15, each with `source` | all engine cases red |
| **E1** | `types.ts` | the frozen declarations — « voir § 2 — le contrat du moteur » | `bun run typecheck` |
| **E2** | `catenary.ts` | §4.1 `x(s)`/`z(s)`, `sampleCable`, `levelSpanShortcut` | `F1` `x(L₀)`/`z(L₀)` |
| **E3** | `jacobian.ts` | §4.2 analytic partials | `F1`'s three partials + FD + symmetry |
| **E4** | `solve.ts` | §5 damped Newton, §5.2 seeds, §5.3 tol, §4.3 branches, `invalid-input` | `Z1` `Z2` `Z3` + 3 invariants |
| **E5** | `loads.ts` | §6.1 multi-segment `V(s)`, `solveAtHorizontalPosition` | `Z4` + C11 balance |
| **E6** | `sweep.ts` | §6.2 `sweepGrid`, march, warm start, `coverage` | `Z5` + `grid.length === 260` |
| **E7** | `terrain.ts` + `clearance.ts` | `Terrain` union, `zGroundAt`, §11 `evaluateClearance` | geometric clearance cases |
| **E7b** | **the bench** | `sweep.bench.ts` + the p95 tripwire test | ≤ 8 ms at 240 steps |
| **E8** | `materials/steel.ts`, `materials/textile.ts` | §13.1 K-factors, §13.2 `EA = k·MBL` | 9074 daN / 6,90 MN / 0,5465 kg/m |
| **E9** | `parameterise.ts` | §7 `resolveL0`, the bound re-emitted in the driving quantity | `Z6` + monotonicity |
| **E10** | `verdict.ts` | §10 regimes, `T_adm = η_t·MBF/k`, §10.4 gate, `T_DAF` | 8,80 / 9,06 · 12,29 vs 8,80 |
| **E11** | `index.ts` | the public barrel — the only path app code imports | no deep import outside `sim/` |
| **A0** | `package.json` | **pin the five TanStack versions** to the measured tree (§ 13) | `bun install` reproduces `router-core` 1.171.15 |
| **A1** | `scenario/first-run.ts` | `FIRST_RUN_SCENARIO` + the formatted-string lock | 11 strings + 3 structural |
| **A2** | quantity formatting | the display-precision table, one implementation, three consumers | table-driven cases |
| **A3** | CSV parse → `Trace` | the file contract, the seven parse failures, the provenance block | three real traces |
| **A4** | `url/p1.ts` | `encodeP1` / `validateP1` / `decodeP1` | 910 / 859 / 897 + safe-side |
| **A5** | `url/schema.ts` | `validateSearch`, the salvage table, canonical order, completion | first-run URL = 141 chars |
| **A6** | `session/session.ts` | `scoutech:session:v1`, read / write / clear | round-trip + version key |
| **A7** | the shell | `__root.tsx`, one route, `ssr: false`, Skeleton, refusal page, store | mount order, no hydration warning |
| **A8** | Chart Block | `SharedX`, both charts, layers, the four failure signals, VE | inverse round-trip, ×1 floor |
| **A9** | Rail input half | five groups, Tightness Spec, `Valeurs déduites`, **the three gates** | gate matrix |
| **A10** | Rail output half | `Résultats`, criteria × ends grid, five verdict states, Provenance | « Résultats sans régime » |
| **A11** | error surfaces | banner precedence, three refusals, « Saisie en cours », ignored keys | injected refusals |
| **A12** | narrow-screen reflow | B1 container query, B2 reflow, collapse, wrap, Handle Lane, touch | three pixel budgets |
| **P3** | **deletion** | remove `prototype.profile-view.tsx` and `-prototype-07/` | the three-item gate below |

`docs/conventions/routing.md` is already amended and is binding from A7 onward. `CONTEXT.md`'s
new terms and the two ADRs land with the seams they describe — « voir § 12 — décisions à
consigner ».

### P0 — the harness exists before the first line of physics

Three things are missing today and each blocks the first test rather than the last.

1. **`vitest` has no configuration at all.** `bun run test` is `vitest run` and `vite.config.ts`
   declares no `test` block, so `docs/conventions/testing.md`'s claim that « environment is
   jsdom » is currently false — the default is `node`. **The environment is `node`, always** —
   [amendment A](../zipline-simulator-v1-build/AMENDMENTS.md#a--vitest-is-for-unit-tests-only-no-component-tests):
   no `@vitest-environment` docblocks, no per-file DOM, and `jsdom` /
   `@testing-library/*` come out of `devDependencies`.

   *`node` only, because the engine is the bulk of the suite and is pure, and because component
   tests are out: what a DOM would have decided is now `agent-browser`'s. The link-arrival cases
   that wanted a `window.location` take the **raw query string as a parameter** instead — a pure
   function, and a better seam than the one the docblock was propping up.*
2. **A `bench` script.** `vitest bench` globs `*.bench.ts` / `*.benchmark.ts` and replaces
   `test.include` while it runs, so a bench never executes inside `bun run test` unless
   `benchmark.enabled` is set — leave it unset. Add `"bench": "vitest bench"` beside `"test"`.
3. **`docs/conventions/testing.md` is amended before the first test file exists**, not after —
   the diff is in « The test plan » below. The doc's standing `1e-3` TODO is discharged there.

Then `src/lib/sim/__fixtures__/theory-15.ts`, exporting `F1` and `Z1`–`Z6` with a `source` field
each. **`Z7` (thermal, §9) and `Z8` (dynamics, §12) are absent on purpose** — their physics is
out of v1 scope; add them with the feature, not before. §15's own « Excluded fixtures » list is
never ported, and no value is ever transcribed from `docs/references/` (§14 lists which worked
examples are fabricated).

### P1 — the engine, in §17's order

§17's staged plan is quoted exactly: « parabola prototype → inclined rigid catenary → **elastic
catenary + Jacobian (§4–§5) → point load (§6.1) → rolling sweep (§6.2) → inverse modes (§7) →
clearance (§11)** → dynamics & braking (§12) ». Two of those stages are not modules in this
build and one is out of scope, which is worth stating so nobody builds them:

- **The parabola ships as a seed, not as a model.** It survives only as §5.2's initial guess
  inside `solve.ts` (`H ≈ w·L²/(8·D_est)`, `D_est = √(3·L·(L₀−chord)/8)`, `V₀ ≈ −w·L₀/2 +
  h·H/L`). *A seed rather than a selectable model, because §3's hierarchy is a validity map, not
  a menu — and a second visible model would need its own verdict story.*
- **The inclined rigid catenary is a test, not a file.** It appears once, as the `EA → 10¹⁴`
  limit invariant that §15 already cross-checked against the closed-form inextensible catenary
  to 7 s.f. *An invariant rather than a module, because the elastic catenary reduces to it and
  ticket 03's layout has no file for it.*
- **§12 (dynamics, braking) is out of scope** and so is §9 (thermal, creep) — the map's physics
  scope is §4–§7, §10, §11. `resolveL0` exists partly so that §9's `ΔL₀` story *can* compose
  later, which is not the same as building it.

Stage-by-stage, with what each one is gated on:

| Stage | Depends on | Gate — the fixtures that must go green |
| --- | --- | --- |
| E2 `catenary.ts` | E1 types | `F1` `x(L₀) = 97.0329`, `z(L₀) = 24.2392` — the §2.1 sign trap fires here or nowhere |
| E3 `jacobian.ts` | E2 | the three partials, `∂z/∂H = ∂x/∂V₀`, finite differences < 2×10⁻⁹ |
| E4 `solve.ts` | E3 | `Z1` `Z2` (level, mild + deep slack), `Z3` (inclined), symmetry, `EA → ∞` |
| E5 `loads.ts` | E4 | `Z4` at `s_P = 50.2500`, then the same state reached from a typed `x_D = 50` |
| E6 `sweep.ts` | E5 | `Z5` at `s_D = 60.15`, `sweepGrid(L₀, 240).length === 260`, determinism |
| E7 clearance | E6 | flat-ground geometry, the three `contact` states, deep-slack cable contact |
| E8 materials | E1 only | steel derivation; textile `EA = k·MBL`; **no shared base module** |
| E9 `parameterise.ts` | E4 + E8 | `Z6`: `L₀ = 100.670` from `H_target = 2451`, seed 100.669 valid |
| E10 `verdict.ts` | E6 + E8 | the criteria × ends grid, `T_DAF`, `coverage < 1 ⇒ Indéterminé` |

Three ordering choices inside P1 that look arbitrary and are not:

- **`jacobian.ts` before `solve.ts`**, although Newton is what uses it. §15 gives `F1`'s
  partials as *first-class fixture values*, so the Jacobian is verifiable in isolation; verified
  after the solver, a sign error in it shows up as a convergence mystery instead of a failed
  assertion.
- **`clearance.ts` before `verdict.ts`**, although the verdict is the more visible output. The
  clearance reducer is the larger half of the frame budget (p95 ≤ 1,7 ms against `sweep()`'s ≤
  0,6 ms) and the bench cannot exist without it — and E7b is the deletion gate.
- **`parameterise.ts` after `materials/`**, because `resolveL0`'s failure path re-emits the §5.4
  bound *in the driving quantity* (« Prétension maximale atteignable : 9 074 daN »), and
  9 074 daN is the steel module's output. Built before E8 it would be tested against a
  hand-typed strength.

### E7b — port the bench, and what does not survive the port

**Port the bench harness and `check.ts`'s §15 assertions into the real build the moment
`evaluateClearance` exists.** Ticket 12's tripwire — « re-run the bench if anything raises `260
× 60` » — currently names `/prototype/profile-view?variant=D&scenario=taut`, a route scheduled
for deletion. Ported, it names `bun run bench`. Not ported, the only measured performance
evidence on this map becomes unreproducible.

What the port reproduces: the harness shape (40 runs per cell; `SWEEP_STEPS` at 100, 240, 500;
the three worst-conditioned spans — 1000 m quasi tendu, 1000 m très mou, 1500 m long; p95 of
`sweep()` **plus** `evaluateClearance`, the pair, since both run on a parameter change).

**What does not survive, stated rather than quietly lost:**

| Measurement | Fate after deletion |
| --- | --- |
| `sweep()` + `evaluateClearance` p95 (1,30 ms at 240, 2,30 ms at 500) | ported — but re-measured in Node, not Chromium |
| the auto-drag frame p50 16,70 ms / p95 18,40 ms against a 16,7 ms vsync | **not portable** — jsdom has no vsync, no rAF cadence, no layout |
| the ~0,1 ms `performance.now()` clamp caveat | Node's timer is finer; several cells will stop reading equal |

So the ported bench is a **relative tripwire against the 8 ms criterion**, not a reproduction of
1,30 ms. Assert the criterion, never the measurement: the margin is ≈6× at 240 steps and ≈3,5×
at 500, which is what makes a threshold assertion stable in CI. The drag figure is re-taken **by
hand** in the real app at A12, in a browser, and recorded — *because the conclusion it supports
(dragging is vsync-bound, so off-threading the sweep would not make it smoother) is one of the
two independent reasons there is no Web Worker.*

### P2 — the app layers, in dependency order

- **A1 before everything else in the app.** `FIRST_RUN_SCENARIO` is one constant object serving
  the opening state, the reset target *and* the link-completion values — and its
  formatted-string lock is the engine's only end-to-end test. It is also the sole data source
  A8's charts have before A9 exists. *One object rather than three copies, because
  « Réinitialiser le scénario » and a link that omits keys must land on exactly the same values
  a fresh visit does.*
- **A2 before A4 and A5.** Display precision is load-bearing *state*, not formatting: a prefill
  writes the **displayed** value into state, and the stale-Override badge compares override
  against formula **at display precision**. Built after the URL codec, it would be written twice
  and the badge would fire on every load for every override (the formula gives 9073,98 where
  state holds 9074). One implementation, three consumers — « voir § 3 — le modèle de paramètres,
  *Précision d'affichage par grandeur* ».
- **A3 before A4.** `encodeP1` takes a `Trace`; the parser is what produces one.
- **A5 after A1 + A2 + A4.** `validateSearch` needs the completion values (A1), the
  daN-in-the-URL precision rule (A2) and `validateP1` (A4) — the `tr` key is validated
  structurally and kept **verbatim**, never decoded inside `validateSearch`.
- **A7 after A6.** The Session is read in a **lazy initialiser at the client-only subtree's
  first render**, so `readSession()` must exist before the store does.
- **A8 before A9.** The Chart Block renders from `FIRST_RUN_SCENARIO` alone; the Rail's fields
  then drive something visible from their first keystroke.
- **A9 carries its own gates.** The three named gates (Solve / Clearance / Verdict) are wired
  field by field as the fields are built. *Gates with the fields rather than as a later pass,
  because a field built without its gate is a field written twice — and « régime choisi » is
  **struck** from the solve tier, so the app must open verdict-less and still compute, which is
  a property of how A9 is built, not something bolted on at A11.*
- **A11 after A10.** The banner slot holds exactly one occupant across four ranks, and the ranks
  include the verdict — so the verdict must exist before precedence can be implemented at all.
- **A12 last.** The reflow is CSS over a finished tree (`flex-col`, `order-last`, one container
  query on the chart column, no `useMediaQuery` branch anywhere), and its own re-checks can only
  be measured on a real layout.

### P3 — the deletion, and the three things it is gated on

`src/routeTree.gen.ts:12` still carries `src/routes/prototype.profile-view.tsx`, so **a build
today ships the prototype as a public route**. Deletion is *sequenced*, not performed at
assembly time, and it is gated on three items — the first two because the evidence dies with the
artifact, the third because the prototype is the only place the comparison can be made:

1. **P0.2 + E7b done** — §15 fixtures are Vitest cases and the bench runs from `bun run bench`.
2. **A12's re-checks done** — the three pixel budgets (390×664, 768×1024, 1440×900, each summing
   to the viewport), the banner heights **44 / 60 / 76 px** and the View Strip's label widths.
   These are computed from the prototype's Tailwind classes, *not rendered* — the only numbers
   in the layout contract that are not measured on the artifact.
3. **The first-run screen's auto-fit VE confirmed at ≈ ×1,5** on a 1080×420 plot — the first
   concrete point on the published ×1…×5,5 band that the app actually ships, and a check that it
   sits inside the Plot Aspect Band [0.35, 0.50] and clear of the ×1 floor.

Then delete `src/routes/prototype.profile-view.tsx` **and** `src/routes/-prototype-07/` (nine
files) and let `@tanstack/router-plugin` regenerate `src/routeTree.gen.ts` — never hand-edit it.

**Do NOT lift `src/routes/-prototype-07/solver.ts` into `src/lib/sim/`.** Ticket 07 §12 laid it
out deliberately so it could not be mistaken for a head start, and this is not only an appeal to
that intent — lifting it means rewriting it:

| The prototype has | The contract needs |
| --- | --- |
| `SpanInput = { L, h, L0, w, EA, P?, sP? }` — one load, flattened | `SpanInput = { L, h, L0, w, EA, loads: PointLoad[] }` |
| no Jacobian export | `jacobian.ts` as its own file, because §15 asserts its partials |
| no `resolveL0`, no `Z6` | §7's three modes and the bound re-emitted in the driving quantity |
| `SolveError` without `bound` / `boundKind` | the amended payload — « Tension impossible » is unwritable without it |
| no `verdict.ts`, no `materials/` | §10, §13.1's K-factors, §13.2's `EA = k·MBL`, the §10.4 gate |
| one file | eleven modules by concept, each citing its § in a header comment |

*Rebuild TDD from §15 rather than port, because the prototype's value was answering design
questions — which it did, and its numbers are quoted throughout this spec as measurements. Its
code's only remaining use is as a **checklist**: `check.ts` records exactly which fixtures were
verified and to what error.*

---

## § 11 — The test plan

Provenance: `docs/simulation-theory.md` §15 (the only fixture source), ticket 03 §8 (the layout,
already written into `docs/conventions/testing.md`), ticket 07 §10 and §12 (the measured
tolerance and the bench), ticket 09 §6 as discharged (the `p1` traces), ticket 14 (the first-run
lock) and ticket 11 with the final elections (the injected-only states).

### Tolerance — 4×10⁻⁶ is the bar, and the `1e-3` TODO is discharged

`docs/conventions/testing.md` says « assert at `1e-3` relative for now », with §15's instruction
to tighten « once the production solver's own tolerance is tuned ». The tolerance is now fixed
by §5.3 (`1×10⁻⁶ m` absolute for spans ≲ 1000 m, `1×10⁻⁶·L` scaled beyond) and a solver at that
tolerance was **measured against every ported fixture at ≤ 4×10⁻⁶ relative** (`F1` and
`Z1`–`Z5`, ticket 07's answer preamble). That is the bar. Four tiers, because they measure four
different things:

| Tier | Bar | Applies to | Why this number |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Solved scalars** | `4×10⁻⁶` relative | `H`, `V₀`, `V_B`, `T_A`, `T_B`, `T_max`, `L₀`, `z_D`, `s_P`, `σ_D` | measured achievable; §15 states 6 s.f., whose own rounding floor is ~5×10⁻⁷, so the bar is above the transcription noise and below anything a real regression does |
| **Positions of scanned extrema** | absolute, **2× the sample spacing** | `x` at `z_min`, `x` at max sag, `x_D` recovered from a node | an argmax found by scanning a sampled polyline is limited by the spacing, not by the solver: 0,10 m at `Z1`'s `n = 2000` over 100 m, 0,06 m at `Z3`'s `n = 4000` over 120 m — which is what `check.ts`'s `2e-3` / `5e-3` relative amounted to |
| **§15's own stated invariants** | as §15 states them | Jacobian vs finite differences **< 2×10⁻⁹**; `EA → 10¹⁴` vs closed-form inextensible **7 s.f.**; global force balance on point-load cases **10⁻¹⁰**; level-span `V₀ = −w·L₀/2` **exact** | quoted, not chosen |
| **`[MEASURED]` cases** | `1e-3` relative | anything sourced from a resolved ticket's measurement rather than from §15 — the first-run statics, the clearance sensitivities, the textile verdict pair | the recorded value *is* the measurement's precision (3–4 s.f.); a tighter bar would assert digits nobody measured |

**Tag the source in the fixture, not in a comment.** §15 rows carry `source:
"simulation-theory.md §15 Z4"`; measured rows carry `source: "[MEASURED] ticket 14 §2"`. *Two
provenance classes rather than one pile, because a §15 row is MoorPy-cross-validated physics and
a measured row is this app's own output at a moment in time — treating them alike invites
someone to tighten the wrong one and to trust the wrong one.*

### Which fixture lands where

One test file per source file, colocated, same basename. All of these run in the **`node`**
environment: statics is terrain-free and the engine is sync and pure, so **not one engine test
needs a terrain stub, a DOM or an async harness** — that seam is exactly what ticket 03 bought.

| §15 | File | Asserted | Tier |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `F1` | `catenary.test.ts` | `x(L₀) = 97.0329 m`, `z(L₀) = 24.2392 m` | solved |
| `F1` | `jacobian.test.ts` | `∂x/∂H = 1.16024×10⁻³`, `∂x/∂V₀ = ∂z/∂H = −4.55088×10⁻³`, `∂z/∂V₀ = 1.82564×10⁻²` (m/N) | solved |
| `Z1` | `solve.test.ts` | `H = 1423.94 N`, `V₀ = −251.250 N`, `V_B = +251.250 N`, `T_max = 1445.94 N` | solved |
| `Z1` | `catenary.test.ts` | `z_min = −4.39987 m`; `x` at `z_min` = 50.0 m | solved / scanned |
| `Z2` | `solve.test.ts` | `H = 1442.11 N`, `V₀ = −510.000 N`, `T_A = T_B = 1529.63 N`, `z_min = −8.75376 m` | solved |
| `Z3` | `solve.test.ts` | `H = 1286.38 N`, `V₀ = −468.701 N`, `V_B = +141.299 N`, `T_A = 1369.11 N`, `T_B = 1294.12 N`, **and `T_A > T_B`** | solved |
| `Z3` | `catenary.test.ts` | sag below chord `D = 7.0811 m` at `x ≈ 59.7`; `z_min − h = −1.5476 m` | solved / scanned |
| `Z4` | `loads.test.ts` | `s_P = 50.2500 m` (= `L₀/2`), `H = 7898.16 N`, `V₀ = −1091.00 N`, `V_B = +1091.00 N`, `z_D = −5.31434 m`, `T_A = T_B = 7973.16 N` | solved |
| `Z4` | `loads.test.ts` | `solveAtHorizontalPosition(x_D = 50)` recovers the same state and `s_P = 50.2500` | solved |
| `Z5` | `sweep.test.ts` | `H = 7765.67 N`, `V₀ = −973.369 N`, `x_D = 59.9578 m`, `z_D = −5.19064 m`, `σ_D = 60.1969 m` | solved |
| `Z6` | `parameterise.test.ts` | `L₀ = 100.670 m` from `H_target = 2451 N`; forward re-solve gives `H = 2451.000`; sag `D = 5.1164 m`; the §7 closed-form seed **100.669** lies inside the bracket | solved |
| `Z7` `Z8` | — | **not ported.** §9 and §12 are out of v1 scope | — |

Four of those assertions are **new work, not a port** — `check.ts` never made them, because the
prototype exposes no Jacobian and implements no inverse mode. Do not read a green `check.ts` as
coverage:

1. `F1`'s three partials, and `∂z/∂H = ∂x/∂V₀` as an equality.
2. The finite-difference agreement (< 2×10⁻⁹).
3. `Z6` entirely.
4. `Z5`'s stretched arc position `σ_D = 60.1969 m` — the quantity that makes `s_D` ≠ `x_D`
   concrete (60,15 against 59,96, ≈0,3 %), which is *why* `s_D` never appears in the UI.

### Invariants get their own cases, because fixture values let sign errors through

§15 says so explicitly and §2.1 is the reason: a mixed sign convention returns `z(L₀) = 24.2342`
against the correct **24.2392**, and R1 claimed 24.84 — three plausible-looking numbers.

| Invariant | Where | Bar |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `∂z/∂H = ∂x/∂V₀` | `jacobian.test.ts` | same expression ⇒ assert equality, not a tolerance |
| analytic vs finite differences | `jacobian.test.ts` | < 2×10⁻⁹ |
| `V₀ = −w·L₀/2` for `h = 0`, `loads: []` | `solve.test.ts` | exact — it is `levelSpanShortcut`'s whole claim |
| `EA → 10¹⁴` vs closed-form inextensible catenary | `catenary.test.ts` | 7 s.f. |
| global force balance with a point load | `loads.test.ts` | 10⁻¹⁰ |
| `V_B = (w·L₀ + P)/2` on `Z4` | `loads.test.ts` | **and explicitly not `(qL + 2P)/2`** |
| `sweepGrid(L₀, 240).length === 260`, and the grid is a pure function of `(L₀, n)` | `sweep.test.ts` | exact |

The C11 case is a carried rejection, not a duplicate of `Z4`: the old report's `V_high =
(qL+2P)/2` **double-counts `P`**, and `Z4`'s `V_B = 1091.00 = (10·100.5 + 1177)/2` is what
disproves it. Assert the correct closed form *and* that the double-counted one does not match,
or the next reader "corrects" the formula back.

### Physics with no §15 fixture — §10, §11, §13

Three modules have no cross-validated fixture, and inventing one is forbidden. Their cases come
from measurements recorded in resolved tickets, tagged `[MEASURED]` and asserted at `1e-3`:

| Module | Cases | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `materials/steel.ts` | `d` 12 mm, `R₀` 1770, 6×19 âme métallique ⇒ `MBF` 9074 daN, `EA` 6,90 MN, `μ` 0,5465 kg/m; the un-rounded formula value **9073,98 daN** (the Override badge's input) | ticket 14 §3 |
| `materials/textile.ts` | `EA = k·MBL` at `k` 22; and that steel and textile share **no** helper — a `materials/index.ts` barrel is the specific "tidying" that undoes the A/B separation | ticket 05, ticket 03 §7 |
| `clearance.ts` | garde min **2,44 m at x 74,1**, `contact: "none"`, 0 steps in violation on the first-run line; garde is **independent of dénivelé** (2,46 / 2,44 / 2,37 / 2,25 m at `h` = 0 / −18 / −40 / −60) and moves metre-for-metre with flèche (3,39 / 2,44 / 1,44 / 0,40 m at flèche 5 / 6 / 7 / 8) | ticket 14 §1 |
| `clearance.ts` | `contact: "cable"` — `L₀` 400 m on a 200 m span ⇒ garde **−169,67 m**; `contact: "rider"` — anchors A 12 / B 6 at flèche 6,0 ⇒ **−1,53 m** | elections §0.2, ticket 14 §1 |
| `verdict.ts` | `FS effectif` **8,80 (A) / 9,06 (B)**, `T adm` 2722 daN, réserve **−111 daN**, `T_DAF` 927,9 / 901,5 daN from `T_vide` 263,4 / 253,7 and `T_chargé` 817,1 / 793,5 at `DAF` 1,2 | ticket 14 §1 |
| `verdict.ts` | the suspended-load cell is **one** cell with numerator `min(η_A, η_B)·MBF` over `DAF·(T_chargé − T_vide)` ⇒ **12,29**, against **8,80** on total tension, so **total tension governs**. Assert the wrong readings too: bare `MBF/P` scores **77** and can never bind | ticket 14, elections §5 |
| `verdict.ts` | §10.4 gate: `PERMANENTE` + textile is rejected; the textile pair `MBL` 100 kN ⇒ **11,94** « dans les limites » and 60 kN ⇒ **7,63** « hors limites » on the same line | ticket 14 §3 |
| `verdict.ts` | `coverage < 1` **suppresses** the verdict (`Indéterminé`); it never qualifies it | ticket 03 §6 |

The taut-line double warning is a case, not a bug: the minimum attainable flèche/portée is
**0,15 % at 200 m** and **0,72 % at 1000 m**, both at the breaking-load bound, so §8.2's `< 3 %`
⚠ fires across the whole feasible taut band *together with* the clause invalidating the DAF's
measurement regime. Assert that both fire, so nobody later removes one as a duplicate.

### `no-convergence`, `slack-limit` and every `SolveNote` are injected, never hunted

Measured inside the admissible input envelope: **349 440** instrumented solves with `reseeded`
firing **0** times and §4.3's near-taut condition `w·L₀/H < 10⁻⁴` firing **0** times (minimum
observed ratio **3,51×10⁻⁴** — ≈3,5× more tension than the §5.4 floor admits); damping halved
`α` in **2 114** iterations (0,6 %); **3 792** sweeps all returned coverage **1,000**. Deep
slack does not fail either — it converges and lands as `contact: "cable"`.

**A test that waits for a pathological input to reach one of these states tests nothing.** It
either never gets there, or it gets there through an input the app's own ranges refuse, which
proves nothing about the guard.

| State | Reachable from input? | How the test produces it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `invalid-input{field:"L0"}` | **yes** — it is the §5.4 chord bound | real values: `L₀` **197,404 m** on a 200 m level span (`T_max` = 100,0 % MBF), **987,02 m** on 1000 m (101,1 %). Assert `bound` and `boundKind`, not just the kind |
| `no-convergence` | no | `vi.mock` `jacobian.ts` to return a singular matrix; assert `attempts` reflects §5.2's **≤ 5** re-seeds and that `residual` is carried |
| `slack-limit` | no | `vi.mock` the branch that floors horizontal stiffness (`slack_kx = 0.01·|w|`) so `H → 0`; assert `hFloored` |
| `damped` note | in aggregate only (0,6 %) | call the damping branch directly — it is a named module export |
| `reseeded` note | no | forced through the mocked singular Jacobian above, with a cap that lets attempt 2 succeed |
| `near-taut-fallback` note | no | call the straight-elastic-bar branch directly at `w·L₀/H < 10⁻⁴` |
| `Indéterminé` (coverage < 1) | no | `vi.mock` `solveSpan` to fail at one step index inside `sweep`; assert `coverage === 259/260`, `failures[0].index`, the suppressed verdict, and that **« — » appears here and nowhere else** |

**Inject by mocking the module under test, not by widening a signature.** `solveSpan(input,
warmStart?)` and the rest are **contract** — « voir § 2 — le contrat du moteur » — so a
test-only third parameter would grow the public surface in order to make a guard reachable,
which is the tail wagging the dog. `vi.mock` on the collaborator (`jacobian.ts`, `catenary.ts`,
`solve.ts`) reaches every branch without touching the API.

**Two things the suite does not do, deliberately.**

- **No test asserts that a state is unreachable.** Unreachability is a property of the *input
  envelope*, not of the code; the measurements above are the record, and CI's job is
  regressions.
- **The 349 440-solve envelope sweep does not run in CI.** It is a minutes-long one-off. Re-run
  it by hand if §5's constants, §5.2's seeds or ticket 04's ranges move — that, and not a green
  suite, is what would invalidate « no `SolveNote` ever reaches the UI ».

Also assert the **surfaces**, since the mapping is what users see: `no-convergence` and
`slack-limit` both render **« Calcul impossible »** (one surface, two kinds — both map to the
same user action), `invalid-input{field:"L0"}` renders **« Tension impossible »** with the
attainable bound and **no fix button**, `resolveL0`'s bracket failure renders **« Cible
inatteignable »** naming the *target*, and `resolveL0` re-emits the bound in the driving
quantity — « Flèche minimale atteignable : 0,29 m », « Prétension maximale atteignable :
9 074 daN », **never** an `L₀` in metres beside a tension field.

### The first-run lock asserts formatted strings, not floats

`src/lib/scenario/first-run.test.ts`, colocated beside `first-run.ts`. A **regression lock, not
a physics fixture** — §15 keeps that job and its own tiers.

**`1e-3` relative on `L₀` is ±0,15 m, and 0,15 m of `L₀` moves garde by ≈0,15 m — a tolerance
that would let the opening screen drift by 6 % of its safety margin and stay green.** What is
frozen is what the user reads, so the assertion is the rendered string:

```
« 151,64 m » · « 253,5 daN » · « 6,00 m » · « 4,00 % » · « 2,90 % » · « 817,1 daN »
· « 927,9 daN » · « 7,48 m » · « 2,44 m » · « 8,80 » · « 9,06 »
```

Plus three structural assertions no string carries: **`contact === "none"`**, **`coverage === 1`
over `grid.length === 260`**, and **zero steps in violation**.

Side effects worth having, and reasons not to split this test up: the same lock freezes the
**French decimal comma**, the **per-quantity display-precision table** (every row appears inside
one of those strings) and the shipped rider position `x_D = 75,0 m` — which is 0,9 m from the
traverse minimum and therefore inside ticket 07's `max(1 m, 1 % of span)` rule, so the clearance
cell must read plainly « Garde min » on first paint instead of relabelling itself.

One collision the lock settles rather than hides: it freezes « 817,1 daN » at 1 decimal while the
rail sketches print that same quantity — the **`T max statique` detail row**, and the strip's
per-position `T_A` cell — at 0 decimals, « 817 daN ». **The frozen strings govern**; anyone
preferring 0 decimals there changes the lock's expectations in the same commit. Note this is a
*precision* collision on one quantity and nothing to do with the headline: the Rail's headline
`T max` is the **DAF-amplified** 928 daN, never 817, because `FS effectif` divides by it.

### The `p1` codec — round-trip against the three real traces

`src/lib/url/p1.test.ts`, `node` environment, reading the three
`docs/references/example-profile-trace*.csv` files from disk (183,4 / 224,0 / 188,4 m, **200
rows each**). There are **three**, not two, as tickets 06, 09 and 14 all say.

| Assertion | Value | Why it is a test and not a comment |
| --- | --- | --- |
| URL cost, exact | **910 / 859 / 897** characters | discharges ticket 09 §6's estimate; a change here means the codec changed |
| synthetic 200-point spans at 1000 / 1500 / 2000 m | **1065 – 1071** characters | the general (non-uniform-`d`) case |
| whole URL with the readable keys | **≤ 2000** characters | the budget chat clients, mail and QR codes actually impose |
| `d` round-trips **exactly** | bit-for-bit | stations are full-precision scalars `sa`/`sb`; `L = sb − sa` is unaffected by cm quantisation |
| altitude error | **≤ 0,010 m** | cm quantisation, an order under the ≈5 mm convexity finding |
| altitude is **never lower** than the original, every row | `z_decoded ≥ z_original` | the ceiling rule — the one lossy step is provably **safe-side**. This is the assertion that catches someone "fixing" ceiling to nearest |
| `decodeP1(encodeP1(t))` re-encodes to the identical string | idempotent | catches non-canonical varint emission |
| `dev` round-trips, sentinel included | — | « rectitude non vérifiable » must survive a link, or every shared trace degrades to unverifiable and the recipient loses a disclosure the sender had |
| point count is unchanged | 200 in, 200 out | **no resampling** — resampling was killed for making a coarse import indistinguishable from a fine one; shrinking the payload by resampling smuggles it back in |
| `validateP1` is structural only | valid ⇒ returned **verbatim**; garbage ⇒ throws | decoding cannot happen inside `validateSearch`, which runs synchronously inside `buildLocation` |
| oversize | a hand-authored 4 812-point trace refuses at « Copier le lien » | refuse rather than degrade: a terrain-less link of an imported scenario is a *different* scenario |
| the first-run URL | **141 characters**, exact | discharges ticket 09 §6's ≈200-char estimate and locks the key table plus canonical order |

**Decode timing (2,3–2,5 µs) belongs in the bench, not in a test.** A microsecond assertion in
CI is a flake generator; the same number in `p1.bench.ts` is a tripwire that a human reads.

`src/lib/url/schema.test.ts` covers the salvage table as a table-driven case set — refusal (`v`
unknown, `tr` undecodable or oversize, two tightness drivers present) throws with
`error.routerCode === 'VALIDATE_SEARCH'`; an unknown or unrepresentable key is **ignored and
named**; an out-of-range but representable value is **applied as-is** (the URL is a typing
boundary, not a validation boundary); recognised absent keys are **completed from
`FIRST_RUN_SCENARIO`**, with `reg` and `mbf`/`ea`/`mu` exempt. Two limits worth stating:
`validateSearch` runs an unknown number of times per request, so its purity is a **review**
rule, and `Route.useSearch()` returning raw ∪ validated at runtime while typed validated-only is
a trap **no test can catch** — « clé ignorée » is an app-level guarantee the router does not
enforce.

### App-layer tests — what jsdom can decide, and what it cannot

The layout contract is arithmetic on one input (the chart column's inner width) precisely
because **no chart dimension may read the window height**. That makes the arithmetic
unit-testable in `node`, with no DOM at all:

| Testable as a pure function (`node`) | Bar |
| --- | --- |
| profile inner height `= 0,42 × W_inner` (Plot Aspect Band [0,35 ; 0,50] as guard); strip outer height `= clamp(0,16 × W_inner, 96, 200)`, policy band [72, 200] | exact |
| `VE = plot aspect × (x-domain span / z-domain span)`, with the **×1 floor — auto-fit never compresses** | exact; the floor is its own disclosed badge state |
| the published band ×1 … ×5,5, checked against ticket 15's seven-scenario `k` table | inside the band |
| `SharedX`: one object per (column inline size, `L`, terrain source), `GUTTER = 24` on both sides of **both** charts, and `xFromPointer(pointerFromX(x)) === x` | round-trip to float precision |
| the read-out row wraps to 3 columns below **600 px** of chart-column width | exact threshold |
| the three validation gates as predicates over a Scenario | gate matrix |
| the banner slot: exactly one occupant across four ranks | pure reducer |
| the per-field tier-one messages (17 rows) and the culprit table keyed by (quantity × mode) | table-driven |
| the stale-Override badge: compared **at display precision**, so an untouched 9074 daN override does **not** badge against a 9073,98 formula value | must not fire |

The `SharedX` round-trip is a regression test against a defect found in the artifact, which is
the best reason to write one: the prototype padded the profile `{left 16, right 16}` and the
strip `{left 24, right 24}` — two x axes misaligned by **8 px at each end, 34 m at a 1500 m
span** — and its hand-typed pointer inverse is wrong by **0,05 L** under the real `1,1 L`
domain.

**What jsdom cannot decide, and must therefore not be tested there:** jsdom has no layout, so
every element measures 0×0 and `useSize` never fires. The three pixel budgets, the banner
heights (44 / 60 / 76 px), the View Strip's label widths and the first-run VE (≈ ×1,5) are
**manual re-checks in a real browser at A12**, listed in P3's deletion gate. *Writing them as
jsdom tests would produce a suite that passes on a broken layout — worse than no test, because
it reads as coverage.*

> ⚠ **Superseded** by
> [amendments A and B](../zipline-simulator-v1-build/AMENDMENTS.md#a--vitest-is-for-unit-tests-only-no-component-tests):
> **there are no component tests and no jsdom.** The Vitest environment is `node`, always. The four
> states below keep their specified behaviour and change owner — `agent-browser` drives them in a
> real browser ([tickets 29–35](../zipline-simulator-v1-build/issues/29-responsive-layout-browser-evidence.md)),
> which also take over the five manual re-checks named just above. Two seams become pure so they stay
> unit-testable in `node`: the dropped-keys reporter takes the **raw query string** as a parameter, and
> `readSession` takes the **storage object**.

The four states that were to be component-tested, now `agent-browser`'s: the Skeleton renders the
Rail's five headings and both chart frames but **no control, no value and no « — »**; the refusal page
is the *index* route's `errorComponent`; « Saisie en cours » keeps the last valid Sweep marked as such
while suppressing the verdict and the URL write; « Aucun résultat » blanks the output half and leaves
the strip a scrub track with its axes.

### The diff to `docs/conventions/testing.md`

The doc already carries ticket 03's layout and needs six changes. Apply them **before the first
test file exists** (P0.1):

1. **Environment.** « Environment is `jsdom` » becomes **`node`, always**, and the
   « React component tests use `@testing-library/react` » line is **deleted** — Vitest is
   unit-only, and `agent-browser` owns point-to-point app testing
   ([amendments A and B](../zipline-simulator-v1-build/AMENDMENTS.md)). `jsdom`,
   `@testing-library/react` and `@testing-library/dom` leave `devDependencies`. Note that
   `vite.config.ts` has no `test` block today.
2. **Tolerance.** Replace « assert at `1e-3` relative for now » and its single TODO with the
   four-tier table above. **The TODO is discharged** — record why (§5.3's tolerance is fixed and
   ≤ 4×10⁻⁶ was measured), so nobody re-opens it as an oversight.
3. **Fixture provenance.** Keep « never transcribe from `docs/references/` » (§14) and add the
   two `source` classes: §15 rows versus `[MEASURED]` rows, and that `check.ts` is a **coverage
   checklist, not a value source** — with the four assertions it never made.
4. **A benches subsection.** `*.bench.ts`, colocated; `bun run bench` → `vitest bench`; never
   part of `bun run test` (leave `benchmark.enabled` unset). What the bench measures, the `260 ×
   60` tripwire, and the caveat that it runs in Node so it does not reproduce 1,30 ms. One API
   note: the bench result exposes tinybench's `p75` / `p99` / `p995` / `p999` and **no `p95`**,
   while the criterion is stated in p95 — so the **threshold assertion is a plain `test` that
   times 40 runs and takes its own p95**, against **8 ms**, never against the measurement.
   (`retainSamples` would also expose the raw samples; the hand-rolled loop is kept because it
   reproduces the prototype harness exactly.)
5. **An injected-states subsection.** The no-hunting rule, the envelope measurements as the
   record, `vi.mock` on the collaborator rather than a test-only parameter, and the standing
   rule that **no test asserts unreachability**.
6. **One line the convention did *not* need**: colocated one-test-per-source-file survives every
   app layer unchanged — `first-run.test.ts` beside `first-run.ts`, `p1.test.ts` beside `p1.ts`.
   **No `tests/` directory and no suite file spanning modules**, and no exception was needed to
   keep it.

### Decisions deferred to implementation, recorded rather than left vague

Two, both small, both stated so they are choices rather than omissions:

1. **The formatting module's path.** No ticket fixed one. The requirement is fixed: **one**
   implementation of the display-precision table, shared by the prefill rule, the stale-Override
   badge and the first-run lock — never a per-component `toFixed`. `src/lib/format/quantity.ts`
   is the suggestion; the path is free, the singleness is not.
2. **The CSV parser's path.** Likewise unfixed. `terrain.ts` in `src/lib/sim/` owns the
   `Terrain` union and `zGroundAt` and must stay engine-pure, so **the parser lives outside
   `src/lib/sim/`** and the Trace → `Terrain` derivation is the single seam between them — which
   is the property that keeps the §15 fixtures free of terrain stubs.


---

## § 12 — Décisions à consigner — les ADR

Five decisions on this map are worth a durable record outside this spec. They are the ones a
future reader meets in the code, assumes are accidents, and "fixes". Everything else that was
decided is stated where it is used and needs no second home.

### The bar, and how it was applied

`/domain-modeling`'s bar is three conditions, all of which must hold: **hard to reverse** ·
**surprising without context** · **the result of a genuine trade-off**. Ticket 10 named two
candidates (the engine execution model, the terrain model), ticket 08 added a third (the verdict
grammar), and two more were weighed for a fourth slot. All five clear. Six further decisions
were tested against the bar and **rejected** — listed below with the reason, because an ADR set
diluted by obvious choices stops being read, which defeats the purpose.

One reading of the bar governs three of these five and should be stated once rather than argued
five times: **"hard to reverse" is about the cost of being wrong, not the size of the diff.**
Resampling a terrain array, deleting a `max(autofit, 1)`, and changing « Dans les limites — EN
15567-1 (k = 3,0) » to « Conforme » are each a few characters. Each is also silent, plausible,
and in the direction that kills. Those are exactly the reversals that need a record: cheap to
make, expensive to detect.

| Where they live | `docs/adr/`, created lazily — **there is no `docs/adr/` in the repo today**, so this is the repo's first ADR set |
| --- | --- |
| Numbering | sequential, `0001`–`0005`, filenames as given below |
| Language | **English prose** — an ADR is developer documentation. Only quoted UI copy is French, in « guillemets » with the decimal comma |
| Written | **all five before step 1 of § Ordre d'implémentation.** They record decisions already taken, not decisions the build makes, and ADR-0001 and ADR-0002 constrain the code the first step produces |
| Linking | an ADR **must not depend on `.scratch/` surviving.** Cite `docs/simulation-theory.md` by §, cite the ticket path for provenance, and name the spec section by title rather than by link |

Each ADR below is the file's whole content. Keep them at one screen: the detail lives in the
spec section each one names, and an ADR that grows into a second spec stops being read for the
same reason a diluted set does.

---

#### ADR-0001 — `docs/adr/0001-synchronous-pure-engine-with-a-sweep-seam.md`

**The engine is synchronous and pure, results are scalars, and clearance is a reducer**

**Context.** `src/lib/sim/` implements `docs/simulation-theory.md` §4–§7, §10 and §11 for spans
of 50–2000 m. The rider is moved by dragging, so the whole traverse is recomputed interactively;
and §11's clearance minimum is global over rider positions, so the traverse must exist before
any verdict can be honest. Ticket 03 fixed an execution model **before** any timing existed, and
ticket 12 then measured against it.

**Decision.**

- One `solveSpan(input)` with `loads: PointLoad[]` — the unloaded case is `loads: []`, because
  §6.1 keeps the unknowns at `(H, V₀)` whatever `n` is. §7's three tightness modes resolve to
  `L₀` **above** the solver, in `resolveL0`, which makes `L₀` first-class state.
- **Results are scalars only.** `SpanState` plus analytic `sampleCable(state, n)` (§4.1 closed
  form); **no polyline is ever stored in a result object**.
- **§11 clearance is a reducer over the sweep**, not a field on it. The solver never sees
  terrain; terrain enters the engine at exactly one seam, `zGroundAt`.
- The sweep is **precomputed and deterministic from `(L₀, n)`** — 240 uniform steps plus 10
  geometric tail steps per end, endpoints excluded, `grid.length === 260`. The slider is an
  index lookup, never a solve.
- **Failure is a value**, a union of three kinds (`invalid-input`, `no-convergence`,
  `slack-limit`), never a throw. Terrain crosses as **data** (`Terrain` union), never a closure.
- The core is **plain sync pure functions with no knowledge that workers exist**, and the app's
  only access to it is one hook, `useSweep` — **the seam stays even though there is no worker.**

**Consequences.**

- `useSweep`'s `status` never reports `"computing"` in v1. That is expected, not dead code: the
  seam earns its place on stale-while-recomputing rendering, which a pretension drag needs
  anyway.
- §15 is a set of one-line Vitest assertions — no async, no worker harness in jsdom, no terrain
  stub, because the statics fixtures are terrain-free by construction.
- Performance guard rail and tripwire: **re-run the bench if anything raises `260 × 60`** (grid
  length × `samplesPerStep`) or adds per-step work heavier than arithmetic on values the sweep
  already holds. The bench must be the **ported Vitest bench**, not the prototype route.
- Terrain being cloneable keeps the worker door open for free, now that the door is shut.

**Alternatives considered, and why they lost.**

- **A Web Worker.** Rejected on the pre-agreed criterion, not on taste: p95 of `sweep()` +
  `evaluateClearance` is **1,30 ms at 240 steps and 2,30 ms at 500** against **8 ms**, a ≈6,2×
  margin — and dragging is **vsync-bound** (frame p50 16,70 ms against a 16,7 ms interval), so
  off-threading would not have made it smoother either.
- **Dropping the async seam once the worker was ruled out.** The obvious wrong inference.
  Retrofitting async later touches every call site.
- **A stored polyline on the result.** 240 steps × ~200 render points is ~50 k points churned
  per parameter change to display one.
- **Clearance inside the solver.** It puts terrain in the statics path, so §15's fixtures need a
  terrain stub and terrain enters at more than one point.
- **Solve-on-demand per slider position.** It pays a cold start per frame *and* still
  precomputes the traverse for the verdict — it solves the traverse twice.
- **Throwing on failure.** If step 137 fails you want the other 259 and you want to know where
  the failing band is; throwing means `try/catch` in the hot loop and discards partial results.
- **Coarse-live / fine-on-release.** The conventional answer to a slow sweep, and here a safety
  failure rather than a fidelity one: **a coarse sweep can miss the worst clearance point and
  flash a green verdict.**
- **Raising `steps` or `samplesPerStep`, adaptive refinement, a time-sliced sweep.** The
  convexity miss on vertex-only clearance evaluation is **≈5 mm at 1500 m**; adaptivity makes
  runs non-reproducible; a time-sliced sweep is interruptible-and-partial, and a safety verdict
  cannot consume a partial envelope.

Detail: « voir § 2 — le contrat du moteur ». Provenance: ticket 03, amended by ticket 11 §1/§7;
ticket 12.

---

#### ADR-0002 — `docs/adr/0002-terrain-keeps-the-files-own-samples.md`

**Terrain uses the file's own samples and discloses the spacing; it is never resampled**

**Context.** v1's only terrain source is the Swiss geoportal profile CSV (`Distance;Altitude`).
Measured on three real traces, the exporter emits a **fixed 200 points, not a fixed pitch**, so
spacing is `length/199`: 0,92 m at 183 m, **5,0 m at ≈995 m**, 7,5 m at 1500 m, 10,0 m at 2000 m
— coarsest exactly where spans are longest. **200 is a ceiling per export, not per line**
(operator-attested, not documentation-verified; corroborated by three traces of three different
lengths — 183,4 / 224,0 / 188,4 m — all landing on exactly 200 rows), so **no re-export helps.**

**Decision.** `Terrain` keeps **exactly the points the file gave**. `zGroundAt` interpolates
linearly, so non-uniform input costs nothing. The **measured max spacing is always disclosed**,
warning or not, with a ⚠ above 5 m whose copy states the limitation instead of offering a
remedy. The consequence is stated plainly: coarse terrain is the **normal** state on the upper
half of the 50–2000 m range — and **that frequency claim is arithmetic, not observed**, since
all three repo traces are ≤ 224 m and nobody has ever seen a coarse file.

**Consequences.**

- `xs`/`zs` carry no uniformity requirement, which is what keeps ticket 03's `Terrain` type
  unchanged.
- The disclosure block is **always present, in manual mode too** — otherwise the *absence* of a
  block reads as « no terrain concern » in the mode where terrain is a two-point idealisation.
- The denser-terrain escape hatch is a **hand-authored two-column CSV**, which has no
  `Easting`/`Northing` — so an operator who buys clearance fidelity from another source pays for
  it with the straightness check. Ticket 16's remedy *is* the « Rectitude non vérifiable »
  state.
- Deliberately unchased, so nobody re-opens it believing a decision hangs on it: the DEM's own
  native resolution. It would sharpen the *explanation* and changes no behaviour, because the
  app is designed never to need to know what the source could have provided.

**Alternatives considered, and why they lost.**

- **Resample to a uniform grid.** The obvious-looking fix, and the reason this ADR exists.
  Linear interpolation between two DEM samples 10 m apart adds **zero** terrain knowledge while
  making the array look ten times more resolved: a bump between two samples stays exactly as
  invisible, and **a coarse import becomes indistinguishable from a fine one.** For a clearance
  tool that is the non-conservative direction — it manufactures confidence.
- **Hard-reject a coarse file.** It blocks the upper half of the supported range outright.
- **Import what parses, skip malformed rows.** Same non-conservative move: a skipped row is a
  lost terrain bump, so the profile comes out smoother than the data supports. Any bad row
  rejects the whole file, with its line number.
- **Shrink the URL trace payload by resampling.** The side door. `p1.` compresses by delta +
  zigzag varint at cm resolution instead, with altitude quantised by **ceiling** so the one
  lossy step is provably safe-side.
- **Densify the clearance scan to catch the between-samples minimum.** The convexity miss is the
  cable's own sag across one terrain segment, **≈5 mm at 1500 m**, and the clearance reducer is
  the larger half of the sweep budget.

Detail: « voir § 4 — terrain et import CSV ». Provenance: ticket 06, ticket 16.

---

#### ADR-0003 — `docs/adr/0003-the-verdict-names-the-regime-and-never-says-conforme.md`

**The verdict never says « conforme »; it always names the chosen regime inside the sentence**

**Context.** The app sizes a person-carrying line on constants it openly cannot verify: three of
the five selectable regimes are `[CONVENTION]` or `[UNVERIFIED:R1]`, `m_p` 120 kg is
`[UNVERIFIED:R1]`, `h_rider` 2,1 m is `[CONVENTION]`, EN 15567's load combination is paywalled
and unapplied, and WorkSafe QLD's 2× proof test **cannot be evaluated at all**. A verdict is
therefore never a statement about the law. It reads like a copy question and it is not.

**Decision.** The verdict **never** uses « conforme » / « non conforme », and **always names the
regime the user chose inside the sentence** — « Dans les limites — EN 15567-1 (k = 3,0) »,
« Hors limites — WorkSafe QLD, critère charge suspendue ». The claim is about the rule the user
selected. Supporting mechanisms, all part of the same decision: **five verdict states** each
mapping to a different user action; margin reported as **`FS effectif` against the required
`k`**, never percentage utilisation; **no free `k` field anywhere**; the 2× proof test carried
as an **uncomputable obligation**; and a standing « Ce que cet outil ne calcule pas » block
ending on « Aucune attestation de conformité. »

**Consequences.**

- **A print or report view and any print stylesheet are out of scope**, refused on this grammar
  rather than on effort: a printed page is precisely what gets read as an attestation, and a
  printed « Indéterminé » — or a printed « Choisissez un régime » where a verdict should be — is
  a document that **outlives its context**.
- Every banner string is reviewable against one rule: one sentence, one criterion, the governing
  cell named. WorkSafe's two criteria bite on different quantities and *which one binds* is
  information the sentence must carry.
- **`Refusé — usage interdit` is a state, not a warning above a pass**, because §10.2's grips
  prohibition survives an ample margin — `FS effectif 8,0` against `k = 3` and still refused.
- The shipped line is allowed to read « Hors limites » under ACCT 10:1 (8,80 against `k` = 10).
  Choosing hardware so a `[CONVENTION]` passes would turn the shipped Material Spec into an
  implicit safety recommendation; **the spread is the teaching.**

**Alternatives considered, and why they lost.**

- **« Conforme » / « Non conforme ».** Shorter, blunter, and what a user would ask for — and it
  is exactly the claim the app has no standing to make. With an unevaluable proof test and three
  conventioned regimes, a printed « conforme » asserts something nobody verified.
- **« Conforme » plus a disclaimer banner.** It is the green that gets read. Same argument that
  refuses reporting a DAF-amplified figure beside a statically-judged verdict, and the same
  argument that refuses a noisy warning above a passing verdict.
- **Percentage utilisation instead of `FS effectif`.** Actively harmful: it recruits the « 80 %,
  ça va » heuristic from engineering contexts with far more design margin than a zip line sized
  on paywalled constants.
- **A single WorkSafe entry reduced to its strictest criterion**, and **one ACCT entry with a
  5/10 toggle.** Both hide information: which criterion binds is the answer, and a single entry
  with a sub-choice would imply somebody knows which of two contradictory paywalled sources is
  right. They ship as two criteria and two adjacent picker entries.

Detail: « voir § 6 — sorties, verdict et provenance ». Provenance: ticket 08, ticket 13 §1,
ticket 14 §4.

---

#### ADR-0004 — `docs/adr/0004-charts-letterbox-and-vertical-exaggeration-is-floored-at-1.md`

**Every chart band height derives from width, the layout letterboxes, and VE is floored at ×1**

**Context.** One identity, which nobody had written down through three tickets:

> `VE = plot aspect × (x-domain span / z-domain span)`,
> where *plot aspect* is `plot inner height / plot inner width`.

**Vertical exaggeration never depended on pixel size** — only on the plot's aspect and the
scenario's flatness. Tickets 02 and 07 fixed the auto-fit policy at **one** box shape, 900×420,
and published ×1,2…×4,7. Measured on a second box, that budget is not a property of the app:
`flex-[3]`/`flex-1` at 1440×900 gives aspect 0,4978 ⇒ **×5,02**, so the desktop was already out
of budget; a wide-short plot (844×330 landscape, aspect 0,1601) gives **×0,59…×1,78 with five of
seven scenarios vertically compressed** — auto-fit was unbounded in both directions.

**Decision.**

```
profile plot height = 0.42 × W_inner          // rule; Plot Aspect Band [0.35, 0.50] guards it
strip outer height  = clamp(0.16 × W_inner, 96, 200)      // policy band [72, 200]
VE                  = max(autofit, 1)                     // never compress
```

Published range **×1 … ×5,5**. Invariant: **no chart dimension may read the window height.**
Leftover vertical space is **left empty — the layout letterboxes rather than fills**: 43 px at
1440×900 and 463 px on a 768×1024 tablet in portrait. The ×1 clamp is **its own disclosed badge
state** (« Éch. verticale ×1 — vraie échelle », and « … · câble hors cadre » when the sag leaves
the frame), with the « ×1 vraie échelle » peek control **disabled because it is already in
force**.

**Consequences.**

- One rule fixes 390 px and 1440 px, so the narrow case is a special case rather than an
  exception — and it reproduces the prototype's desktop proportions to within 2 px.
- Ticket 13's Skeleton is dimensionally correct at 390 px **for free**, because it uses the same
  width-derived layout.
- Three rules, three guards, all written down, **because what broke here was a rule with no
  guard.** The Plot Aspect Band's floor of 0,35 alone guarantees `VE ≥ ×1` for all seven
  scenarios; compression would need aspect < 0,2705.
- The ×1 clamp's frame-clipping row is self-limiting: every case reachable inside the admissible
  envelope is already a ground-contact case (measured garde −169,67 m, `contact: "cable"`), so
  **the picture is never cut without the banner saying the line is on the ground.**

**Alternatives considered, and why they lost.**

- **A flex ratio between the two charts** — the shipped prototype's approach. It makes VE a
  function of **window height**, which is *literally* how ×5,02 happened.
- **Filling the letterbox margin.** Both ways of filling it are the defect: growing the profile
  is the ×5,02 mechanism again, and pushing the growth into the strip runs it to its 200 px
  ceiling on every desktop (a 665 px tail on a 1024×1366 portrait tablet). **A height-fed band
  is precisely the mechanism both measured defects came from.**
- **Letting auto-fit compress when the scenario is steep.** The reason for the floor:
  **compressing the sag in a clearance tool is the same class of error as exaggerating it, and
  it is worse, because it reads safe.**
- **Widening the x domain until ×1 fits.** It breaks the shared x domain, which is the alignment
  mechanism the whole two-chart layout exists for.
- **A user-facing VE selector.** Dead on evidence: forcing ×20 at 1500 m throws the cable out of
  the top of the frame and collapses the terrain into a triangle. A manual selector is a loaded
  gun pointed at the chart's legibility; the hold-to-peek ×1 control replaces it.

Detail: « voir § 5 — visualisation et mise en page ». Provenance: ticket 15 §1/§4/§5, amending
tickets 02 and 07.

---

#### ADR-0005 — `docs/adr/0005-the-url-is-a-typing-boundary-not-a-validation-boundary.md`

**A shared link restores state faithfully, including invalid state**

**Context.** A Scenario travels as readable flat search params (`?L=250&h=-40&…`, 141 characters
measured on the first-run line). Two plausible boundaries were available: validate at the URL,
or type at the URL and validate in the app. The app already owns three validation gates and a
per-field message table.

**Decision.** **The URL is a typing boundary, not a validation boundary.** The parser rejects
only what is **unrepresentable** — not a finite number, not a member of the enum, a key the
schema does not know. Nothing else.

- `?L=-5` loads into the field and lights up the Solve Gate's existing form validation exactly
  as typing `−5` would.
- `?reg=secours10&fam=ac` — forbidden under steel — **arrives already invalid** and raises its
  Blocking Error with its two Fix Actions, on the first frame.
- Corollary on both sides: **a link is applied whole or refused whole.** Individual keys are
  dropped only when the remainder still describes a complete line.
- One elected exception, and it is not a design input: `x` (`x_D`) **clamps** to `[0, L]` on
  arrival, because it selects a *view* — with a transient inline note, never silently.

**Consequences.**

- **Every error state must read correctly on the first frame of a link.** That is what forbids
  error *messages* asserting an act (« vous avez saisi », « maintenant ») while allowing button
  labels to describe their effect.
- The **stale Override badge must be derived on every load**, not carried: the schema has no
  staleness key, so a sender's stale override would otherwise arrive as a plain override and
  show a 12 mm breaking force on a 16 mm rope with no badge — and `MBF` drives the Verdict.
- Out-of-range values **must be representable**, which is one of the two reasons `type="number"`
  is rejected in favour of `<input type="text" inputMode="decimal">`.
- Refusal is reserved for the genuinely unrepresentable: unknown `v`, an undecodable or oversize
  `tr`, and two tightness drivers in one link — the last because with no single driver the
  remainder does not describe a line at all.

**Alternatives considered, and why they lost.**

- **Re-check hard ranges at the URL.** It makes **three validation tiers with one rule living in
  two of them** — the drift this app can least afford.
- **Silently repair an out-of-range or contradictory link.** It yields a scenario **neither
  party ever saw**, and it does so in `reg`, the one field the provenance aggregate pins first.
- **Refuse any link carrying an out-of-range value.** The sender's line becomes unopenable and
  the recipient cannot see *what* was wrong — the opposite of the reason readable keys were
  chosen.
- **`stripSearchParams`, or "absent = default".** It adds a **fourth meaning of absence** where
  absence already means three things, in exactly the field that matters most; and `mp=120` /
  `hr=2.1` are Unconfirmed Defaults, so a link that omits them says « whatever your build thinks
  the unattested number is » — the inverse of the binding provenance rule.

Detail: « voir § 8 — état, URL et coquille applicative ». Provenance: ticket 09, ticket 13 §4.

---

### Tested against the bar and rejected

Each of these is a real decision with a real rejected alternative. None of them needs an ADR,
and saying why is part of keeping the set readable.

| Decision | Why it does not clear the bar |
| --- | --- |
| **No Web Worker** | A measurement against a criterion fixed *before* the numbers existed, with a written tripwire (`260 × 60`) and a ported bench that reproduces it. Reversing it is cheap, pre-authorised and self-triggering. The surprising half — the `useSweep` seam surviving the worker's absence — is a consequence **inside ADR-0001**, where it belongs. |
| **Hand-rolled SVG, `d3-shape`, no charting library** | No lock-in and no surprise. `visx` primitives are recorded as the **pre-approved fallback**, so the alternative was shelved rather than killed, and a swap behind two affine functions is contained. |
| **`ssr: false` on the index route only** | Clears *surprising* and *trade-off*, but already has a canonical durable home: **`docs/conventions/routing.md` is amended** to assert it, together with the `defaultSsr` silent no-op and the `useSearch` raw ∪ validated trap. A reader hits the convention doc before the code, and a second record would drift from the first. |
| **The three validation gates** (Solve / Clearance / Verdict) | The split is visible in shipped copy — « aucun résultat » / « garde au sol indisponible » / « aucun verdict » — so it cannot be collapsed without changing user-visible strings, and its evidence (the three `Passager` fields landing in three different gates) travels with the table itself. Documented, not archaeological. |
| **Zero shipped product presets; `MBL` blank and required** | Surprising at first sight and answered at the point of contact: the empty field carries its own reason inline, and the zero-preset rule is a scope ruling already recorded in the map's *Scope decisions fixed while charting*. Nothing is left to wonder about at the code. |
| **« — » is reserved to `Indéterminé` alone** | Same family as ADR-0003, one tier down: a three-row rendering-discipline table stating *attempted-and-unavailable* against *nothing attempted* against *blocked*, side by side with its reason. It earns a **test obligation** rather than an ADR — see immediately below. |

**One obligation this section hands to the test plan.** Three tickets spent effort keeping « — »
unambiguous, and a later "consistency" pass that dashes empty cells would undo all three. The
first-run regression lock asserts the **formatted strings that are present**, which does not
catch a dash appearing where a row is supposed to be **absent**. So `first-run.test.ts` must
additionally assert that the four regime-dependent rows and the criteria × ends grid are
**absent** under « Résultats sans régime » — « voir § 11 — plan de test ».

---

## § 13 — Known thin spots

Everything here was **seen, not missed**. Each line is a place this spec is thinner than it
looks — reported by the agent that resolved the ticket it comes from — so that an implementer
who hits one knows it is a known edge and not a hole in the specification. None of them is an
open decision; each is either an assumption whose evidence is weaker than the rest, or a cost
accepted with its eyes open.

### Shell, routing and versions

- **Every router behaviour § 8 rests on is version-specific, and `package.json` currently pins
  every TanStack entry to `latest`.** The 307-on-`undefined`, the `defaultSsr` silent no-op,
  `useSearch` returning raw ∪ validated, the `strict` gate on `buildLocation`'s rebuild and the
  500-vs-200 split on a thrown validator could all change on the next `bun install` **with no
  error and no test failure** — none of them is observable from app code.

  **Decided at assembly time, because it was the one escalation that was a real decision rather
  than an assumption: pin the TanStack dependencies to the exact measured versions as the first
  step of § 10's app phase**, before A1 — `@tanstack/react-start` 1.168.34 · `react-router`
  1.170.18 · `router-core` 1.171.15 · `start-server-core` 1.169.17 · `router-plugin` 1.168.23 —
  and record the reason in `package.json`'s vicinity or in
  `docs/conventions/routing.md`, which is already the binding doc for all of it.

  > ⚠ **The versions are superseded, the mechanism is not**, by
  > [amendment C](../zipline-simulator-v1-build/AMENDMENTS.md#c--tanstack-versions-the-actual-latest-pinned):
  > pin the **actual latest** instead — `react-router` **1.170.27**, `react-start` **1.168.44**,
  > `router-plugin` **1.168.30**, transitives as resolved. A patch bump, so it is cheap; but the five
  > behaviours listed below carry a **re-probe obligation**, and if one has changed it is a § 8
  > decision to re-open, not a version to nudge.

  The argument is that `latest` is not a neutral default here. Six of § 8's decisions are
  *workarounds for measured behaviour that contradicts the documentation*, so an upgrade that
  fixes any of them silently converts a correct workaround into dead code, and an upgrade that
  changes one breaks a feature — ticket 11's ignored-keys banner — in a way no test can see,
  because the evidence is destroyed on the server before app code runs. Upgrading is then a
  deliberate act with a re-measurement attached, which is the only honest way to hold a contract
  built on measurements. **Not** pinning was rejected on that asymmetry alone: floating versions
  are cheap when the framework's documented surface is the contract, and this is the case where
  it is not.
- **The Skeleton's « same CSS layout, therefore correct at 390 px for free » is reasoned, not
  rendered.** What was measured is only that the body ships empty without a `pendingComponent`.
  Cheap to verify at A7; treat it as a checked item, not an assumption.
- **The `interactive-widget` / `visualViewport` pair is untested here** — no iOS device in this
  environment, and « older iOS Safari ignores the tag » is inherited round-1 research rather
  than measured. The fallback is written to be harmless if the tag does work (the inset is 0
  with no keyboard up), but **the narrow-screen keyboard is the one part of the layout nobody
  has seen.**
- **`ssr: false` means a refused link returns HTTP 200** to any crawler, link-preview bot or
  proxy, which will render as « a working app » in a preview card. A cosmetic consequence of the
  200-not-500 election, not a safety one — but a real behaviour change nobody has looked at.
- **The React-context store rests on the sweep being cheap** (p95 1,30 ms at 240 steps) and on
  almost every field feeding it. If a later feature makes some subtree both expensive and
  independent of most state, subscription granularity comes back as a real question — and the
  answer would then be a store, not a context memo.
- **The `<noscript>` surface is new and thinly scoped.** § 7 gives it its one sentence, but no
  ticket owns whether the app should degrade further without JS. It cannot — the engine is
  client-side by § 2 — so that line is the whole story and it had better be right.

### Error states and the test surface

- **The three refusal states have no natural fixture.** Measured, `no-convergence` and
  `slack-limit` are unreachable inside the admissible envelope, so every test of « Calcul
  impossible » and of `Indéterminé`'s scrub-track gaps runs against an **injected** failure. The
  gap rendering, the pointer skip and the typed-`x`-in-a-gap solve are therefore specified
  against a state nobody has produced: a wrong guess about what `failures[]` looks like in
  practice would be caught by no fixture.
- **« Tension impossible »'s flèche- and tension-mode bounds (0,29 m, 9 074 daN) depend on an
  engine capability that does not exist yet** — `resolveL0` re-reporting the bound in the
  driving quantity, stage E9. The seam is one § 2 already has and the mapping is a forward
  evaluation of a function `resolveL0` already owns, but if that amendment is skipped the two
  mode-specific messages are **unwritable** and the state degrades to quoting an `L₀` floor at a
  user who is editing a tension.
- **The stale-Override comparison now runs on every load and every link arrival**, which makes
  the display-precision table a **correctness** dependency rather than a formatting one. Its
  failure is silent and in the wrong direction: a missing or too-coarse row produces a badge on
  every load, training the user to ignore the badge that exists to catch a 12 mm breaking force
  on a 16 mm rope.
- **The per-field microcopy has never been read aloud by a French rigger.** The register was
  chosen for consistency — a fixed template, the rule stated as a property of the world — rather
  than validated; « une hauteur négative n'existe pas » in particular is a stylistic bet. Cheap
