  to revise, and revising it touches nothing structural.
- **The geometry-invalid exception (§ 7) is scoped to `L` / `h` / station failures.** If a later
  change gives the plots a fallback domain, the exception silently becomes unnecessary and
  « Portée invalide — aucun tracé possible » becomes dead copy nobody removes.
- **« Saisie en cours » suspends the Session write**, so a browser crash mid-edit loses the
  *edit* and keeps the last complete Scenario. That is the right trade, and it is the one place
  in the app where the user's most recent keystrokes are deliberately not persisted — **and
  nothing on screen says so.**
- **Ticket 11's answer runs 599 lines** against 250–330 for tickets 08 and 09. The excess is
  enumeration this spec required — four tables, a payload block, thirty-odd French strings — not
  argument; but a reader hunting for the *decisions* will find them at lower density there.
  § 7's four invariants are the compression: everything else is their application.

### First run and defaults

- **The 12 / 12 m anchor heights are defensible only because manual terrain is a plane.** The
  first screen therefore models a case the repo's real traces never exhibit, and the whole
  defence rests on § 6's terrain-provenance copy actually saying that dénivelé buys no
  clearance. **If that sentence gets trimmed, the defaults become misleading.**
- **A completed `?L=250&h=-40` fires a ⚠ on its first frame** (flèche/portée 2,40 % < 3 %), so a
  hand-authored link's first impression is a warning. The measured alternative is worse (garde
  −2,39 m, « Câble au sol »), so the trade is deliberate — but nobody has watched a user meet
  it.
- **« Saisie en cours » is focus-scoped**, which makes it depend on a UI concern rather than on
  state. Trivially implementable and it is what « mid-edit » means, but it has no representation
  in the URL or the Session, so it cannot be restored or shared. **This thin spot closes**:
  [amendment D](../zipline-simulator-v1-build/AMENDMENTS.md#d--tanstack-form-for-forms-valibot-for-data-validation)
  puts the rule on `@tanstack/react-form`'s `field.state.meta.isTouched` / `isBlurred`, so it is
  library state and **is** unit-testable — which is also what stops amendment A's removal of
  component tests from costing it its only check.
- **« Réinitialiser le scénario » discards a 20-field scenario with no undo and no
  confirmation.** The argument (a confirmation on a non-destructive action devalues the one
  dialog guarding real data loss) is sound; the accepted cost is that a mis-click in the
  overflow menu is unrecoverable, and « Copier le lien » is the only mitigation.
- **« Réutiliser ce tracé » is a fourth state on the file-identity line**, invented late to make
  « kept cached » mean something. It is small, but **nobody has laid that line out with four
  states at 390 px.**
- **The first-run lock asserts formatted strings, so it is coupled to the precision table.**
  That is why A2 precedes A1's test in § 10 — and it means the freeze this spec promises is
  **not in force at the moment the spec is handed over.**
- **`Pente locale`'s unit and sign were fixed in this assembly, not in a ticket.** Ticket 14
  flagged that neither ticket 07 nor ticket 10 had fixed the cell's convention while its
  first-screen inventory already quoted « −11,1 % ». Settled in § 5: **percent, 1 decimal,
  downhill negative.** Recorded here because it is the one display convention this document
  decided rather than carried.

### Layout — all of it arithmetic, none of it rendered

- **Nothing in ticket 15 was rendered.** The entire layout contract is arithmetic over the
  prototype's real Tailwind classes and its §15-verified solver. That is why § 10's deletion
  gate carries three re-checks: the prototype is the only place the comparison can still be
  made.
- **The banner heights 44 / 60 / 76 px are the only numbers in the budget not measured on the
  artifact** — they are computed from `text-sm` / `leading-snug` / `py-2`. If the verdict wraps
  to four lines at 390 px, the resting rail loses its headroom over `MIN_RAIL` and shows the
  header plus a heading only.
- **The View Strip's width arithmetic is character-count based**: 93 + 76 + 111 + 128 px of
  controls against 342 px available, 303 px abbreviated. The abbreviation *threshold* survives a
  10 % error; the **fit** at 342 px is tight enough that the first build should verify it before
  the prototype goes.
- **The ~336 px soft-keyboard height (~180 px in landscape) is a platform-typical figure, not a
  device measurement.** The three pinned states degrade gracefully if it is wrong — `compact`
  fires on `--kb > 0` whatever the value — but « 284 px of usable form » is only as good as the
  estimate.
- **The « câble hors cadre » clip state is new and unprototyped.** Its mitigation — every case
  reachable inside the admissible envelope is already « Câble au sol » (measured garde
  −169,67 m, `contact: "cable"`) — is an **argument, not a proof**: a deep gorge under a short
  span could in principle clip while safe.
- **The strip's `clamp(0,16 × W_inner, 96, 200)` refines the elected « the strip takes the
  remainder » mechanism**, because absorption reintroduces height-fed band sizing — the
  mechanism both measured defects came from. On the reference desktop the two differ by 41 px of
  strip height (177 against 136); both are legal, and the elected band, the letterbox principle
  and the ×1 floor are all preserved.
- **Landscape-always-collapsed is derived, not tested.** It follows from collapse condition (c),
  which is what lets § 5 refuse a rotate prompt without special-casing orientation anywhere. If
  `MIN_RAIL` or the banner height moves enough for (c) to stop firing in landscape, the
  aspect-0,16 compression case the ×1 clamp exists to refuse becomes reachable **through the
  box**, not just through the data.
- **B2's stated mechanism no longer matches its evidence.** The threshold (viewport < 768 px) is
  retained, but ticket 15's aspect 0,91–1,25 / VE ×3,1…×12,6 at a 479 px column were measured
  under the **rejected** `flex-[3]`/`flex-1` sizing. Under the elected width-derived rule the
  aspect is 0,42 at every width by construction, so what breaks below 768 px is the plot's
  **width** — see § 5, where the bullet is restated on that ground. Worth re-confirming that 768
  is still the right number once a real layout exists.

### Two figures carried as measured and not reproducible from this document

- **The chart column at a 390 px viewport is recorded as 101 px** while `390 − 288 = 102`.
  Carried as ticket 15 measured it; the 1 px changes nothing downstream (69 px of plot on the
  prototype's padding, 53 px under the elected `GUTTER = 24`).
- **The 665 px strip tail on a 1024×1366 portrait tablet** is ticket 15's figure for the
  *rejected* absorption mechanism. It is carried as the reason absorption lost; it cannot be
  recomputed from the constants in § 5, which describe the elected rule instead.
